#2352. Inter-Como, il big match dell'11a giornata: la guida completa alla sfida

Inter-Como, il big match dell'11a giornata: la guida completa alla sfida

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Nuovo impegno di campionato per l'Inter, che dopo la vittoria per 0-3 contro i Feyenoord in Champions League torna a giocare davanti al popolo nerazzurro: la squadra di Chivu affronterà il Como a San Siro nell'undicesima giornata di campionato, con appuntamento domenica 8 novembre alle ore 18:00. La sfida del Meazza vedrà i nerazzurri impegnati contro la squadra di Fabregas, reduce dal successo in campionato contro il Venezia e dalla roboante vittoria per 1-9 in casa del Lens in Champions League. Un confronto che è andato in scena in quattro occasioni nella passata stagione tra campionato e Coppa Italia, con le due squadre che hanno dato vita a partite emozionanti e ricche di spettacolo.

Sono trenta i precedenti totali in Serie A tra Inter e Como, con i nerazzurri avanti nel bilancio grazie alle 21 vittorie conquistate contro le sei dei lariani, con tre pareggi a completare il quadro. L'Inter ha vinto le ultime 11 partite di Serie A contro il Como, registrando la striscia di successi consecutivi più lunga nella storia del Club contro una singola avversaria nella competizione: i 21 k.o. subiti dai lariani contro i nerazzurri rappresentano il maggior numero di sconfitte subite dal Como contro un singolo avversario in Serie A. Inter in vantaggio anche nel conto dei gol segnati: 63 in 30 confronti (2.1 in media a partita), contro i 28 realizzati dal Como. Per quanto riguarda i 15 precedenti a San Siro, l'Inter ha collezionato 14 vittorie e una sconfitta contro i lariani.

L'Inter arriva all'appuntamento di San Siro contro la squadra di Fabregas al secondo posto in classifica con 25 punti, frutto di otto vittorie, un pareggio e una sconfitta. Quella del Meazza sarà la sfida tra il miglior attacco (Inter, 115 gol) e la miglior difesa (Como, 37 reti subite) nelle ultime due stagioni di Serie A (dal 2025/26 - considerando solo le formazioni presenti in entrambi i campionati).

I nerazzurri sono stati protagonisti di un grande avvio di stagione in casa: la squadra di Chivu ha segnato 17 gol nelle prime cinque gare casalinghe di questa Serie A, vincendole tutte. Negli ultimi 60 anni, l'Inter ha segnato più di 17 gol nelle prime sei partite casalinghe di campionato solo in due occasioni, realizzandone 18 sia nel 1999/00 che nel 2021/22. L’Inter (12) è anche la squadra con più gol realizzati nell’ultima mezz’ora di gioco in questo campionato; nove dei quali sono arrivati negli ultimi 15 minuti (altro record), mentre sono 13 i punti guadagnati da situazione di svantaggio (miglior dato nei migliori cinque campionati europei).

Quella del Meazza sarà una sfida tra due squadre che amano tenere in mano il pallino del gioco: Inter (220) e Como (203) sono le due squadre che hanno effettuato il maggior numero di tiri totali in questa Serie A. Nerazzurri (62.5%) e lariani (62.1%) sono anche le due formazioni con il possesso palla più alto

#2351. Barcelona: French defender tracked by Arsenal and Chelsea takes step towards move, January deal unlikely

By Axel Clody | 05 Nov 2026 11:23

Castello Lukeba wants to join Barcelona and has taken a step towards the Catalan club. Although Barcelona are looking to sign a centre-back in the next winter transfer window, the arrival of the Frenchman appears unlikely.

Barcelona want to sign a centre-back this winter and have several players in their sights, including Natan and Alessandro Bastoni. Rumours are multiplying about the Catalan club’s search. Sport now reports that Lukeba has been one of the centre-backs attracting the most attention at Barcelona for some time.

The French centre-back wants to leave RB Leipzig soon to join a European heavyweight. To that end, the former Lyon player has decided to change agent. The 23-year-old has signed with Excellence Sport, a choice that is far from insignificant as it is the same agency as Jules Kounde.

The Catalan media outlet indicates that Lukeba wants to join Barcelona and is making a clear step towards the club with this choice.

His new agent already met Deco around ten days ago. However, even if the interest is mutual and the French international is a unanimous choice at Barcelona, a deal is not expected to happen in the next winter transfer window.

Indeed, Leipzig have made it clear that they will not let Lukeba leave mid-season. The financial conditions will not be in place in January either for such an investment by the Spanish champions. The next summer transfer window could be the ideal time, since the Frenchman’s release clause will drop from £68m (€80m) to £55m (€65m) in June 2027.

Chelsea and Arsenal are also reportedly monitoring the Frenchman, whose contract runs until June 2029.

That amount could still be negotiable provided the player clearly expresses his desire to play in Catalonia.

The former Lyon player, who interests several big Premier League clubs, considers Barcelona his ideal destination. ‘For now, contacts have started and Barcelona have put his name in capital letters in case this transfer is possible this summer,’ says Sport.

Lukeba’s arrival at Barcelona this summer could be financed by the departure of Ronald Araujo to Liverpool, as the Reds appear set to trigger his £46m (€55m) purchase option.

Before the next summer window, the Catalan club is therefore looking for another left-footed centre-back who could arrive this winter on reasonable financial terms.

#2349. Plymouth Argyle vs Alvechurch - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 11:32, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 11:42

Reaching the first round of the FA Cup for the second time in three seasons, seventh-tier Alvechurch travel to League One Plymouth Argyle hoping to continue their journey in football's oldest cup competition.

The Pilgrims head into Saturday's clash after a goalless draw with Reading last weekend that kept them in the top five, while the Church have already played five matches to reach this stage, most recently beating Spalding United 4-0 in a replay.

Plymouth Argyle will be hoping to avoid a cup upset on Saturday afternoon when they welcome their Midlands opponents to Home Park.

Tom Cleverley's side, who were knocked out in the first round of the FA Cup last season, head into the weekend in good form, having lost just two of their last seven outings in all competitions.

Since suffering back-to-back league defeats against Huddersfield Town and Stevenage, the Pilgrims have responded with a narrow victory over Cambridge United before sharing the points with Reading in a goalless draw.

After 14 League One matches, Plymouth have collected 23 points and sit comfortably in the playoff places, six points behind the league leaders as they look to mount a serious push for promotion back to the Championship.

The Devon outfit failed to progress beyond the opening round last season but enjoyed runs to the fourth and fifth rounds in the 2023-24 and 2024-25 campaigns, where they were eventually eliminated by Leeds United and Manchester City respectively.

Looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup for the second time in three years, Alvechurch make the journey south hoping some cup magic can help them produce one of the shocks of the season.

The Worcestershire-based side, who compete in the Southern League Premier Division Central, know they face a significant challenge against opponents four tiers above them, but arrive at Home Park in good form.

Led by former Wrexham and Cheltenham Town midfielder Kyle Storer, the Church are currently enjoying an unbeaten run of six matches in all competitions, including an impressive 3-1 away win against Leiston on Halloween and a 4-0 victory over Spalding in their FA Cup replay.

During that run, Storer's side have kept just one clean sheet but found the net 16 times, and will be hoping to showcase their attacking threat against League One opposition this weekend.

So far in this season's FA Cup, Alvechurch have overcome Wolverhampton City, Warwick, Cambridge and Spalding, with the latter requiring a replay after a 2-2 draw in the initial meeting.

As mentioned, the Church have previous experience of causing a cup upset, having beaten League One Cheltenham Town 2-1 away from home in the first round in November 2022, before reaching the second round and falling to Forest Green Rovers.

Ahead of a clash against opponents from several divisions below, Cleverley is likely to name a much-changed lineup and could stick with a similar XI to the one used in the EFL Trophy this season.

Murphy Cooper has been Plymouth's first-choice goalkeeper this season but withdrew from their match against Reading the day before the game due to a family issue.

James Storer came in for his league debut and impressed, meaning he could retain his place between the sticks for this one.

Teenager Sep Campbell, the club's youngest-ever goalscorer, has not featured for Plymouth since an EFL Trophy victory over Newport County but could return to the attack on Saturday.

As for the visitors, they reported no fresh injury concerns following their most recent outing and could name a similar starting XI to the one that secured their place in the next round a few weeks ago.

Miracle Okafor, who scored a brace in their 4-0 victory over Spalding, is likely to lead the line for Storer's men alongside Danny Newton.

Plymouth Argyle possible starting lineup:

Storer; Ross, Mitchell, Rendle; Edwards, Bakinson, White, MacKenzie; Amaechi, Campbell, Evans

Evans; Rask; Forsyth, Crowther, Ngandu, Sullivan; Reilly, Osborne, Stephens; Okafor, Newton

There is an obvious gulf in quality between these sides, and while Alvechurch have already shown they can trouble Football League opposition in the FA Cup, Plymouth should have enough to avoid an upset.

We expect the Pilgrims to progress comfortably into the next round with a 4-1 victory over the Church.

#2347. Provedel e l’Inter: «Un sogno che si realizza». Il ricordo di Toldo

Il portiere ammette l’incredulità iniziale e racconta la sua reazione da bambino al gol dell’idolo contro la Juve.

Ivan Provedel ha raccontato il suo trasferimento estivo dalla Lazio all’Inter nella quarta puntata di Welcome Kit, il format di Inter TV powered by Betsson Sport. Il portiere ha parlato della chiamata ricevuta dai nerazzurri, dell’obiettivo che si è dato nella nuova squadra e del ricordo di Toldo, il suo idolo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il contatto dell’Inter ha colto Provedel di sorpresa: «Quando mi ha chiamato l’Inter sono stato un po’ incredulo». A quella prima reazione non è seguita subito la certezza del trasferimento. Il portiere ha spiegato di aver aspettato che l’operazione fosse conclusa prima di lasciarsi andare all’entusiasmo: «fino a che non ho firmato non ho voluto pensare o illudermi, fino a che non è stato tutto fatto». Solo dopo la firma, ha raccontato, è stato felicissimo.

Provedel ha indicato anche quale sia il suo primo obiettivo da interista: «Dimostrare che qui ci posso stare e che posso dare il mio contributo alla squadra». Il portiere vuole quindi mostrare di poter far parte dell’Inter e aiutare i compagni. Alla domanda su che cosa rappresenti per lui indossare la maglia del club, ha risposto: «Un sogno che si realizza».

Nelle sue parole, l’attesa prudente prima della firma lascia spazio alla felicità per il passaggio all’Inter. Provedel ha collegato l’arrivo in nerazzurro al desiderio di dare un contributo alla squadra, oltre che al significato personale della maglia che ora indossa.

#2346. Juventus have £87m budget and five strikers on their radar, including two from the Premier League

By Axel Clody | 05 Nov 2026 11:33

Juventus have a budget of around £85m (€100m) to invest in the transfer market. The club are looking at the striker market with five names in mind.

On Tuesday, the Juventus shareholders’ meeting was held to approve the club’s accounts. The £212.5m (€250m) capital increase aims to ‘strengthen the capital structure and sporting competitiveness, to fund possible improvements to strategic real estate assets (mainly the Allianz Stadium), to enhance the brand and to ensure the economic and financial viability of the club’.

In this respect, CalcioMercato reports that the capital increase could allow Juventus to have a budget of around £85m (€100m) to invest in the market, from the winter window through to next summer. It is a substantial sum with which to sign high-level players.

Given the difficulties encountered by Luciano Spalletti’s side at the start of the season, investment as early as the next winter window cannot be ruled out.

Signing a striker appears to be Juventus’ priority, as Randal Kolo Muani and Nick Woltemade are not delivering: they have scored only one goal between them in ten Serie A matchdays.

Spalletti is looking for a forward with a profile similar to that of Dusan Vlahovic, who left last summer, and who is capable of being particularly effective in front of goal. The Italian outlet reveals the existence of a shortlist of five strikers on Juventus’ radar.

Benjamin Sesko and Folarin Balogun are high on the list and will be monitored closely by Juventus over the coming weeks.

The American is one of the five players most likely to leave Monaco, while the Slovenian, who is currently injured with a shin problem, is struggling to live up to the £65m (€76.5m) Manchester United invested to sign him in the summer of 2025.

Brian Brobbey is also on this Turin shortlist, but the Dutch forward is unlikely to leave Sunderland this winter, as he has just extended his contract until June 2031. Juventus are also interested in two young forwards in great form: Antonio Raimondo and Younes Ebnoutalib.

The 22-year-old Italian striker has scored eight goals in nine league matches for Frosinone, but he injured an ankle last weekend. A winter move therefore looks unlikely, as he is not expected to return to action before 2027. Ebnoutalib, a 23-year-old German striker, is also in sparkling form for Eintracht Frankfurt, with eight goals in ten matches this season.

#2344. Il Flaco Lopez è il 2° argentino più determinante del 2026: dietro a Messi, davanti a Martinez

José Manuel Lopez continua a essere uno dei protagonisti della straordinaria stagione del Palmeiras. L'attaccante argentino, soprannominato "Flaco", si è ormai imposto come uno dei centravanti più decisivi del calcio sudamericano, grazie a numeri che raccontano un rendimento di altissimo livello.

L'ex Lanus ha collezionato ben 40 partecipazioni dirette a un gol in 68 partite disputate nel 2026. Con la maglia del Palmeiras il classe 2000 ha segnato 24 reti e fornito 14 assist in 60 presenze, contribuendo in maniera determinante al percorso della squadra brasiliana, attualmente seconda in campionato, semifinalista di Copa do Brasil e finalista di Copa Libertadores. Nell'ultima sfida contro il Remo, terminata con un netto 6-1, López ha confermato il suo momento straordinario segnando una rete spettacolare e servendo anche un assist.

Anche in nazionale il centravanti ha iniziato a ritagliarsi spazio. Dopo aver disputato il suo primo Mondiale con l'Argentina, López ha raccolto otto presenze con l'Albiceleste, realizzando un gol e fornendo un assist. La prima rete è arrivata nell'amichevole vinta 4-0 contro la Bolivia, mentre l'altro contributo è stato nella vittoria per 3-1 contro la Svizzera al Mondiale, quando ha favorito il gol di Julian Alvarez. In questa stagione il suo impatto è stato costante in tutte le competizioni: quattro gol in Copa Libertadores, quattro in Copa do Brasil, sei nel Paulista e dieci nel Brasileirao.

Con 24 reti complessive, López è il secondo argentino più prolifico dell'anno alle spalle soltanto di Lionel Messi (40 gol), davanti a Lautaro Martínez (21). Ora lo attende un finale di stagione da protagonista, con la finale di Libertadores contro l'Estudiantes in programma il 28 novembre allo stadio Centenario di Montevideo.

#2342. Cristiano Ronaldo: Portugal icon releases video statement confirming international retirement

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 11:41, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 11:47

Portugal icon Cristiano Ronaldo has officially announced his retirement from international football.

Widely regarded as one of, if not the, greatest player of all time, the 41-year-old has decided to call time on his legendary 23-year career with Portugal.

Ronaldo holds the record for the most international caps (234), most international goals (146) and most international victories (141) in the men’s game, while he has also won Euro 2016 and two Nations League titles with Portugal.

The five-time Ballon d'Or winner's decision to bring the curtain down on his glittering international career comes after a fall-out with head coach Jorge Jesus during the September/October international break.

Ronaldo was handed a 30-day suspension and €255 fine by the Portuguese FA after he walked out of the Portugal camp last month after being told he would not start a UEFA Nations League match against Denmark.

Jesus refused to close the door on bringing Ronaldo back into the first-team fold for November’s break, but the Al-Nassr star has taken to social media to end his Portugal career once and for all.

Ronaldo’s decision to retire from Portugal duty comes just a month after long-term rival Lionel Messi played his 208th and final game for Argentina.

A post shared by Cristiano Ronaldo (@cristiano)

In a video posted on Instagram, Ronaldo said: “The day has come back for me to hand back the number 7 shirt of our national team.

“I never felt it belonged to me alone, but for 23 years I had the privilege and the responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me into stadiums all around the world.

“Today, the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the mind alone. They are also made with the heart.

“A journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end. When I look back at everything with the national team, I don’t just see the matches, goals, records or trophies.

“I see people. I see the Portuguese people living abroad who, for 90 minutes, felt a little closer to home. I see millions of Portuguese people I have never met but who became part of my life.

“Because at the end of the day, football is so much bigger than a game. Football is about people.

"I spent my life working to be the best player in the world. Today I realise that the greatest victory was to have deserved to represent the best country in the world."

Ronaldo thanked his teammates, coaches, family and supporters from around the world, adding: "Thank you for these 23 years. Thank you for having believed in me. Portugal will always be in me. Forever."

Meanwhile, a statement from the Portuguese Football Federation expressed “deep gratitude and immense admiration” for Ronaldo’s service to the country.

“Cristiano Ronaldo has raised Portugal’s profile and inspired generations of young people who dream of following in his footsteps; for many of us, he is the greatest player of all time!” the federation said.

They have also stated that they intends to organise a tribute to Ronaldo “in which the fans and the country can one more time applaud and celebrate a player who has given us so much”.

Ronaldo’s decision to retire from Portugal duty means that he will miss November’s international fixtures against Denmark and Wales.

His 234th and final game for his country was a 1-0 victory against Wales in the Nations League in September, while his final goal was scored from the penalty spot against Croatia (2-1 win) in July during the 2026 World Cup.

Ronaldo will now focus on his club career with Saudi Pro League side Al-Nassr as he continues to chase down 1,000 career goals – he currently sits on 985 goals in all competitions.

CR7 has previously stated that “this is probably my last year of football, and I want to leave a spectacular legacy”, with retirement from all football a genuine possibility in 2027.

#2339. A Bologna si è tornati a parlare d'Europa, Palladino: "Un mese fa non ci avrei creduto..."

Raffaele Palladino ha risollevato il Bologna. Con quattro vittorie, un pareggio e una sconfitta in sei partite totali, il nuovo tecnico rossoblù ha preso una squadra che con Domenico Tedesco in panchina non era ancora mai riuscita a vincere e le ha dato delle certezze, oltre a dei risultati, da cui ripartire per il prosieguo del campionato. Tanto che, in città, c'è già qualcuno che parla d'Europa...

Il tecnico dei felsinei ha commentato tutto ciò nella conferenza pre-Parma. Questa la sua risposta specifica: "Sono contento, se un mese fa me lo avessero detto non ci avrei creduto. Non dobbiamo né parlare di Europa né di salvezza, dovremo solo essere equilibrati in quello che facciamo, prendere tutto partita per partita. Dobbiamo creare la mentalità del potercela giocare con tutti, senza guardare la classifica. Non voglio smorzare i toni, sono felice ci sia entusiasmo, ma dovremo essere uniti anche nei momenti di difficoltà con equilibrio".

#2338. Arsenal vs. Hull City: Head-to-head record and past meetings

By Ben Knapton | 05 Nov 2026 11:53

Premier League ever-presents Arsenal and top-flight returnees Hull City clash at the Emirates Stadium in Saturday's gameweek 10 encounter.

The Gunners will temporarily go six points clear at the top of the Premier League table with victory over their newly-promoted foes, having moved three above Manchester City in last weekend's 1-0 success at Liverpool.

Meanwhile, Sergej Jakirovic's Hull will break into the top 10 of the rankings with all three points, but they have failed to win any of their last eight games across all tournaments.

Here, Sports Mole takes an in-depth look at the head-to-head record and previous meetings between the two sides.

Arsenal vs. Hull City has been a collector's item on the English football calendar, compared to some of the most common matchups in the land, as the two clubs have only met on 26 occasions in all competitions.

Unsurprisingly, the head-to-head dominance belongs to the Gunners, who have beaten the Tigers 18 times while suffering just three defeats and settling for a point on five occasions.

Arsenal actually failed to win any of their first three battles with Hull, playing out two draws and suffering one loss between 1908 and 1913 as Woolwich Arsenal, and they only earned two victories from their first seven head-to-heads.

However, the reigning Premier League champions have since triumphed in 16 of their last 19 contests, most notably the 2013-14 FA Cup final, when Arsene Wenger's men fell 2-0 down inside just eight minutes but magnificently fought back to prevail 3-2 and end their nine-year trophy drought.

The Gunners have also avoided defeat in each of their last 14 clashes with the Tigers, although Hull's last win in this particular fixture was a momentous one, as a Geovanni-inspired side shocked the Emirates with a 2-1 success in 2008, shortly after their maiden ascent to the top flight.

Arsenal responded to that harrowing evening with a seven-match winning run against Hull, and their current victorious sequence now stands at three games, including a Premier League double in 2016-17.

Alexis Sanchez was Hull's nemesis that year, scoring twice in a 4-1 away win before bagging another brace in a 2-0 home victory, and the Chilean is the highest documented scorer in this fixture with eight in total.

Nicklas Bendtner, Theo Walcott and Aaron Ramsey each scored three against the Tigers, meanwhile, the latter memorably snatching 2014 FA Cup glory for Arsenal in extra time.

#2335. Harry Kane: Record-breaking Bayern Munich contract confirmed as Ballon d’Or winner surpasses Kylian Mbappe’s salary

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 11:55

Bayern Munich have announced that 2026 Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane has committed his future to the club by signing a new contract until June 2029.

The 33-year-old striker was approaching the final six months of his pre-existing deal, with La Liga champions Barcelona believed to have been keeping tabs on his situation.

Despite a lengthy negotiation process, Kane was determined to continue his career with Bayern where he has scored a staggering 156 goals in 161 games since joining from Tottenham Hotspur three years ago.

According to multiple reports, including The Athletic, Kane’s new deal is the most lucrative in Bayern’s history, with the striker now said to be the highest-paid player in Europe by salary.

In comparison, Manchester City’s Erling Haaland became the highest-paid Premier League player in January 2025 when he agreed a £500,000-per-week contract until the summer of 2034.

Kylian Mbappe, meanwhile, is thought to be pocketing around £518,000 a week at Real Madrid, but Kane is now believed to be earning more than both strikers.

It is also claimed that Bayern do not view this significant contract as Kane’s last agreement with the club, as their clear desire is to retain his invaluable services well into the future.

???????? ????? ???? ????? ????????? – ??? ?????? ????! ????? ???? ???? ?? ?????? – ??? ?????????? ???? ??????. ? https://t.co/qrJmpbXzwu

“I feel right at home at FC Bayern. I love the club, the fans and the city of Munich,” Kane told the club’s official website after signing his new deal.

“We have a fantastic team and, in Vincent Kompany, a fantastic manager. It makes me very proud to be part of this club. My whole family is very happy here.

“I was keen to stay. I’m now looking forward to the future and I want to win many more titles with this great club.”

Herbert Hainer, Bayern’s club president, added: “What began as a major transfer has, over time, become a special chapter in FC Bayern’s history.

“Harry Kane lives up to every promise football has to offer, and he has secured his place in the hearts of the fans as one of the greatest figures in the sport of our time.

“My compliments to Jan-Christian Dreesen and our sporting management team. This is a special moment for FC Bayern today.”

CEO Dreesen continued: “We’re delighted to have successfully extended Harry Kane’s contract. This is fantastic news for us, for our fans and for the Bundesliga.

“The Ballon d’Or winner is staying at Bayern Munich! Since joining us in 2023, he has achieved extraordinary things with his goals, his influence on the game and as a role model both on and off the pitch.

“He has long since made FC Bayern his home, has continued to develop with us and crowned that development with the Ballon d’Or. His contract extension is a commitment to FC Bayern and to a story that is far from over.”

Bayern board member for sport Max Eberl, who has regularly provided updates on Kane’s future in the media, added: “This key contract extension gives us certainty in our planning. It sends a message that the biggest goals are within reach at FC Bayern.

“Players like Harry Kane offer a team unique opportunities. We’re delighted that he will continue to be a key figure who helps shape the direction of the club, and with whom we can continue to build our squad for the future.”

Kane is hoping to win a third consecutive Bundesliga title with Bayern this season as well as fire the German giants to their first Champions League trophy since 2020.

#2334. Casarano, Zauri carica i suoi: “A Caserta senza paura e con personalità”

Alla viglia della trasferta in casa della Casertana il tecnico del Casarano, Luciano Zauri, ha presentato la sfida del 'Pinto':

"Ciò che è importante è lo spirito con cui i ragazzi sono scesi in campo e hanno affrontato la gara - si legge su TuttoC.com -. Resta un pizzico di rammarico per il risultato finale, ma ci teniamo la prestazione e un punto in classifica che in questo momento fa sicuramente bene. La stagione, fin qui, ci sta mettendo davanti a queste situazioni, ma è chiaro che le condizioni del campo, quando è impraticabile, penalizzano entrambe le squadre. Vorremmo tutti disputare la gara su un terreno di gioco adeguato, ma purtroppo questo non dipende da noi. Casertana? Giochiamo contro una squadra costruita per vincere e che, fin qui, probabilmente, non è riuscita a esprimere tutto il proprio potenziale.

Il valore della formazione non è in discussione e nutriamo massimo rispetto, soprattutto considerando che giocheremo sul loro campo. Noi, però, dovremo andare lì e giocare senza paura, con personalità e consapevolezza dei nostri mezzi. Rispetto alla settimana ideale, abbiamo avuto un giorno in meno, ma ogni allenamento è importante per curare i dettagli e consolidare alcuni aspetti delle nostre gerarchie tattiche. Dispiace non poter contare sulla presenza di Negro: sarà assente Leonetti, ma dovremo essere bravi a trovare le soluzioni migliori per affrontare la partita".

#2333. Nesta e i derby: "A Roma ho fatto i danni, soffrivo troppo. A Milano giocavo in scioltezza"

L'ex capitano biancoceleste racconta le forti emozioni vissute nelle stracittadine del passato e presenta il derby contro il Benevento sulla panchina dell'Avellino

Alla vigilia del sentito derby di Serie B tra Benevento e Avellino, l'allenatore biancoverde Alessandro Nesta fa il punto sulle sue emozioni. Mettendo a confronto la sfida con la sua celebre esperienza vissuta nella capitale, l'ex capitano biancoceleste spiega come il derby contro la Roma rappresentasse per lui una grandissima pressione e una fonte di ansia immensa, vissuta ben diversamente rispetto alla tranquillità del derby tra Inter e Milan.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

I derby? In quelli di Roma ho fatti i danni. Essendo la mia città lo soffrivo troppo. Nessun'altra partita, neppure la finale di Champions League, mi ha mai trasmesso una simile ansia. Quelli di Milano li ho giocati in scioltezza. Uno l'ho perfino giocato in occasione di una semifinale di Champions, ma il giorno dopo ero in bicicletta a Corso Vercelli. Un altro mondo.

Da allenatore il derby lo sento. Non come quello di Roma, ma lo sento. So che è una partita importante. So come si sente la gente, perché sono stato anche tifoso. So che dobbiamo andare a fare una partita vera. Trasferta vietata? I derby devono avere entrambe le tifoserie. A noi mancherà qualcosa, ma questo deve darci forza. Dobbiamo andare a lottare: per noi e per loro, che non potranno esserci.

#2332. Celtic star handed 'ideal springboard' as sensational transfer return route emerges

Carl Ikeme reckons Sam Johnstone has landed the "ideal springboard" at Celtic to revive his career, with the former Wolves goalkeeper insisting his old club can also benefit.

Johnstone was pitched straight into the heat of battle at Ibrox after his arrival, before briefly coming back out of the side. But the 33-year-old has since established himself as Celtic's clear first choice between the sticks and is beginning to put together the regular run of football he was craving after finding opportunities limited at Wolves.

And Ikeme believes playing every week at Celtic can help the former England international rediscover his best form, putting him in a position to either secure a permanent switch or return to Wolves ready to fight for the No.1 jersey.

Ikeme was speaking as part of the Midnite Express, providing luxury coach travel for 100 Wolverhampton Wanderers supporters' and said: "It simply didn't happen for him at Wolves, largely because José Sá kept the shirt and the club went through a turbulent period last season. Sam was in and out, but he’s a top goalkeeper who needs to be playing week in, week out.

Read more: Martin O'Neill fires 'a lot more' Celtic demand at £3.5m signing

"It makes no sense to have both Sam and José sitting in the same squad, especially with John Ruddy returning to provide veteran cover.

"Going to Celtic is a fantastic move. It’s a massive football club where he’ll play every week and rebuild his confidence.

"Whether he uses it to secure a permanent move or returns to challenge for the number one shirt if Wolves go up, it’s a win-win. Goalkeeping is unique: you can find yourself out of favour very quickly through no fault of your own. The crucial thing is that he’s playing regularly again.

"It's an ideal springboard. You step outside the intense Premier League goldfish bowl, even though playing for Celtic or Rangers brings immense pressure of its own.

"It allows experienced keepers to get regular minutes, find their rhythm, and compete for trophies. It’s worked brilliantly for several top keepers, and it will do the same for Sam."

#2331. Liverpool transfer news: Reds in ‘advanced talks’ over new contract for player ‘trusted’ by Andoni Iraola

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 12:02

Liverpool have reportedly entered advanced negotiations with teenage starlet Trey Nyoni over a new contract.

The Reds suffered a disappointing 3-2 Champions League defeat away against Fenerbahce on Wednesday, but it was a memorable night for Nyoni who scored his first goal for the club.

At the age of 19 years and 127 days, Nyoni became the youngest player in the club's history to score his first goal for the team in a European Cup match, while he is Liverpool’s second youngest Champions League goalscorer after Trent Alexander-Arnold.

Nyoni has stepped up to Andoni Iraola’s first team this season after the Reds rejected a £30m offer from Nottingham Forest during the summer transfer window.

The academy graduate was handed his first Premier League start for Liverpool in their 1-0 defeat to champions Arsenal at Anfield last weekend and has played 12 times for the Reds across all competitions this term.

Nyoni has made a notable impression in Iraola’s midfield playing alongside either Alexis Mac Allister, Dominik Szoboszlai or Ryan Gravenberch, and the youngster could soon be rewarded with an improved deal.

According to The Athletic, Nyoni is in advanced talks with Liverpool over extending his contract, even though his current deal runs until June 2030.

The report adds that Nyoni has been discussing his future with Liverpool for 'months', with the club keen to reward him with fresh terms to reflect his first-team emergence under Iraola.

While a contract extension has yet to be finalised, it is understood that there is optimism that an agreement will be reached, with a breakthrough in negotiations expected soon.

Speaking to reporters before Liverpool’s loss to Fenerbahce, Iraola made it clear that he believes Nyoni is now “in a very good place” to enhance his development.

“Trey just has to continue in the same way as since the beginning of pre-season, be ready for all of the games,” said the Spaniard.

“We trust him, he’s doing very well. We have to encourage him to continue learning, improving and getting experience. But he’s in a very good place.”

With Gravenberch facing a spell on the sidelines through injury and Szoboszlai having only just recovered from an injury of his own, Nyoni is a strong contender to start again for Liverpool in Sunday’s Premier League clash against Crystal Palace.

#2328. Fortuna Sittard vs Telstar - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 12:26

High-flying Fortuna Sittard return to Eredivisie action this weekend, hosting Telstar at the Fortuna Sittard Stadion on Saturday.

The home side begin this weekend’s round of fixtures in sixth place with 19 points - 10 places and 10 points clear of the visitors.

A trip to Feyenoord, who had won three straight meetings between the teams, threatened to end Fortuna’s six-game unbeaten run, especially after Luciano Valente opened the scoring just before the hour mark. However, Shiloh ‘t Zand equalised with four minutes remaining to force a share of the spoils.

While some may argue the point flattered Danny Buijs’s men given their performance, it was a reward for their resilience, a quality they have displayed in abundance since a 5-1 drubbing at the hands of Ajax in mid-September.

Having collected just seven points from a possible 15 in Sittard-Geleen, Fortuna’s return to form still has room for improvement via consistent results at home. A 1-0 win over Excelsior in their last home outing suggests they are moving in the right direction.

Bottom of the league in average ball possession this term (35.7%), Fortuna remain a side best known for their off-the-ball work. They finished second-bottom in this metric last season, only ahead of relegated Heracles.

With each of their next six matches coming against teams currently below them in the table, Fortuna have an opportunity to build a sustained run of positive results and enter the second half of the season well positioned for a European push.

After almost three months, Telstar finally ended their wait for a first victory since beating NEC Nijmegen on the opening weekend, running out emphatic 4-2 winners over FC Utrecht in Sunday’s clash at BUKO Stadion.

The victory came at a crucial time for manager Henk Brugge, who was under increased scrutiny after his side were knocked out of the KNVB Beker first round by lower-league opposition Almere City just four days earlier.

Two points adrift of safety, another Telstar defeat this weekend could pile the pressure back on Brugge and their away record this season has been dismal, with just one win, four points, and four goals scored from five matches on the road.

However, De Witte Leeuwen are unbeaten in their last five trips to the Fortuna Sittard Stadion dating back to August 2014, and will draw confidence from their most recent trip in March, when they cruised to a 4-1 victory.

Facing a side with the best big-chance conversion rate in the division (76.9%), Saturday’s visitors will be wary of conceding too many opportunities, despite boasting a significant advantage in clear-cut chances created (23-10).

Rodrigo Guth and Lequincio Zeefuik both limped off with injuries in Fortuna’s last outing and are doubtful for Saturday’s clash.

This could open the door for Ivan Marquez and Kristoffer Peterson - both substitutes last time out - to come into the hosts’ starting lineup.

Twenty-one-year-old Gerald Alders has missed Telstar’s last three outings and remains a doubt for this weekend.

Patrick Brouwer and Rui Mendes were also absent against Utrecht, although the former is likely to return to action in Sittard.

Tyrone Owusu has scored five goals and provided one assist in his last five matches for the visitors, making the midfielder one to watch.

Fortuna Sittard possible starting lineup:

Branderhorst; Pinto, Van Ottele, Marquez, Hubner, Dahlhaus; Ihattaren, Descotte, Brittijn, ‘t Zand; Zeefuik

Koeman Jr; Peersman, Soualhia, Valk, Caschili; Rossen, Ahmed; Demircan, Owusu, Martha; Thorisson

Fortuna are playing their best football in some time, and that momentum makes them strong favourites to edge an out-of-sorts Telstar side.

We expect the home side to make light work of their visitors and head into the international break on a high.

#2327. Celtic support group issues 'have a look' call over Dermot Desmond's stake

Celtic support group Celtic Supporters Limited has called for the Takeover Panel to examine issues surrounding Dermot Desmond’s shareholding in the club.

The group wants the body to look at the circumstances around Desmond’s stake and whether questions over influence and control merit further examination.

Speaking on the Let Me Be Frank podcast, Celtic Supporters Limited chairman Duncan Smillie stressed the support group was not accusing Desmond of wrongdoing, but believes there are grounds for the Takeover Panel to look at the matter.

Smillie said: “If a single shareholder takes their stake in a company above 30 per cent... they are obliged to make an offer for all the shares in a company. That happened to Dave King at Rangers.

“Dermot, in 2005... bought his shares pretty much at a third of the market price... took his shareholding up to 34 per cent or 35 per cent. That then triggers the Takeover Panel. One of the main things you have to guarantee is that you will not try to exert control on that club.

“We at CSL are not making an accusation; we’re not saying he’s done something he shouldn’t have done.

“All we’re asking the Takeover Panel is to have a look at this, because there’s some anecdotal and some real evidence that he has exerted control over the club.”

The intervention comes as Celtic Supporters Limited prepares to raise wider concerns over the club’s shareholding structure and the number of supporters who have become disconnected from their shares.

CSL director Brian McLaughlin said: “There are 37,000 shareholders in Celtic PLC, and many of them have become disconnected from their shares over the years... died, moved house, whatever it may be.

Celtic know they 'need strengthening' as January transfer intel emerges

WATCH: Ex-Celtic star nets first goal in new manager’s dramatic debut

Celtic handed major injury boost as key star returns to training

“They were given a piece of paper in 1994. That piece of paper could be anywhere, in the loft, in the bin, you know.

“Our AGM [resolution] is quite simply tasking Celtic... to go to the company that is the registrar of the shareholding... and say: reunite all our shareholders with their shares. Put the power that they’ve got back in their hands.”

The support group believes reconnecting shareholders with their stakes is important to restoring the balance between supporter and corporate ownership.

McLaughlin added: “When Fergus [McCann] sold his shares, it was always about having a balance between the fan shareholding and the corporate shareholding.

“And that balance is gone. It's corporate shareholding now. Between Desmond and Lindsell Train, they're well above 50 per cent.”

Smillie believes the number of disconnected shareholders can also affect how influential a major shareholder is in practical terms.

He said: “When you’ve got 100 per cent of your shares out there, and you’re a principal shareholder like Dermot Desmond is... let’s say you’ve got 35 per cent for argument's sake. If you’ve got 35 per cent of 100 per cent, that’s a genuine 35 per cent.

“But if there’s a whole chunk of those missing and you actually have 35 per cent of 80 per cent or 85 per cent, you start to have an even more dominant position.

“So it’s maybe not in his personal interest... I think the club like that everybody’s detached.”

Celtic Supporters Limited will now seek to put the share-register issue firmly on the agenda ahead of Celtic’s upcoming AGM.

By Byron David | 05 Nov 2026 12:43, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:40

Decision Day in MLS sees Chicago Fire searching for home advantage in the Best-of-Three series when they host Columbus Crew at Soldier Field on Saturday, November 7.

The visitors have every incentive to win the final regular-season match, as it would help them avoid the single-elimination wild card match.

Chicago have already done better than their regular-season performance from last term, as they cannot finish below fifth, which is three spots better than 2025.

The Fire will hope to go a step further, though, after the Philadelphia Union bundled them out in the Best-of-Three series last season.

Home advantage will be crucial for them going forward, but they must make it count on the final day to secure Soldier Field as the destination for the next phase of the playoffs.

Gregg Berhalter's charges are now unbeaten across their last three MLS fixtures, but they have not won any of their previous two games that they have hosted.

The Fire won the corresponding fixture last season, and avoided defeat in the reverse match earlier this term, but the visitors have a better head-to-head record.

Nonetheless, the hosts must win here to control their own destiny, as even a draw may not be enough for them to secure home advantage in the next round.

Alternatively, Chicago fans can quietly get behind Inter Miami, who will be entertaining Charlotte at the same time, as if the Herons win or draw with the Crown, then the Fire will be comfortably through to the next round, with the first game set to be at Soldier Field.

Columbus Crew have qualified for the playoffs in some form, but as things stand, they must contest the one-off elimination match.

Laurent Courtois will hope that his men can make a play for a Best-of-Three series spot with a win in Illinois.

The Black and Gold have won their last five MLS matches in a row, which means they will head into this fixture full of confidence.

The fact that they won three of the last four head-to-head matches in Chicago will further inspire them to cause an upset this weekend.

The visitors will secretly hope for Nashville to beat or draw with FC Cincinnati, which will open their path to the next phase of the competition.

It is mostly good news for the hosts as they head into the most important part of the MLS season, with South African defender Mbekezeli Mbokazi returning from injury.

Robert Lewandowski snapped an eight-game goal drought last weekend, when he came off the bench to hand his team the lead in their 4-2 win away to Colorado Rapids.

The Polish international then turned creator for Puso Dithejane to add some glitter to the scoreline, but he will certainly be important for Berhalter's men.

Another man that the home side will depend on is Ivorian Jonathan Bamba, who scored a brace last week, taking his goal contributions this season to 12.

The Fire will be without Robin Lod and Sam Rogers, who are both injured.

The Crew are sweating over the fitness of Brais Mendez, who has been excellent since joining as a Designated Player from Real Sociedad.

Wessam Abou Ali and Rudy Camacho are definitely out of contention for the trip to Chicago.

Dylan Chambost will be crucial for the visitors' creativity, as he has done recently for the Crew.

#2322. England squad: Choose Thomas Tuchel's 26 players for Spain, Croatia fixtures

By Ben Knapton | 05 Nov 2026 12:47

A spot in the UEFA Nations League knockout stages is on the line for England when they complete their League A campaign against Spain and Croatia during the November international break.

Thomas Tuchel's side kicked off Group 3 on a sour note with a 3-2 home defeat to the world champions, but back-to-back victories over the Czech Republic and a 7-0 crushing of Croatia on the road have put them on the brink of progression.

The Three Lions just need one point from their final two games to progress to the quarter-finals, and here, Sports Mole gives you the chance to pick your England squad for the November international break - be sure to download and share with your friends!

Tuchel should have no major quandaries on the goalkeeping front, as James Trafford and Jordan Pickford are shoo-ins, while Dean Henderson is likely to displace Brighton & Hove Albion veteran Jason Steele after recovering from injury.

However, the England boss now faces the age-old Ben White dilemma in defence, as the Arsenal right-back has stayed injury-free in recent weeks and has enjoyed a strong start to the campaign with the Premier League champions.

White has provided three assists in 12 games for the Gunners while starting seven of their nine top-flight fixtures, but he has not been selected for England since March; a hamstring injury ruled him out of the extended September/October period.

Tuchel must also weigh up whether to recall Harry Maguire, who has been a stalwart in an under-performing Manchester United side, and Djed Spence, who has forced his way into the Inter Milan XI after recovering from injury.

The biggest dilemma for the former Chelsea head coach undoubtedly comes in the final third of the field, though, as all eyes will be on whether Blues playmaker Cole Palmer is included in Tuchel's ranks.

Palmer pulled out of the September/October squad through injury, triggering a frustrated response from Tuchel, who admitted that the 24-year-old misses too many camps and his England future is potentially in jeopardy under the current regime.

However, a fully-fit Palmer could be offered a route back into the squad due to Bukayo Saka's injury, as the Arsenal winger is expected to be out until December with the quadriceps problem he sustained against Bayern Munich in the Champions League.

Noni Madueke and Phil Foden will also be fighting for attacking recalls, although the latter - who was also snubbed from the World Cup group - has already suggested that the November squad may come a little too soon for him.

Madueke, on the other hand, may be directly competing with Arsenal teammate Eberechi Eze, who was included in the September/October selection despite his fringe status at the Emirates.

Meanwhile, Dominic Calvert-Lewin was summoned to play second fiddle to Harry Kane last month, but having scored just once in his last five Premier League games, the Leeds United striker's spot could come under threat from Ollie Watkins.

#2321. Mikel Arteta admits Arsenal star 'needed' a good performance as star grabs chance

Mikel Arteta admitted Viktor Gyokeres “needed” a good performance after he starred in the 2-1 Champions League win at Slavia Prague.

Sweden striker Gyokeres made one goal and scored the other in one of his best displays since joining the Gunners in July 2025.

The 28-year-old has played second fiddle to Kai Havertz this season and had scored only one goal before Wednesday night, in the Carabao Cup penalty shoot-out defeat at Fleetwood last month.

But against Slavia, Gyokeres laid on the opener for Noni Madueke before scoring a stylish second on a hugely encouraging evening in the Czech capital.

Read more: This can't go on at Celtic – or can it? O'Neill fears and the McGregor dilemma

“I thought he was really good,” Gunners boss Arteta told reporters. “It was a game we believed would be really good for him, for the demands the opponent puts in and the spaces he could use.

“He’s done it really well, not just for the assist and the goal, but he was really connected to the team. It’s really great news for him, because he really needed that performance.

“He did that really well, understood really well when to appear, when to threaten, because they leave people, they follow man-to-man in the midfield and leave spaces there.

“So when I said he was really connected to the team, that was another example. I thought he had a great performance alongside the goal and assist.

“I’d imagine every player wants to play every game, and Viktor is no different.

“The players need to impact the game like he has done when they get the opportunity, to show the level they can produce. It’s really positive for the team.”

Despite his lack of minutes on the pitch, Gyokeres has now contributed to five goals in his last five matches.

“I feel very good,” he told Arsenal’s website. “Obviously, I’m happy with the win and to contribute to the two goals.”

Premier League leaders Arsenal are up to third in the Champions League table despite a wobble in the second half, when Wiktor Nowak’s 90th-minute goal left them clinging on throughout stoppage time.

“A good feeling in the first half – and then obviously in the second half, with how that went, maybe the feeling isn’t as good. But overall, if you look at the first half, it’s a good feeling,” added Gyokeres.

“It’s my job as a striker and of course I want to contribute every game when I’m on the pitch and want to be involved as much as I can in attacks.

“So far we’ve had a good run in the Champions League and in the league as well.

“At the stage we’re in right now, we’re in a good spot, but I think we’re all clear on what we can improve and what we need to do to take the next step.”

#2319. Gabigol senza rimpianti: "All’Inter e al Benfica non stavo bene, il Brasile è diverso"

Gabriel Barbosa, meglio conosciuto come Gabigol, è diventato uno dei grandi protagonisti del calcio brasiliano grazie ai gol e ai trofei conquistati soprattutto con il Flamengo. La sua avventura in Europa, però, non ha avuto lo stesso successo e il ricordo di Inter e Benfica non è accompagnato da particolare nostalgia.

In un’intervista a Globoesporte, l’attaccante del Santos ha raccontato il rapporto speciale costruito con alcuni club della sua carriera, spiegando invece le difficoltà vissute lontano dal Brasile: "Al Santos sono nella mia città, amo giocare qui. Quando ero al Flamengo guardavo tutte le partite del Santos. Con il Flamengo era la stessa cosa: i tifosi, il Maracanã pieno, la maglia. Mi sentivo molto bene lì. Vedevo qualcosa di diverso in quei tifosi", ha raccontato Gabigol.

Poi il confronto con le esperienze europee: "Forse all’Inter e al Benfica non è successo molto questo. Mio padre adorava quando ero al Benfica. Io invece non amavo il Portogallo. Qui è tutto perfetto e poi c’è anche la spiaggia. Sto iniziando a pensare che mi trovo bene solo nelle squadre che si trovano in posti con il mare". .

Gabigol arrivò all’Inter nel 2016 dopo essersi messo in mostra con il Santos, ma la sua esperienza in nerazzurro fu deludente. Dopo pochi mesi passò al Benfica, dove non riuscì comunque a lasciare il segno: appena un gol in cinque presenze: "L’Inter fece una proposta irrinunciabile. Avevo anche voglia di giocare in Europa. Poi le cose non andarono bene e andai al Benfica perché pensavo di avere più opportunità. Era una squadra portoghese, la lingua non sarebbe stata un problema", ha spiegato.

La svolta arrivò con il ritorno in Brasile e soprattutto con il Flamengo, dove Gabigol è diventato un simbolo: 161 gol in 306 partite con il Mengão. Dopo una parentesi complicata al Cruzeiro, il classe 1996 è tornato al Santos, dove in questa stagione ha già realizzato 25 reti e 11 assist in 49 incontri.

#2318. York City vs Northampton Town - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matthew Cooper | 05 Nov 2026 12:51

York City will be looking to get back to winning ways when they welcome Northampton Town to the LNER Community Stadium on Saturday for an FA Cup first round clash.

The hosts currently sit seventh in the League Two table, while the visitors are 21st.

York are looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup for just the second time since 2010, when they beat Rotherham United and Darlington before losing to Bolton Wanderers in the third round.

The Minstermen were a National League side back then, but they have since earned promotion to the EFL, winning the National League last season, and they currently occupy a League Two playoff spot.

However, they head into Saturday's cup tie after suffering successive away defeats against Barnet and Grimsby Town.

Scott Lindsey's side lost 3-1 at Barnet before suffering an even more damaging 5-2 loss at Grimsby four days later, despite initially taking the lead through Alex Hunt and Jack Hunt.

As a result, York will be looking to produce a much-improved performance against Northampton and end their losing run.

Northampton, meanwhile, were relegated from League One last season and have made a poor start to life back in League Two.

The Cobblers currently sit just two points above the relegation zone and will be hoping an FA Cup run can provide a much-needed boost.

Northampton have failed to get past the first round of the competition for the last six years and Chris Hogg's side head into Saturday's tie off the back of a 1-0 defeat to Oldham Athletic on Tuesday.

However, it is worth noting that Northampton have not lost to York in their last five meetings across all competitions, including a 1-1 draw at the LNER Community Stadium last month.

Northampton Town form (all competitions):

York could make some changes after their heavy loss to Grimsby last weekend, with Alex Newby in line to replace Joe Grey.

Newby is York's joint top scorer alongside Ollie Pearce with six goals, but he has not found the back of the net in his last seven appearances and will be keen to rediscover some form.

Northampton will be hopeful Sam Hoskins's hamstring injury is not too serious, but it appears unlikely he will be able to feature against York.

As a result, Matty Warhurst is expected to lead the line, with support from Owen Dale and Joe O'Brien-Whitmarsh.

McNally; Fallowfield, Johnson, Kitching; J. Hunt, A. Hunt, Read, Boyes; Newby, Pearce; Stones

Northampton Town possible starting lineup:

Jeacock; Donacien, Moore, Guthrie; Wormleighton, L. Evans, C. Evans, Powell; Dale, O'Brien-Whitmarsh; Warhurst

Despite suffering two disappointing defeats in recent weeks, York have made a good start to the season and should have enough to beat a struggling Northampton.

#2317. Date and time of Celtic AGM revealed as 18 resolutions on table

Celtic have announced the date of the club's AGM for 2026.

The annual general meeting will take place on Tuesday, December 1. It will be held at Celtic Park with a start time of 12pm. The notice for the plans for the AGM is available on the club website.

The AGM agenda makes clear that there will be 18 resolutions.

The resolutions to be considered are as follows:

13.That, pursuant to section 551 of the Companies Act 2006 and Article 6 of the Company'sarticles of association ("Articles"), the directors be and are generally and unconditionally

This authority is in substitution for all existing authorities under section 551 of the Companies Act 2006 which authorities, to the extent unused at the date of this resolution, are revoked with immediate effect.

14. That, subject to the passing of Resolution 13 and pursuant to section 570 of the Companies Act 2006 (and Article 6 of the Articles), the directors be and are generally empowered to allot equity securities (within the meaning of section 560 of the Companies Act 2006) for cash pursuant to the authority conferred by Resolution 13 as if section 561(1) of the Companies Act 2006 did not apply to any such allotment, provided that this power shall be limited to:

14.1 the allotment of equity securities in connection with an offer (whether by way of a rights issue, open offer or otherwise):

14.1.1 to holders of ordinary shares (within the meaning of section 560(1) of the Companies Act 2006) in the capital of the Company in proportion (as nearly as practicable) to the respective numbers of ordinary shares held by them; and

14.1.2 to holders of other equity securities in the capital of the Company, as required by the rights of those securities, or subject to such rights, as the directors otherwise consider necessary,

but subject to such exclusions or other arrangements as the directors may deem necessary or expedient in relation to treasury shares, record dates, fractional entitlements or any legal or practical problems under the laws of any territory or the requirements of any regulatory body or stock exchange;

14.2 the allotment of equity securities in place of a cash dividend pursuant to any authority conferred upon the directors in accordance with and pursuant to Article 40.8 of the Articles; and

14.3 the allotment of equity securities otherwise than pursuant to sub-paragraphs 14.1 and 14.2 above, up to an aggregate nominal amount of £47,577.62;

and (unless previously revoked, varied or renewed) this power shall expire on the earlier of 1 March 2028 and the conclusion of the next annual general meeting of the Company after the passing of this resolution, save that the Company may make an offer or agreement before the expiry of this power which would or might require equity securities to be allotted for cash after such expiry and the directors may allot equity securities pursuant to any such offer or agreement as if this power had not expired.

This power is in substitution for all existing powers under section 570 of the Companies Act 2006 which powers, to the extent unused at the date of this resolution, are revoked with immediate effect.

Resolutions 15, 16, 17 and 18 are ordinary resolutions, requisitioned on behalf of members of the Company in accordance with section 338 of the Companies Act 2006 and are not proposed by the directors:

15. That, the members of Celtic plc, noting the number of shareholders who have become untraced and whose dividend and other entitlements remain unclaimed, request the Board to appoint Georgeson, the asset reunification service operated by the Company's registrar, Computershare Investor Services PLC, to carry out a shareholder tracing and asset reunification programme on the customary contingent fee basis under which that service is provided at no upfront cost to the Company, and to report to shareholders on the programme at the next Annual General Meeting.

16. This AGM instructs the Board to prepare a strategic review and a published report with a football, financial, and strategic plan (medium term I.e. 3-5 years). The plan should contain a clear stated strategic aim and measurable football and financial goals for the Board to target. Remuneration should be linked to achieving those goals.

17. This AGM instructs the Chairman of the Board of Directors of Celtic PLC to:

17.1 Undertake, in cooperation with elected representatives of the Celtic support, a full review of the security operations at Celtic Park. The aim of such review to examine, among other things:

17.1.1 The training and management of security staff to assess their understanding of how best to ensure the safety and security of supporters.

17.1.2 The training and management of security staff to assess the extent to which medical emergencies are responded to appropriately.

17.1.3 Any systems intended to capture lessons from any incidents that do take place.

17.1.4 The recruitment of senior staff inside the security department; the extent to which they understand their role and the extent to which they are able to work with other departments (chiefly supporter liaison) to ensure the safety of supporters and their respectful treatment.

18. This AGM instructs the Chairman of the Board of Directors of Celtic PLC to:

18.1.1 Issue a full and unreserved apology to the shareholders and supporters of Celtic FC for giving a platform at the 2025 AGM to an individual, who holds no role within the governance of Celtic PLC, which he then abused to insult and denigrate our shareholders and our supporters.

18.1.2 To publicly commit to ensure that such an abuse of the AGM is never allowed to happen again.

The confirmation of the AGM plans come after fan groups called on Dermot Desmond to attend the AGM in person for the first time in 20 years.

A Celtic Fans Collective statement read: A Celtic Fans Collective statement read: "For twenty years, Dermot Desmond has not attended Celtic's Annual General Meeting, despite wielding considerable influence over the direction and decision-making of our club.

"Although only a minority shareholder, Desmond appears to exercise influence far beyond his shareholding. With that influence comes a responsibility to be accountable.

"We call on Dermot Desmond to publicly commit to attending the 2026 AGM in person and answer questions about his role in the running of Celtic Football Club.

"Sending his son, another representative or a prepared statement in his place is no substitute for attending personally.

"The decision by Peter Lawwell to prematurely conclude last year's AGM left a clear impression among supporters that the Board had taken an opportunity to avoid scrutiny. Responsibility for how that meeting was conducted and ultimately concluded rests entirely with the Board.

"This year, however, the focus must be firmly on scrutiny rather than protest. There is a clear understanding across the support that the AGM must provide the opportunity for important questions to be put directly to those running the Club, without allowing scrutiny to be evaded once again.

"Celtic supporters have repeatedly sought meaningful dialogue and a more constructive relationship with the Club.

"At a time when fans want to see decisive leadership, a public commitment from Dermot Desmond to attend the AGM in good faith would be an important demonstration of his willingness to engage directly with supporters and shareholders."

#2316. Malaga vs Racing de Santander - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 05 Nov 2026 12:55, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 12:57

Malaga's search for their first victory of the 2026-27 La Liga campaign will continue on Saturday afternoon when they welcome Racing de Santander to Estadio La Rosaleda.

The hosts will enter the match off the back of a 2-1 defeat to Real Betis, while Racing suffered a 3-0 home loss to Real Madrid last time out.

Malaga boast a record of five draws and six defeats from their 11 matches in Spain's top flight this season, with five points leaving them at the bottom of the La Liga table.

The White and Sky Blues did record a 3-0 victory over Alcazar in the Copa del Rey on October 27, but they were unable to back that up, losing 2-1 against Real Betis in their last league fixture.

Juan Francisco Funes' side are already five points from the safety of 17th spot and will certainly be wary of that gap widening ahead of the November international break.

Malaga have faced Racing on 30 previous occasions in all competitions and lead the head-to-head, having posted 13 victories to the latter's nine.

That said, Malaga are winless in their last six matches against Racing, with their last success in this fixture coming back in February 2020 in the Segunda Division.

Racing picked up a point on their last visit to Malaga - in last season's Segunda Division campaign - while they have been victorious in three of the last five games between the two sides, which is a record that will hand them confidence.

The Green and Whites were beaten 3-0 by Real Madrid last time out, with the result leaving them 16th in the La Liga table, picking up 11 points from 11 matches courtesy of a record of three wins, two draws and six defeats.

Jose Alberto's team actually have the worst defensive record in Spain's top flight this season, conceding 29 times in their 11 games, which is eight more than Malaga.

That said, Racing have been relatively strong down the other end, scoring 15 goals, while Malaga have only found the back of the net on eight occasions.

These are the type of matches that could have a big say in determining whether Malaga, Racing or both teams are capable of avoiding relegation this season.

Racing de Santander form (all competitions):

Malaga can certainly take plenty of positives from their performance against Real Betis last time out, so it would not be a surprise to see the same XI take to the field.

Adrian Nino has scored five times in seven league appearances for Malaga this season and will once again feature in the final third of the field for the hosts.

However, all of Juan Cruz, Angel Recio, Diego Murillo, Pablo Martinez, Joaquin Munoz, Julen Lopete and Aaron Ochoa are set to miss the match due to fitness issues.

As for Racing, Jorge Salinas will make a welcome return to the squad, having served his suspension against Real Madrid last time out.

However, Andres Martin is once again expected to miss out with a muscular issue; Alvaro Mantilla is also set to be absent with the injury that forced him off early against Real Madrid.

Yassir Zabiri has scored eight times in 11 league appearances this season, and the 21-year-old will continue in the final third of the field.

Herrero; Puga, Calero, Galilea, Rafita; Enriquez; Larrubia, Dotor, Lorenzo, Aznou; Nino

Racing de Santander possible starting lineup:

Agirrezabala; Felipe, Ramon, Belocian, Salinas; I Martin, Prati; Luque, Canales, Vicente; Zabiri

Malaga's winless run will end at some stage, but we are not expecting it to happen this weekend; Racing are capable of picking up a point on the road in this fixture.

#2314. Il capitano di Cassano nella Primavera del Como è stato convocato dal Perù: chi è Andrealli

Dalla fascia da capitano della Primavera del Como alla convocazione con la Nazionale maggiore del Perù. Per Francesco Andrealli, classe 2007, in queste ore si è aperta una nuova fase di un percorso calcistico iniziato molto presto proprio sulle rive del Lario. Nato a Como il 5 agosto 2007, il centrocampista è entrato nel settore giovanile biancoblù quando aveva appena otto anni, completando nel vivaio lariano gran parte della propria formazione umana e professionale. Il suo percorso di crescita lo ha portato progressivamente a diventare uno dei riferimenti della formazione giovanile dei lariani, condividendo peraltro il reparto con Riccardo Cassano, fresco di chiamata - a sorpresa - dall'Italia di Mancini.

Andrealli è un centrocampista centrale che fa dell'equilibrio una delle proprie qualità principali. Si distingue per la capacità di recuperare palloni e per la gestione del possesso, caratteristiche che gli permettono di dare ordine alla manovra e garantire sostegno alla squadra in entrambe le fasi di gioco. È un profilo che abbina attenzione tattica e qualità tecniche: sa contribuire al lavoro di interdizione, ma anche accompagnare l'azione offensiva quando la situazione lo richiede. La sua disponibilità al lavoro e la capacità di interpretare compiti differenti nel reparto gli consentono di offrire soluzioni sia in fase di copertura sia nella costruzione della manovra.

Andrealli è nato in Italia, ma ha origini peruviane da parte di madre. Ha scelto di rappresentare il Paese andino, con il quale aveva già avuto modo di confrontarsi attraverso le selezioni giovanili. La recente chiamata del commissario tecnico Mano Menezes per la Nazionale maggiore rappresenta un'opportunità importante per misurarsi con un livello competitivo diverso e proseguire il proprio percorso internazionale. Per il Como, intanto, è un'altra conferma del lavoro svolto anche a livello di settore giovanile.

#2313. Bradford City vs Marine AFC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 13:10, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 13:18

Aiming to cause a major FA Cup upset this weekend, Marine AFC travel from Merseyside to Yorkshire to face League One side Bradford City on Saturday afternoon.

The Bantams head into the cup tie without a league win in their last few outings and on the back of a weekend defeat to local rivals Huddersfield Town, while the Mariners are also winless in three matches and have slipped to 18th in the National League North table.

Bradford City will be looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup for the first time in two years when they welcome opponents from three divisions below them.

The Bantams, now led by Michael Duff following Graham Alexander's departure to Preston North End, have enjoyed some memorable cup runs over the past decade, with their journey to the quarter-finals in the 2014-15 season a notable highlight.

In January 2015, Bradford travelled to Stamford Bridge to face Jose Mourinho's Chelsea, producing one of the biggest upsets in FA Cup history as they came from two goals down after 38 minutes to secure a remarkable 4-2 victory over a side competing in the Champions League at the same time.

At the time, Chelsea were top of the Premier League while Bradford were sixth in League One, leaving 49 places between them, 20 fewer than the gap separating the Bantams from their upcoming opponents.

More recently, Bradford are still in League One, although just one win in their last five league matches has seen them slip out of the playoff places and down to seventh, seven points behind the division's leaders.

As for Marine, they make the trip to Valley Parade without a win in their last three matches, having followed a draw against Oxford City with defeats to Chester and Buxton in the National League North.

Indeed, Bobby Grant's side last tasted victory in the FA Cup, when a 2-0 win over Hartlepool United secured their place in the first round proper.

Like their upcoming opponents, the Mariners have enjoyed a famous FA Cup occasion of their own, with their 2021 meeting with Tottenham Hotspur at Rossett Park remaining a standout moment in the club's history.

The third-round tie, also against a Mourinho side, took place during the height of the COVID-19 pandemic and ended in a 5-0 victory for Spurs, with Carlos Vinicius scoring a 13-minute hat-trick.

The 161-place gap between the clubs was the largest in FA Cup history, while the sale of 32,202 "virtual tickets" raised more than £300,000 for Marine.

Since that memorable occasion, the Mariners have spent three consecutive campaigns in the National League North, where they have won just five of their opening 14 matches this season and sit 18th, only one point clear of the relegation zone.

Duff was always likely to make changes for a cup tie against opposition three divisions below Bradford, but injuries sustained in the defeat to Huddersfield could limit his options.

Adam Phillips was introduced at half-time in that match but was forced off four minutes from full-time after picking up an injury, making him unlikely to feature on Saturday.

Phillips will join Stephen Humphrys and Josh Neufville in the treatment room.

Captain Antoni Sarcevic was sent off during the Yorkshire derby last weekend, but he is available for the cup tie as his suspension only applies to league fixtures.

As for the visitors, Grant could name the same starting XI that lost 4-2 to Buxton on Halloween.

Goalkeeper Jack McIntyre has made 11 appearances this season and kept two clean sheets, and he is expected to start between the posts at Valley Parade.

Finlay Sinclair-Smith has provided four assists and scored twice in the National League North this season, and he could feature in attack alongside Charlie Jolley.

Dawson; Pennington, Baldwin, Pratt; O'Keeffe, Sarcevic, Pointon, Touray; Powell, Beesley, Swan

With 69 league places currently separating the sides, Bradford are clear favourites to progress and should have too much quality for their non-league visitors.

Marine will hope to make life difficult for the hosts, but we expect the Bantams to take control and secure a comfortable victory.

#2309. St Johnstone vs Dundee - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 13:21

St Johnstone will be looking to end their winless run when they welcome Dundee to McDiarmid Park for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership on Saturday.

The hosts are seventh in the standings with 14 points from 12 games, while the visitors are fifth with 17 points after 11 league matches.

St Johnstone have enjoyed an encouraging start to their first season back in the Scottish Premiership following their Championship-winning campaign last season, which earned them an immediate promotion back to the top division.

Simo Valakari's side have won three, drawn five and lost four of their 12 fixtures to date, leaving them seventh in the standings with 14 points, five points above the bottom two and only two points behind the top six.

The Saints are, however, enduring their longest winless run of the league campaign heading into this match, with St Johnstone failing to emerge victorious from any of their last four Scottish Premiership games, recording three draws and one defeat.

St Johnstone's biggest issue during that run has been their vulnerability in defence, with nine of the 19 goals they have conceded in the league this season coming across those four matches, including a disappointing 4-0 defeat to Rangers.

However, the Saints have also demonstrated a superb mentality and resilience, coming from behind to secure 2-2 draws with both Motherwell and Kilmarnock in their last two matches, including scoring twice with 10-men against Killie last time out.

Eager to build on those results and avoid recording a fifth straight winless match, St Johnstone will be looking to secure their first home win since beating Hibernian 2-1 at the beginning of September, as well as aiming to defeat Dundee for the first time since October 2024, when they won 2-1 on the road.

Dundee, meanwhile, head into Saturday's fixture off the back of a confidence-boosting win against Kilmarnock, with Ashley Hay scoring twice in the final 10 minutes to secure the 3-1 victory for Steven Pressley's side.

That victory lifted Dundee to fourth in the Scottish Premiership table with 17 points from 11 games, although they have since fallen behind Falkirk, following the Bairns' 2-0 victory against Celtic on matchday 12.

Dundee's matchday 12 clash with Hibernian was postponed due to Hibs' involvement in the Scottish League Cup semi-finals, meaning the Dee have also been afforded three days extra rest than their hosts.

Despite Dundee's superb start to the season, Pressley's side have face difficulties on the road, winning two and losing three of their five away fixtures in the Scottish Premiership.

However, the Dee will be able to draw confidence from their formidable recent record at McDiarmid Park, having emerged victorious from each of their last three visits to St Johnstone's home ground, while they are also unbeaten in the last five away clashes with the Saints.

St Johnstone will remain without Sam Stanton and Zach Mitchell due to ongoing injury problems, while Tom Steward is suspended after his 13th-minute red card against Kilmarnock on matchday 12.

Steward should be replaced by Ross Sinclair between the posts, with an unchanged back three of Cheick Diabate, Bozo Mikulic and Enes Alic likely to feature.

Meanwhile, Bobby Wales, Finlay Robertson and Simon Murray are all unavailable due to injury issues, while the remainder of the squad should be fit and fully rested after Dundee's matchday 12 match was postponed.

Hay scored two late goals to seal the victory for Dundee in their last outing, and the striker should continue alongside Bradley Fink, with Joe Bevan and Alan Forrest providing support from the wings.

Sinclair; Diabate, Mikulic, Alic; Todd, McAlear, Holt, Cotterill; Fowler, Steven; Schmidt

Goodman; D Wright, J Wright, Astley, Samuels; Bevan, Jones, Hamilton, Forrest; Hay, Fink

St Johnstone have a lacklustre recent record against Dundee, especially at home, and head into this match off the back of their longest Scottish Premiership winless run this term.

Given Dundee have also had three days extra rest after their matchday 12 fixture was postponed, we are predicting the away side to secure all three points.
