#2307. Carlisle United vs Salford City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matthew Cooper | 05 Nov 2026 13:27

Carlisle United will be looking to cause an upset when they welcome Salford City to Brunton Park on Saturday in the first round of the FA Cup.

The hosts currently sit eighth in the National League, while the visitors are top of League Two.

Carlisle head into Saturday's tie in excellent form, having gone unbeaten in the last seven matches across all competitions with six wins and one draw.

That run includes a 2-1 win over Tamworth to reach the first round of the FA Cup and it is also worth noting that Carlisle pulled off a major upset in last year's competition.

The Cumbrians beat League One side Reading 3-2 in the first round thanks to a dramatic late hat trick from Regan Linney as they defeated a club two divisions above them for the first time in the FA Cup.

Rob Elliot's side will be hoping lightning can strike twice this weekend and they do have a historic edge over Salford, having only lost to them twice in their nine meetings across all competitions.

Salford have enjoyed an excellent start to the new season, with their impressive record of 10 wins, one draw and three defeats leaving them five points clear at the top of League Two.

Peter Cklamovski's side are unbeaten in their last eight games and have won their last five, including a 2-1 win over Exeter City in their most recent match last weekend.

Salford were forced to come from behind after Exeter took the lead in the first half, with Luke Garbutt netting a second half equaliser before Will Aimson scored a stunner from long range to earn an important win.

The Ammies have enjoyed strong showings in the FA Cup in recent years, reaching the third in 2025 and the fourth round in 2026 where they were beaten on both occassions by Manchester City.

Carlisle could be forced to make a change, with Teddy Jenks a doubt after picking up a knock against Altrincham.

Stephen Wearne is likely to replace Jenks in midfield, while David Ajiboye is expected to partner Linney up front.

Salford could also make changes after Luke Molyneux was forced off against Exeter with an injury and Daniel Udoh is expected replace him out wide.

Macaulay Langstaff is expected to lead the line, while Matt Butcher could come into the side for Brendan Wiredu.

Carlisle United possible starting lineup:

Both sides are in impressive form, but Salford are a division above Carlisle for a reason and should have enough quality to emerge victorious.

#2305. FC Thun vs Hearts almost immediately halted as pyro smoke fills stadium

The Conference League match between FC Thun and Heart of Midlothian had to be halted almost immediately.

The match referee stopped the match with less than a minute played due to smoke filling the stadium in Switzerland.

Pyrotechnic devices had been set off in the home end of the stadium, leading to very poor visibility on the pitch.

The match had to be stopped until the smoke cleared. The delay lasted around eight minutes before play was resumed for the Euro clash.

A Hearts statement on social media read: "Well, we did kick-off, but the game was almost immediately stopped, due to smoke from pyrotechnic devices let off in the home end.

"Should be back underway in the next minute or so."

#2304. Aberdeen vs Hibernian - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 13:54

Aberdeen and Hibernian will meet at Pittodrie Stadium on Saturday evening for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership.

The hosts are 10th in the standings with 11 points from 11 games, while the visitors are bottom of the table with eight points from 11 fixtures.

Aberdeen have endured a lacklustre start to the 2026-27 Scottish Premiership campaign, with Stephen Robinson's side sitting 10th in the standings after 11 league games.

The Dons have recorded only three wins and two draws during that time, alongside six defeats, leaving them narrowly just two points above the drop zone and three points above their bottom-placed visitors.

Aberdeen do have a game in hand on the three teams above them, meaning they will have the opportunity to climb up the standings if they secure a victory, while a win on Saturday could also be crucial to avoid slipping into the drop zone.

The Dons will, however, head into the matchday 13 clash short on confidence after suffering three straight defeats, with Scottish Premiership losses to Dundee United (3-2) and Falkirk (1-0) and a heartbreaking late 1-0 loss to Rangers in the Scottish League Cup semi-final last time out.

With their full focus now on improving their league form, the under-pressure Robinson will be desperate for his side to snap their three-game losing streak and claim all three points when they welcome Hibernian to Pittodrie.

Aberdeen have been at their best when playing at their home ground in the league this season, claiming seven of their 11 points from home fixtures, after two wins, one draw and three defeats.

Hibernian, however, have been at their strongest when playing on the road, accumulating seven of their eight points from away matches in the Scottish Premiership.

Hibs have won two, drawn one and lost two of their five away league fixtures, with their disastrous home form the main reason behind their current standing at the bottom of the Scottish Premiership table.

Hibernian remain the only team in the division yet to win a home match in the league this season, with one draw and five defeats from six games played at Easter Road Stadium.

That miserable home form played a large part in David Gray's dismissal in mid-September, with the club legend dismissed after losing 1-0 to Kilmarnock, which marked their sixth consecutive home league defeat.

New boss Marink Reedijk failed to make an immediate impact upon his appointment in early October, losing four and drawing one of his four league fixtures at the helm, but the new boss did finally taste victory for the first time as Hibernian manager at the weekend.

Hibernian came from behind to defeat city rivals Hearts 2-1 in the Scottish League Cup semi-final, booking their place in the final against Rangers and earning plenty of favour for struggling boss Reedijk.

The manner of the victory will be particularly pleasing, with Hibernian creating 2.89 expected goals (xG) to Hearts' 1.01xG, and Hibs, who are the Scottish Premiership's lowest goalscorers with 11 goals, will be hoping to demonstrate that attacking ability once again on Saturday.

Hibernian had the better of this fixture throughout the 2025-26 season, winning two, losing one and drawing one of their three clashes with Aberdeen, including winning 2-1 at Pittodrie in October 2025.

Aberdeen do not have any injury concerns or absences heading into Saturday's encounter, leaving Robinson with a fully available squad to choose from.

Robinson is expected to make several changes from the team that lost to Rangers in the Scottish League Cup semi-final, with the likes of Chris Cadden, Stuart Armstrong and Lewis Smith, among others, potentially in line to start.

Meanwhile, Hibernian are unable to call upon the services of Callum Wright, Nathan lowe, Jordan Obita, Kanayochukwu Megwa and Rocky Bushiri due to ongoing injury issues.

Owen Elding, Jamie McGrath and Martin Boyle all played key parts in their superb 2-1 semi-final win against Hearts last time out, and the trio should all continue their relationship in attack on Saturday evening.

Muller; Cadden, Happe, Molloy, Guinness-Walker; Lyons, Afeez; Smith, Nisbet, Armstrong, Mouloua

Sallinger; Passlack, Kerr, Ivezic, Mayor; Newell, Chaiwa; McGrath, Mulligan, Boyle; Elding

Hibernian's confidence will be hugely raised after their much-needed win at the weekend, and with Aberdeen's morale dampened by a third straight defeat, we are backing the boosted visitors to secure the win.

#2303. Lesione all'adduttore per Giovanni Di Lorenzo

Il Napoli è stato impegnato in una seduta pomeridiana di allenamento nella quale ha preso parte anche Olivera

Secondo quanto raccolto dalle ultime notizie di Alfredo Pedullà, Giovanni di Lorenzo ha riportato una lesione all'aduttore. Il Napoli, in vista della prossima sfida contro la Lazio, è stato impegnato in una seduta pomeridiana di allenamento nella quale ha preso parte anche Olivera.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Gli azzurri si sono allenati in vista del prossimo match di campionato, domenica alle 12:30 al Maradona contro la Lazio. Giovanni Di Lorenzo si è sottoposto a esami strumentali, presso il Pineta Grande Hospital, che hanno evidenziato una lesione distrattiva del muscolo adduttore lungo della coscia sinistra. Il calciatore azzurro ha già iniziato l'iter riabilitativo.

#2302. Varela convocato dal Portogallo: il Monza si gode il bomber che piace anche al Barcellona

Gustavo Varela sta vivendo il momento più importante della sua carriera. L'attaccante del Monza, arrivato in estate dal Benfica dopo l'ultima stagione in prestito al Gil Vicente, ha ricevuto la prima convocazione dalla nazionale portoghese e, poche ore dopo, è stato premiato come "Rising Star" del mese di ottobre in Serie A.

Un riconoscimento meritato per un giocatore che ha trasformato ogni partita in una vetrina: cinque gol nelle ultime cinque gare, 9 nelle prime 9 giornate di campionato, un rendimento che gli ha permesso di portarsi in vetta alla classifica marcatori. Numeri sorprendenti considerando che nella scorsa stagione si era fermato a sei reti complessive.

La crescita di Varela non è passata inosservata nemmeno al commissario tecnico del Portogallo, Jorge Jesus, che ha deciso di inserirlo nel gruppo dei convocati: "È il capocannoniere del campionato italiano. Per riuscirci serve una qualità notevole. Partendo da questo presupposto ho deciso di dargli fiducia, anche perché non dobbiamo dimenticare che ha solo 21 anni", ha spiegato il CT lusitano. che deve far fronte all'addio di Cristiano Ronaldo con nuove soluzioni.

I suoi gol sono ormai diventati una costante per il Monza, che però dovrà presto fare i conti con l'interesse dei grandi club. Tra le squadre che hanno iniziato a monitorare la situazione ci sarebbe anche il Barcellona; adesso la società brianzola vive un dilemma non semplice: da una parte c'è la necessità di raggiungere la salvezza e trattenere il miglior giocatore della rosa, dall'altra la consapevolezza che il valore di Varela potrebbe raggiungere cifre importanti.

La storia recente del mercato e della Serie A - vedi Piatek - insegna che certe esplosioni possono essere difficili da replicare. Per questo, davanti a un'offerta superiore ai 25 milioni di euro, il Monza potrebbe essere chiamato a riflettere sulla possibilità di monetizzare e reinvestire. Anche il Benfica osserva con attenzione: il club portoghese ha mantenuto una clausola di riacquisto (e ha il 20% sulla rivendita), con la possibilità di pareggiare eventuali proposte provenienti da altre società. Una situazione che rende ancora più interessante il futuro di un attaccante che, in pochi mesi, è passato dall'essere una semplice promessa a uno dei nomi più seguiti della Serie A.

#2300. Accrington Stanley vs Wycombe Wanderers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 14:11, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:21

Both looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup once again, League Two side Accrington Stanley welcome third-tier opponents Wycombe Wanderers to the Crown Ground on Saturday afternoon, with both clubs entering the competition at this stage.

The Reds are enjoying their best spell of the season and head into the weekend's clash on the back of a 10-game unbeaten run, while the Chairboys continued their push towards the playoff places last week with a 2-0 victory over Burton Albion.

Accrington Stanley would need to win three matches in this season's FA Cup to equal their best run in the competition, having most recently reached the fourth round during the 2022-23 campaign before being denied another opportunity to repeat that achievement in 2025 when a trip to Anfield ended in a 4-0 defeat.

John Doolan remains in charge of the Reds and will oversee his 135th match as Accrington boss on Saturday, with his side currently enjoying one of the most encouraging spells of his tenure.

The Reds endured a difficult start to the campaign, recording just one victory in their opening eight matches across all competitions, but they have since turned their fortunes around and remain unbeaten since early September.

Impressive victories over top-half opponents such as Cheltenham Town and Gillingham have helped Doolan's side climb to ninth in the League Two table and extend their unbeaten run to 10 matches across league and EFL Trophy action, although seven of those fixtures have ended level after 90 minutes.

Two of those draws came in cup competitions and were ultimately settled by penalty shootouts, but Accrington have still drawn half of their league matches this season, with no side in the English Football League recording more.

Wycombe Wanderers, meanwhile, make the 200-mile-plus journey from Buckinghamshire to Lancashire hoping to extend their unbeaten run to five matches after enjoying an encouraging start under their new manager.

Michael Duff was relieved of his duties at the beginning of September and replaced by Tom Hounsell, who initially took charge on an interim basis before being handed the job permanently following an impressive start.

Since his arrival, the Chairboys have suffered just one defeat in 10 matches across all competitions, losing 4-1 to Luton Town, while their improved results have lifted them from the relegation zone to 13th in the League One table, leaving them four points adrift of the top six.

Their most recent victory came last weekend, when goals from Jayden Wareham and Cauley Woodrow secured a comfortable 2-0 win over Burton Albion.

Hounsell has overseen a 60% win rate since taking charge, marking an impressive beginning to his time at Adams Park, and he will now prepare for his first FA Cup fixture as Wycombe look to reach the second round for the fourth consecutive season.

Accrington Stanley form (all competitions):

Wycombe Wanderers form (all competitions):

Accrington Stanley could have the option of naming an unchanged XI from the side that drew with Northampton Town at the weekend, although the availability of one of their leading goalscorers remains uncertain.

Josh Woods has found the net seven times in 12 League Two appearances this season but missed the latest fixture and could remain unavailable for Saturday's cup tie.

Louie Moulden is expected to retain his place between the posts, with Devon Matthews, Farrend Rawson and Joseph Anderson likely to continue as part of a familiar back three.

As for the visiting Chairboys, Hounsell could take the opportunity to rotate his squad and rest a few key players with a demanding run of fixtures awaiting his side over the coming weeks.

Former England and Liverpool striker Danny Ings has largely been used from the bench since joining Wycombe but could be handed a starting opportunity in the FA Cup this weekend.

Wareham, however, strengthened his case for another start with his sixth goal in just nine appearances last time out, giving Hounsell a decision to make.

Conor Hazard has been Wycombe's first-choice goalkeeper throughout the season but could make way for Joe Wildsmith, who featured in the club's most recent EFL Trophy fixture.

Accrington Stanley possible starting lineup:

Moulden; Matthews, Rawson, Anderson; Love, Borland, Mols, Tutonda; Henderson; Walton, Warren

Wycombe Wanderers possible starting lineup:

Although a victory for Accrington would not rank among the biggest shocks in the FA Cup, we still believe the League Two outfit will struggle to match the quality and depth available to their third-tier visitors.

The Reds have shown plenty of resilience during their unbeaten run and should make life difficult for Wycombe, but we expect Hounsell's side to have enough attacking quality to secure their place in the second round.

Swindon will be hoping top scorer Aaron Drinan can fire against MK Dons, having bagged eight goals so far this season.

MK Dons will have to make one change after Sam Silvera suffered an injury against Doncaster, with Gethin Jones likely to replace him.

#2296. Como, il 9-1 al Lens è solo l'inizio: il ds Ludi e Fabregas svelano i progetti per il futuro

Il 9-1 rifilato al Lens in Champions League ha acceso definitivamente i riflettori sul Como anche a livello internazionale, ma dietro la straordinaria crescita della squadra di Cesc Fabregas c'è un progetto che va ben oltre i risultati del campo. A raccontarne i meccanismi questo venerdì è stato il direttore sportivo Carlalberto Ludi, che ha sottolineato l'importanza del lavoro di scouting e reclutamento costruito dalla società: "La risposta dovrebbe partire dal processo di recruitment che abbiamo costruito internamente, che va dall'algoritmo alla nostra sensibilità tecnica, fino al feedback di Fabregas". Un modello che unisce analisi dei dati, valutazioni tecniche e caratteristiche funzionali all'identità di gioco voluta dall'allenatore. L'esempio perfetto? Anastasios Douvikas, arrivato dal Celta Vigo per 13 milioni e oggi protagonista con già 11 gol stagionali.

L'entusiasmo non manca, ma a Como nessuno vuole bruciare le tappe. Ludi nella medesima intervista ha allontanato le ambizioni scudetto, spiegando che l'obiettivo principale oggi è riuscire a mantenere competitività ogni tre giorni, consolidando un percorso di crescita ancora giovane. Anche mister Cesc Fabregas guarda avanti, consapevole che il salto di qualità passa dalla continuità e dalla capacità di confrontarsi con le grandi d'Europa: "È giusto goderci questo momento perché il gruppo se lo merita, ma il calcio viaggia a velocità altissima. Ai ragazzi ho già detto che dobbiamo archiviare Lens e pensare solo all’Inter. Ci aspetta una partita durissima in casa dei campioni d’Italia in carica, sarà una sfida molto dispendiosa dal punto di vista fisico e mentale, abbiamo poco tempo ma dobbiamo prepararla al meglio".

La crescita del Como, però, non si fermerà alla prima squadra. Sempre Ludi ha individuato nelle infrastrutture, nello stadio e nel settore giovanile le principali aree su cui concentrare i prossimi investimenti, salvaguardando comunque la qualità della rosa. In quest'ottica assume particolare valore anche il percorso di Matteo Papaccioli, giovane cresciuto nell'accademia lariana e capace di trovare il primo gol in Champions League proprio contro il Lens. "Sono molto contento per Papa, tutti noi lo siamo. Quando ha segnato il 9-1 non sono riuscito a trattenermi e siamo andati tutti da lui. Non è stata una mancanza di rispetto ma una reazione istintiva dopo tutto ciò che questo ragazzo ha vissuto. Papa ha iniziato ad allenarsi con noi quando aveva 16 anni, poi si è rotto il crociato ed è stato fuori 9 mesi. È tornato poco tempo fa e ora ha avuto l’opportunità di fare gol", è la fotografia finale del mondo Como concessa dal tecnico spagnolo.

#2295. Dundee United vs Motherwell - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 14:30

Dundee United will welcome Motherwell to Tannadice Park on Saturday for a matchday 13 showdown in the Scottish Premiership.

The hosts are sixth with 16 points from 11 games, while the visitors are ninth with 12 points from 12 league fixtures.

Dundee United had a slow start to the 2026-27 Scottish Premiership season, failing to win any of their opening three games and recording just one victory in their first six, alongside three defeats and two draws.

However, a 3-0 victory over St Mirren on matchday seven sparked a significant improvement in form, with the Tangerines subsequently recording two wins and two draws in their next four league matches, including drawing 2-2 with reigning champions Celtic last time out.

Dundee United now head into Saturday's fixture unbeaten in their last six fixtures, with three wins and three draws, and they will be eager to extend that record when they take on the struggling Motherwell.

The Tangerines have been particularly impressive in forward areas during their ongoing unbeaten run, scoring 11 of their 16 league goals this term across those six matches, while they have conceded only six goals in that time.

Dundee United will also draw confidence from their record at Tannadice Park this season, having won two, drawn two and lost only one of their five league fixtures at home this season, while they are also unbeaten in their last two home clashes with Motherwell.

Another victory for the Tangerines on Saturday could lift them fourth in the standings and only a point behind third-placed Hearts, while they also have a game in hand on several teams below them after last weekend's match was postponed due to Hearts' involvement in the Scottish League Cup.

Motherwell, meanwhile, have had three days fewer rest than their hosts after taking on St Mirren at the weekend, although they will be rejuvenated after securing a much-needed 1-0 win in that clash, marking the end of a disastrous seven-game winless run.

The Steelmen, who actually started the Scottish Premiership season with two wins and two draws from their first four, endured a catastrophic six-game losing run from early September to early October, which was eventually ended with a 2-2 draw against St Johnstone.

Following their much-needed 1-0 triumph at the weekend, Motherwell are now unbeaten in their last two and have climbed to ninth in the table, although they still hold just a three-point lead over 11th-placed Kilmarnock.

Alfred Johansson, who faced a lot of pressure and scrutiny during their miserable winless run, will be eager for his side to record another positive result on Saturday, further easing the pressure on his role as well as extending their lead over the drop zone.

Motherwell must improve defensively if they are to secure a result against Dundee United, as only Kilmarnock (28) have conceded more than their 20 goals conceded this season.

The Steelmen will also be hoping to improve upon their underwhelming record on the road, having won one, drawn two and lost three of their six away fixtures in the Scottish Premiership this season, scoring 10 and conceding 12 goals in that time.

Motherwell will draw confidence from their recent record against Dundee United, as despite failing to win either of their last two visits to Tannadice Park, the Steelmen are unbeaten in their last four meetings with the Tangerines, including a 3-0 win at the beginning of this season.

Dundee United will be unable to call upon Will Ferry, Emmanuel Agyei and Mehdi Merghem due to injury issues, while Neil Farrugia is also unavailable after suffering a muscle injury in the draw with Celtic.

Farrugia could be replaced by Daniel Bennie in attack, while the remainder of the team that started the draw against the reigning champions on October 28 are likely to feature in this one.

Motherwell are contending with ongoing injury issues to Dylan Williams and Paul McGinn, while the remainder of the squad is available for selection.

Following the 1-0 win against St Mirren in their last match, Johansson could opt to name an unchanged team on Saturday, including goalscorer Lukas Fadinger alongside Joe Hodge in the middle of the park.

Dundee United are enjoying a lengthy unbeaten run but Motherwell will be full of confidence following their streak-snapping win. Alongside the Tangerines' home strength and the Steelmen's recent upper hand in this fixture, we are backing this match to be hard-fought encounter that ends all square.

#2292. From Leao to Quenda: Five players who could inherit Cristiano Ronaldo's No.7 shirt for Portugal

By Lewis Blain | 05 Nov 2026 14:57

A few months ago, the thought seemed impossible but now, it is the reality.

For the first time in more than two decades, Portugal are preparing for an international future without the legendary Cristiano Ronaldo.

The curtain has finally come down on one of football's greatest international careers, with the five-time Ballon d'Or winner stepping away after 234 appearances, 146 goals, and a trophy haul that reshaped Selecao's place in the game.

From Euro 2016 glory to two Nations League triumphs, Ronaldo did more than lead a generation. He became the face of an entire footballing nation. And with his departure comes another symbolic passing of the torch.

The iconic number seven shirt, worn by Ronaldo for 23 years and recognised across every corner of the sport, is now vacant. The question facing Portugal is no longer how to replace an icon, but who is ready to inherit the most famous shirt in the country's football history.

Rafael Leao has already worn the number in Ronaldo's absence, but a new generation appears to be emerging for Portugal.

From established stars to wonderkids tipped for greatness, Sports Mole takes a look at the contenders waiting for their chance to carry one of the most prestigious international shirts into a new era.

Leao is perhaps the closest thing Portugal have produced to a superstar winger since Ronaldo.

When the 27-year-old is in full flight, his combination of physicality, explosive acceleration and close control makes him almost impossible to contain, giving the Selecao a frightening attacking outlet.

Now at Galatasaray after leaving AC Milan, Leao has already taken on the number seven shirt during Ronaldo's recent absence. He enjoyed three consecutive seasons of double-digit goal contributions in Milan, although his international return still has room for improvement, with six goals and eight assists in 51 caps for Portugal.

There is little doubt that Leao has the ability to become Portugal's leading attacking figure, but inheriting Ronaldo's shirt brings expectations that go beyond individual brilliance.

He must deliver more consistently on the international stage.

Felix has endured an inconsistent club career since his record-breaking move to Atletico Madrid in 2019, but he remains one of Portugal's most technically gifted attacking players.

The Al Nassr forward, now only a club teammate of Ronaldo, offers something different to Leao. Rather than operating as a traditional centre-forward, he is at his best drifting between the lines as a creative shadow striker, using his movement and close control to unlock stubborn defences.

His international form has also been encouraging, with 21 goal contributions in 61 appearances for Portugal. And with Ronaldo no longer occupying the central attacking role, Felix could benefit from increased responsibility in a more fluid forward line.

Neto wears number 18, which already contains a subtle nod to seven, if you're willing to do the maths. His explosive pace and direct style make him a compelling contender for the role.

Injuries have regularly interrupted his progress, yet the Chelsea attacker remains a dangerous option when fully fit. Blues boss Xabi Alonso has also used him in a wing-back role this season, although his influence in the final third has not disappeared, with four goal contributions in nine Premier League appearances.

Neto may not possess the same natural goalscoring instincts as Ronaldo, but his directness, crossing and ability to carry the ball at speed would give Portugal a different type of attacking threat.

If he can maintain his fitness and improve his finishing, he could force his way into the conversation.

Much like Ronaldo, Quenda began his senior career at Sporting CP before earning a £40m move to Chelsea, with the teenager now hoping to follow in the footsteps of his illustrious predecessor.

The winger has been described by former youth coach Fabio Roque as a player with explosive qualities, unpredictability and creativity, even drawing comparisons with Arsenal's Bukayo Saka.

Quenda's performances for Portugal's youth sides have also attracted attention, with his pace and fearless approach marking him out as one of the country's most exciting prospects.

He is still waiting for his senior international debut and has had limited opportunities since joining Chelsea. However, his pace, bravery in possession and ability to operate on either flank make him an exciting long-term candidate to inherit the famous shirt.

Mora is another teenager who could eventually become the creative heartbeat of Portugal, having already demonstrated remarkable technical maturity for someone so young.

The attacking midfielder progressed through Porto's academy and became the club's youngest professional player at 15, before making his senior debut in 2024. After helping Porto win the Portuguese league in 2026, he joined AS Roma in August in a deal worth an initial £20m, alongside a reported £100m release clause.

Mora is particularly impressive in tight spaces, using his vision and quick decision-making to find passes before defensive lines have time to react. He has already begun gaining experience at senior level but is yet to make his debut for Portugal, despite previously receiving a call-up and being part of the 2025 Nations League-winning squad.

At 19, there is no need to rush him into the senior side, but his ceiling is enormous. If he fulfils his potential, Mora could become one of the defining players of Portuguese football over the next decade.

Ronaldo's number seven will never be an easy shirt to inherit, and Portugal may not immediately settle on one permanent successor.

Leao is the most obvious present-day candidate, with Felix and Neto offering established alternatives, but Quenda and Mora could ultimately represent the country's most exciting long-term possibilities.

#2290. St Mirren vs Rangers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 15:01

St Mirren welcome high-flying Rangers to the Marbill Coaches Stadium on Saturday for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership.

The hosts are eighth in the table with 14 points from 12 games, while the visitors are second with 24 points from 11 matches.

St Mirren enjoyed a fantastic start to the Scottish Premiership season, winning three and drawing one of their opening four games, including keeping three clean sheets and scoring seven goals in that time.

However, the Saints' fortunes quickly took a turn for the worse after a 2-1 defeat to Celtic in early October, with St Mirren subsequently embarking on a five-game losing run.

St Mirren did end that run with a 2-1 win against Dundee, followed by a 1-1 draw with Hibernian, but they were handed their sixth league defeat in 12 matches against Motherwell last time out, losing 1-0 to a late Lukas Fadinger penalty.

The Saints have, as a result of their recent run, slipped to eighth in the standings with 12 points from 12 games, while only Kilmarnock (28) have conceded more than their 20 league goals this term - a far cry from the impressive start to the season that saw them keep three clean sheets in four matches.

Goals have also been hard to come by during that time, scoring only five goals in their last eight matches, although they have managed to find the net three times in their last two matches at the Marbill Coaches Stadium.

Craig McLeish's side have actually been at their best at home throughout the season, winning two, drawing two and losing two of their six home league fixtures, including remaining unbeaten in their last two home games, and they will be relying on that strength when they take on Rangers on Saturday.

The Gers head into matchday 13 as the Scottish Premiership's most in-form team, having remained undefeated in their last nine league games, recording seven victories and only two draws during that time.

Rangers, who have also booked their place in the Scottish League Cup final with wins against Celtic (3-0) and Aberdeen (1-0) during that run, have climbed to second in the league table as a result of their superb run.

Derek McInnes, who actually failed to win any of his first four matches as Rangers manager, will be looking for his team to continue that stunning run and pick up another three points on Saturday, especially as they trail leaders Celtic by just four points - while also holding a game in hand over their Old Firm rivals.

Rangers are the only team still yet to taste defeat away from home in the Scottish Premiership this season, recording three wins and three draws on the road, and they also won 1-0 in their last visit to St Mirren's home ground.

The Gers have also demonstrated their dominance in this fixture twice already this season, recording a crushing 5-1 triumph over St Mirren in the Scottish League Cup second round before winning 1-0 in the league in early September.

St Mirren are contending with at least five injury absences, including Ismael Kabia, Keanu Baccus, Malik Dijksteel, Nikolas Agrafiotis and Jonah Ayunga.

Following an underwhelming offensive performance in the loss to Motherwell last time out, McLeish could look to make changes on Saturday, meaning the likes of Chris Mochrie and Jake Young could start.

Rangers are also dealing with numerous injury issues, with Cameron Devlin, Daisuke Yokota, Jose Cifuentes, Lawrence Shankland, Ross McCrorie and Youssef Chermiti all unavailable.

Ryan Don Naderi scored the winner against Aberdeen in the Scottish League Cup semi-final on October 31 and is also Rangers' top scorer in the league with five goals, and the forward should start just behind Kevin Kelsy on Saturday.

Chapman; Fraser, Gogic, Fieldson; Ramsay, Carr, Phillips, Thomas; Young, Sule, Mochrie

Pandur; Sterling, Fernandez, Makhanya, Penrice; Raskin, Neil; Kim, Naderi, Gassama; Kelsy

St Mirren are enduring a difficult run and take on a Rangers side enjoying a fantastic run of results, and after already securing two victories in this fixture this season, the Gers will be expected to claim a third win against St Mirren this term on Saturday.

#2288. Peterborough United vs Reading - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matthew Cooper | 05 Nov 2026 15:03

Peterborough United will be looking to get back to winning ways when they welcome Reading to the Weston Homes Stadium on Saturday in the first round of the FA Cup.

The hosts currently sit 22nd in the League One table, while the visitors are sixth.

Peterborough are without a win in their last five matches across all competitions, having struggled for form after winning their first two games under new head coach Robbie Savage.

The Posh lost 2-0 at home to Luton in their most recent fixture last weekend, leaving them third from bottom and two points from safety.

Savage was critical of his side's performance against Luton, admitting that "if you can’t defend in the opening moments of halves or take your chances, you won’t win games of football".

Peterborough will be looking to put in a much-improved performance in the FA Cup this weekend and they will be buoyed by the fact they went unbeaten against Reading last season, with one win and a draw.

Reading, meanwhile, will be looking for a response after they lost 2-0 to local rivals Oxford United on Wednesday.

Head coach Leam Richardson branded the result "really disappointing" and admitted his side were "so weak" for both of Oxford's goals.

Richardson has backed the Royals to "come again" when they travel to Peterborough on Saturday and he will be keen to see some real improvements.

There is also an intriguing subplot for this match, with Reading midfielder Charlie Savage going up against a team managed by his father for the first time.

Peterborough United form (all competitions):

Peterborough could hand starts to Matthew Garbett, Brandon Khela and Sam Hughes, with Harry Ashfield, Vernon Masara and Carl Johnston dropping out of the side.

Harry Leonard is expected to continue up front, having bagged nine goals across all competitions so far this season.

Reading remain without George Earthy, Benn Ward and Paddy Lane due to injury and Jack Marriott is expected to be rested, having only just returned to action on Wednesday.

Savage could come into the side for Lewis Wing, while Jacob Brown is likely to replace Marriott.

Peterborough United possible starting lineup:

Bass; Hughes, Mendonca, O'Connor, Mills; Kelly, Garbett; Shofowoke, Williams, Khela; Leonard

Pereira; Godwin-Malife, O'Connor, Dorsett, Roberts; Savage, Rinomhota; Lisbie, Stokes, Kyerewaa; Marriott

Reading have been in better form than Peterborough and should have enough quality to progress to the next round.

#2287. Napoli, riecco lo spettro dell'ammutinamento: il ritiro spacca la rosa e Di Lorenzo va ko

Il Napoli attraversa uno dei momenti più delicati della sua stagione. Dopo le sconfitte contro Juventus e Porto, il clima a Castel Volturno si è fatto particolarmente pesante, con tensioni che coinvolgerebbero sia i giocatori sia alcuni membri dello staff. A far discutere è soprattutto la decisione di mister Massimiliano Allegri, condivisa da Aurelio De Laurentiis, di imporre un ritiro a Pozzuoli dopo la trasferta portoghese. Una scelta accolta malissimo dalla squadra, che avrebbe preferito trascorrere qualche giorno con le rispettive famiglie. #2286. How long West Brom face without George Campbell as James Morrison confirms injury extent

West Brom host Millwall in the Championship this Saturday, and they will hope to extend their league unbeaten run to six games. However, WBA will have to do so without club captain George Campbell, who withdrew from the Southampton draw after feeling his groin.

Now, ahead of the Millwall tie, Baggies boss James Morrison has confirmed Campbell will be absent against the Lions. Speaking to the club’s official media, he said: “Unfortunately George picked up a groin injury against Southampton and we expect him to be out for between ten to 12 days. It’s not ideal, but it could’ve been worse. Hopefully it’ll just be one game that he misses.”

Campbell has played 15 times in all competitions this season, sitting unused on the bench on only one occasion. As captain, he serves as a key presence at centre-back, so while it is a blow to lose him, there will be relief that he isn’t out for an extended spell.

There’s no hiding that Campbell is a loss for Albion. He was instrumental in their survival turnaround last season and emerged as a real leader, earning the captain’s armband and retaining a key role for the 2026/27 campaign.

Thankfully for West Brom and Morrison, he has a straightforward selection decision when dealing with Campbell’s absence. Nat Phillips replaced him at the break on Tuesday night, and he will more than likely do so from the start against Millwall.

In the two Championship games Campbell has not started, it has been fellow centre-back Chris Mepham who has stepped in as captain. As such, he seems the likely candidate to do so against the Lions, although Jayson Molumby has donned the armband before as well.

Morrison will be hoping his remaining WBA centre-backs can avoid suspension or injury, as only Mepham and Phillips remain with Campbell sidelined.

Campbell was called up for the United States national team for their November fixtures. But, with the extent of his injury now confirmed, it will be expected that he withdraws.

That will give the West Brom man the break to recover from his groin issue and work back to fitness for the return to club football. WBA face Swansea City in the first game back, and the hope is that Campbell will be fit.

Morrison and the physios will be sure to be careful with Campbell to ensure he doesn’t suffer a further setback. Losing him for one game is a blow, but seeing him cast to the sidelines for longer would spark real concern given the Baggies’ lack of centre-back depth.

#2285. FC Thun 3 Hearts 1: Another bruising Conference League defeat as injury woes worsen

Depleted Hearts’ Conference League bruising was almost totally self-inflicted in a 3-1 defeat by Thun in Switzerland.

Belgian boss Wouter Vrancken was without 10 players through various ailments and the Scottish outfit were dealt another blow in the 22nd minute when defender Jamie McCart put through his own goal in calamitous fashion.

Thun doubled their lead in the 33rd minute through striker Brighton Labeau after more poor defending, this time from skipper Stephen Kingsley – who went off later with an injury – and then Furkan Dursun grabbed a third in the 56th minute before Jambos defender Stuart Findlay reduced the deficit in the 82nd minute with a header.

Hearts were left with one Conference League point out of nine following a 1-1 home draw against Nordsjaelland and a 2-0 defeat away to Trabzonspor.

Thick smoke from a pyro halted the game soon after kick-off but when play resumed after a lengthy delay – there were nine minutes added on at the end of the first half – Hearts winger Josh McPake drew a save from Niklas Steffen with an angled drive that lacked real power.

In the 20th minute McPake’s effort from 25 yards after he had drifted in from the left had the power but was beaten away by the Thun keeper.

There appeared to be nothing untoward on the horizon but then came a Hearts horror show.

There was no real pressure on the Jambos goal when a long ball came into the box but with Lucien Dahler at his shoulder, a simple pass back from McCart somehow evaded Hearts keeper Beau Reus and slipped into the corner of the net.

Thun’s second goal was also gift-wrapped.

The home side broke clear and with pace following a Heart set-piece and when Nils Reichmuth crossed into the box from the right, Kingsley stuck out a leg to clear but the ball went straight to Labeau who swept it into the net from 10 yards out.

Dursun was shown a yellow card for a forearm challenge into the face of Findlay, and it perhaps deserved more punishment.

The interval gave the Tynecastle side time to reset but three minutes after the break, Reus made a diving save from Dursun and an even better one from Reichmuth moments later.

However, the keeper was beaten once again when Dursun got his toe to a Reichmuth cross.

On the hour mark, Nico Maier raced clear of the Hearts defence but although his chip beat Reus, it sped wide of the target.

The night got worse for the rattled visitors as injured Kingsley was replaced by 19-year-old Matthew Gillies, called back from a loan spell at Stenhousemuir.

Malachi Fagan-Walcott’s header from a Jordi Altena free-kick was brilliantly saved by Steffen.

In the 78th minute Jan Bamert had the ball in the net after Steffen tipped a free-kick from substitute Nassim Zoukit on to the bar but was ruled offside, before Findlay headed in a consolation from another Altena free-kick.

#2284. Torino vs Lecce - prediction, team news, lineups

By Jonathan O'Shea | 05 Nov 2026 15:12, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 15:14

Both beaten again last week, Torino and Lecce will look to end their losing streaks when they meet each other on Saturday.

While Toro have just suffered back-to-back defeats, their visitors are also sliding down the Serie A standings.

After enduring a slow start to the season, Torino then posted their best run of league wins since 2019, but they have been unable to make such form last.

Having previously taken maximum points from three consecutive games, Toro then lost to two of Serie A's in-form clubs - Como and Frosinone.

First, Ignazio Abate's side conceded three late goals at home to Como, before Frosinone struck twice in the second half last weekend.

Only free-falling Udinese have shipped as many goals as Torino's nine during the final half-hour, which will cause Abate significant concern.

Furthermore, before returning to Turin, their last league clean sheet at Stadio Olimpico Grande dates all the way back to early March.

That said, the Granata have kept clean sheets on all of Lecce's last four visits, including a 1-0 win earlier this year.

Either home or away, Lecce have lost five of the last eight Serie A meetings, failing to even score in six.

Finding the net has been a familiar problem for several seasons, but the Salentini kicked off the current campaign relatively well, winning two of their first four league fixtures.

However, they have since lost four of the next five, including a 5-1 thrashing by Frosinone and last week's 2-1 home defeat to Genoa.

Perhaps not now, but in August that pair would have been considered Lecce's direct relegation rivals, making those losses sting even more.

In his second year at the southern club, head coach Eusebio Di Francesco surely faces another survival fight, as his team have now slipped down to 14th place.

Previously poor travellers, Lecce have actually won half of their last eight away games, so at least they can head north with some hope.

Torino will again be without Alessio Cacciamani, who recently dislocated his shoulder, but fellow wing-backs Niccolo Fortini and Ricardo Rodriguez might be involved this weekend.

Another wide man, Rafik Belghali should recover from a bruised knee, but midfield fulcrum Rolando Mandragora may be given the international break to fully overcome his thigh problem.

The matchwinner in this fixture last season, Che Adams is set to partner Giovanni Simeone up front; since his arrival in 2024, the Scotland striker is Toro's top Serie A scorer with 19 goals.

Highlighting their dearth of potent forwards, Lecce's last eight league goals have been scored by as many different players - including midfielder Youssef Maleh last week.

Willem Geubbels and Lassana Coulibaly will both be assessed before Saturday's game, but Joel Monteiro injured his left calf against Genoa; Medon Berisha, Santiago Pierotti and Ivan Ilic are still sidelined.

Bolstering his options at the back, Di Francesco can recall Kialonda Gaspar, who returns after serving a two-match suspension.

Perri; Coco, Ismajli, Comuzzo; Belghali, Gineitis, Braganca, Patterson; Vlasic; Adams, Simeone

Falcone; Veiga, Siebert, Gabriel, Gallo; Kaba, Ngom, Maleh; N'Dri, Stulic, Fatah

Although Torino have been leaking too many goals, they possess more attacking thrust than their limited visitors.

Since April, Lecce have gone 14 league games without a draw, and that streak will continue with their fourth straight defeat.

#2282. Blackburn Rovers vs West Ham United - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 05 Nov 2026 15:27, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 15:28

West Ham United make the trip to Blackburn Rovers on Saturday afternoon looking to build on Wednesday's remarkable comeback against Cardiff City in Scott Parker's first match in charge.

At a time when the Hammers sit in eighth position in the Championship table, Blackburn are in 14th spot after consecutive defeats.

When West Ham were 3-0 down at Cardiff City on Wednesday night, Parker had the defence that he had only been in charge for less than a day.

Fast-forward to full time and the former Hammers midfielder had delivered a team talk that inspired his new set of players to a 3-3 draw against opponents who have not lost at the Cardiff City Stadium in seven Championship games.

Parker will have concerns over West Ham conceding three goals for the third match in succession, yet the potential of this team remains apparent when they have scored at least six more goals than every other club in the division.

With Middlesbrough losing to Wrexham, West Ham actually made ground on top spot, reducing the deficit to five points when not performing to their true ability.

Furthermore, it has gone under the radar that West Ham are yet to lose an away match this season, recording three wins and four draws from seven fixtures.

As far as Blackburn are concerned, they would have taken 14th place at this stage of the season had it been offered to them at the start of August.

However, Tony Mowbray's side have lost three of their last four Championship contests, albeit two of them coming at West Bromwich Albion and Swansea City.

Blackburn also went down 2-1 at home to fifth-placed Stoke City on Tuesday night, ending a run of three successive wins at Ewood Park.

Although Blackburn sit five points adrift of the playoffs, West Ham are in the final spot, so this contest has the potential to make-or-break their ambitions in the short term.

With Ryan Alebiosu an injury doubt, Gady Beyuku could be handed a start at right-back for Blackburn.

Moussa Baradji's goal as a substitute versus West Ham may also see him recalled in placed of Kristi Montgomery.

Unless Yuki Ohashi is brought back into the team, the rest of the side could stay the same.

Ezra Mayers, Mohamadou Kante and Joel Piroe are all in contention for West Ham, a consequence of their half-time introductions versus Cardiff helping spark the turnaround.

While the return of Tomas Soucek to the starting lineup is also a possibility, Parker may opt against further changes.

Blackburn Rovers possible starting lineup:

Toth; Beyuku, Carter McLoughlin, Pickering; Morsy, Baradji; Fevrier, Cantwell, Afolayan; Gudjohnsen

West Ham United possible starting lineup:

Hermansen; Veltman, Mavropanos, Kilman, Mayers; Bowen, Engels, Kante, Solomon; Mukasa; Piroe

While there is a vulnerability about West Ham that Blackburn will hope to exploit, the Hammers will be full of belief in the final third after Wednesday night. With that in mind, we expect their superior attacking quality to come to the fore to deliver a much-needed three points.

#2281. Il Venezia batte l'Udinese e la lascia all'ultimo posto: 2-1 al Penzo, perla di Kike Perez

L'effetto del cambio di guida tecnica non scuote l'Udinese: la prima di Paolo Zanetti sulla nuova panchina coincide con un altro ko, che spedisce i friulani all'ultimo posto, superati proprio dal Venezia, che passa 2-1 al Penzo. Una gara combattuta e tesa, la decide una meraviglia di Kike Perez all'incrocio dei pali. Davide Nicola centra la seconda vittoria in 5 partite da quando è subentrato a Stroppa.

Nicola rilancia Yeboah al fianco di Adams, in mezzo resta in panchina Busio, spazio a Kike Perez e Basic con Sohm. Zanetti schiera dal primo minuto Solet in difesa e Davis in attacco, entrambi in dubbio alla vigilia.

Chance casuale, ma colossale per Hainaut dopo 6 minuti: un rimpallo lo manda in porta, Okoye para. Juan Jesus è pericoloso di testa, poi Mazzocchi va vicino al palo dalla distanza e Adams viene murato da Davis. L'Udinese si vede dopo la mezz'ora: botta di Ekkelenkamp, gran riflesso di Stankovic. Sarebbe clamoroso il gol sbagliato da Davis da due metri, ma viene segnalato un fuorigioco dubbio. Nel finale, su una smanacciata di Okoye, Hainaut calcia malissimo al volo: aveva la porta spalancata.

Si riparte senza cambi, ma Udinese accelera. Solet di testa non c'entra i pali, Stankovic in uscita si salva su Davis con la punta del piede e poi mura anche Ekkelenkamp pochi minuti dopo. Nel momento più difficile, Hainaut pesca Basic che fa 1-0 con il piatto sinistro. L'Udinese pareggia subito: cross di Vojvoda, Moreno fa autogol nel cercare di anticipare Davis. Ancora Ekkelenkamp pericoloso a giro, sfiora solo il palo. Kike Perez pesca l'incrocio: gran gol per il 2-1al 65'. Corner di Kike Perez, Moreno di testa va vicino a colpire, libera Vojvoda. L'assalto finale non porta frutti, il Venezia molla l'ultimo posto.

Così l'allenatore del Venezia, Davide Nicola: "Se stiamo creando un fortino al Penzo? Magari! I tifosi lo meriterebbero per la mano che ci danno, sarebbe bello trasformare questo stadio in un fortino. Dipenderà dall'interpretazione che riusciremo a dare alle partite. Ma non basta essere efficaci in casa, serve equilibrio fra ciò che si ottiene in casa e ciò che si ottiene fuori. Ora facciamo un passo alla volta, i ragazzi hanno lavorato in maniera pazzesca. In questa sosta possiamo recuperare energie, mi rendo conto che lavorare con me può essere anche pesante e i prossimi tre giorni di stacco possono servire".

Le parole di Paolo Zanetti in conferenza: "Non posso dire che la squadra abbia sbagliato atteggiamento, questo assolutamente no, nel ritmo in alcuni momenti siamo andati in difficoltà e non riuscivamo a star dentro al ritmo gara, arrivavamo in ritardo nelle pressioni, nei gol le corse indietro non sono state fatte ai 100 all'ora. Dobbiamo alzare l'intensità e portarla ad un livello molo più alto di questo. Abbiamo delle qualità, l'Udinese ha caratteristiche fisiche di un certo tipo, ma dovrebbe essere molto brillante, molto fisica e intensa. In questo momento devo essere onesto: non lo vedo, dobbiamo lavorare. Sia dal punto di vista fisico che psicologico, perché in alcuni momenti questa squadra ha paura. Fa anche errori tecnici concedendo ripartenze con passaggi banali difficili da accettare. Questo fa capire che la squadra sta soffrendo, è umano e capibile, siamo ultimi e peggio di così non si può fare e spero che questo faccia avere a tutti un bel bagno d'umiltà, sempre se ce ne fosse bisogno, non tanto per l'atteggiamento ma per capire che c'è da lavorare duro per fare molto di più. Davis oggi ha fatto una buona partita, ha avuto due-tre occasioni e se avesse segnato magari sarebbe cambiato qualcosa, poi abbiamo preso gol all'incrocio. Grande calma, abbiamo un ambiente che ci sostiene, ma dobbiamo fare di più".

#2280. Birmingham City team news and predicted XI to face Swansea City: August Priske remains a doubt

Birmingham City welcome Swansea City to St Andrew’s this weekend looking to strengthen their bid for automatic promotion. The Blues currently sit 3rd in the Championship table with 26 points from 14 games, level with Stoke City and just two points behind leaders Middlesbrough.

Chris Davies’ side head into the contest in excellent form after a convincing 3-0 win over Millwall last time out. Alex Cochrane, Max Bird and Jay Stansfield all got on the scoresheet in the first half, while summer arrival Liam Millar has also made an impact with four goals to his name.

Swansea City are also firmly in the promotion hunt, occupying 5th place with 26 points from their opening 14 fixtures. Vitor Matos has enjoyed an encouraging start to his tenure in South Wales, with the Swans demonstrating plenty of quality throughout the campaign.

However, the visitors will be keen to bounce back after suffering a damaging 3-1 defeat away at Portsmouth. Swansea looked to have rescued a point after fighting back from a goal down, but two goals in stoppage time, arriving in the 92nd and 98th minutes, condemned them to defeat.

August Priske is a major doubt for Birmingham City’s Championship clash with Swansea City this weekend, with the striker set to be assessed on Friday before a decision is made on his availability, as reported by Birmingham Live.

The Dane missed Tuesday night’s 3-0 victory over Millwall after picking up an injury during the previous weekend’s meeting with Lincoln City. Priske has scored seven goals this season and would be a significant absence for Davies’ side.

The Blues boss provided a positive update but admitted it remains too early to know whether his leading scorer will feature. He said: “He’s doing OK. He’s made progress. It was a heavy knock that he took and he’s made progress but it’s probably too early to say.”

Davies added that it would be a close call, with Birmingham hoping to get Priske involved in some capacity. Paik Seung-ho and Dael Fry could return to the squad, while goalkeeper James Beadle is expected to miss out again.

Allsop; Laird, Neumann, Klarer, Cochrane; Solis, Iwata; Roberts, Bird, Millar; Stansfield

After the performance against Millwall in midweek will likely see Davies keep the same team. The only change may depend on Priske’s fitness.

Should Priske be fit then Bird will likely drop out of the side with Stansfield sitting deeper. However, both Bird and Stansfield impressed in midweek so it would be harsh to drop them.

Ryan Allsop will keep his place between the sticks with Beadle remaining out. While Fry and Paik will start from the bench should they return to the squad.

#2279. The turning point that inspired Celtic to big European victory against Besiktas

On Guy Fawkes night, Celtic finally provided some European fireworks. Martin O'Neill's side got their Europa League campaign up and running with an enthralling 3-2 win over Besiktas.

Junior Olaitain put the visitors ahead in the 54th minute, scoring from the rebound of his own penalty kick that was initially saved by Sam Johnstone.

Celtic Park was engulfed by an all too familiar feeling before O'Neill introduced Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran in the 60th minute.

Osmand scored the equaliser seconds after coming on, with Duran latching on to the youngster's through ball to round Alex Nubel and put Celtic ahead with 12 minutes remaining.

Orkun Kokcu's pile-driver levelled things up for less than 120 seconds as Duran struck again in the 88th minute to give Celtic a priceless victory.

Here are the key talking points from Parkhead...

Celtic's Callum Osmand scored against Besiktas. (Image: Andrew Milligan/PA)

O’Neill made five changes from Sunday’s defeat at Falkirk, most notably drafting Jordan Lotomba in for his first start at right-back.

The Switzerland international had played just 31 minutes in green-and-white hoops before Besiktas, making his inclusion all the more surprising.

Lotomba was tasked with keeping Besiktas' left side quiet though former Arsenal attacker Leandro Trossard and Ernest Poku kept him well occupied.

The 28-year-old sought to maraud forward when he could, but once tripped up on his own feet when attempting a series of step-overs in the opposition third.

The concession of a second-half penalty compounded Lotomba's tough evening. He was withdrawn in the 60th minute for Colby Donovan.

Elsewhere, Kasper Hogh, James Forrest, and Sebastian Tounekti made up a second string frontline.

Duran, Celtic’s top scorer this season, dropped to the bench along with Haissem Hassan. But it wasn’t a completely rotated XI.

Regulars Kieran Tierney, Cameron Carter-Vickers, Liam Scales, and captain Callum McGregor all began, with Benjamin Nygren and Mika Baur also in from the off.

That said, it was the first time O’Neill hadn’t gone full strength in Europe this season, perhaps indicating his feelings at Celtic’s current plight.

There are dull halves of football, and then there are dull halves of football. Besiktas enjoyed the majority of possession (61 per cent) in the opening 45 minutes without mustering many opportunities.

Celtic's sound structure limited the visitors' attacking play. Carter-Vickers defended ably but O'Neill's side struggled to get forward themselves.

By the break, Celtic had just one shot on goal, two touches in the opposition box, and 73 per cent pass accuracy, compared to around 88 per cent in their first three Europa League defeats.

Yet again, their inability to progress play through the lines was apparent. Celtic resorted to going long on a number of occasions. Hogh failed to make it stick and Tounekti struggled greatly.

Besiktas were quite content to pop the ball around, without taking any major risks. You still felt the Turks had a few more gears to move through.

Celtic shouldn't have needed a second-half wake-up call but just seconds after the interval, Trossard fired just shy of the left post.

Moments later, Johnstone's clearance ricocheted off the Belgian attacker and into Lotomba's right-back area. The Celtic defender slid needlessly in on Poku and referee Sascha Stegemann pointed to the spot without hesitation.

Olaitan stepped up and Johnstone made a heroic save that flew into the smoke-filled Glasgow sky, only for the initial taker to score on the rebound.

Just like Johnstone, Lotomba almost redeemed himself in the following seconds, driving forward from his defensive position before tumbling over the ball when attempting to shoot.

But Besiktas weren't ahead for long. O'Neill looked to his bench for fresh impetus on the hour mark and within just a minute of coming on, Osmand scored.

McGregor latched brilliantly on to a loose midfield pass and cut back for the substitute, who scored with practically his first touch.

Celtic Park had all of a sudden rediscovered its European voice.

For the goal, McGregor looked like his old self, driving forward from the engine room to kickstart a promising attack and Osmand provided the killer touch with his first European goal.

If O'Neill's plan was to wait until the game opened up in the second half and then call on the cavalry, it worked a treat.

Just 16 minutes after Osmand drew Celtic level with a fine strike of his own, he turned provider, playing a slide-rule pass in behind Besiktas' defence for Duran.

The Colombian got there ahead of Nubel and slotted beyond the goal line to turn the game on its head.

Duran didn't have to wait long to make his presence felt again, once more beating Nubel, this time from close range, after brilliantly controlling Tierney's mishit shot. It came seconds after Kokcu's leveller.

A true difference-maker, Duran is now up to 13 goals for the season. His impact could not have been greater here.

Well, who saw that coming? After four matchdays, Celtic are finally up and running in the Europa League. It really was now or never for O'Neill's charges, who have given their chances of progression to the knockout stages fresh hope with an unlikely victory.

Beyond their run of six defeats in ten leading into Thursday night, what made the win even more unexpected was the fact Besiktas are the best side they've played at home in the league phase so far.

Celtic struggled greatly against both Ferencvaros and Celta Vigo on their own turf, flailing whenever faced with adversity. They went down meekly in Lisbon too, losing 4-1 to Benfica last month.

No one should get carried away based on one result. Celtic still need somewhere in the region of six to eight points to finish in the top 24 and three out of their four remaining fixtures are away.

The next comes in Leiria against Portuguese second tier side Torreense after the international break. They could realistically gain three more points there before a trip to face Omonia Nicosia in December. Ties against Marseille and Union St-Gilloise lie ahead in January.

O'Neill will take great satisfaction from a European result that's long evaded his team. Time will define its influence on Celtic's wider Europa League picture.

#2278. Espanyol vs Deportivo La Coruna - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 05 Nov 2026 15:31, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 15:33

Deportivo La Coruna will be aiming to make it three straight wins in all competitions when they continue their La Liga campaign against Espanyol on Saturday afternoon.

The visitors will enter the match off the back of a 2-1 victory over Osasuna, while Espanyol recorded an impressive 1-0 win over Villarreal last time out.

Espanyol boast a record of three wins, two draws and six defeats from their 11 league matches this season, with 11 points leaving them in 14th spot in the La Liga table.

The White and Blues had been on a three-game losing run ahead of their clash with Villarreal last time out, including a shock 4-3 defeat to fourth-tier outfit Sestao in the Copa del Rey.

A second-half goal from Leandro Cabrera secured a valuable three points away to the Yellow Submarine, and it proved to be the perfect introduction for new head coach Eder Sarabia, although first-team coach Sergio Garcia took the team for that particular contest.

Espanyol just about lead the overall head-to-head record between these two sides, posting 39 wins to Deportivo's 35, but their last meeting ended in a goalless draw in February 2018.

The Catalan outfit are actually unbeaten against Deportivo since September 2015, winning three and drawing two of the last five meetings between the two sides.

Deportivo have not been victorious away to Espanyol since June 2007, meanwhile, which is an indication of the size of their task in this contest.

The Blue and Whites, who finished second in last season's Segunda Division to secure a return to the top flight, are currently eighth in the La Liga table, boasting a record of three wins, five draws and three defeats to collect 14 points.

Antonio Hidalgo's side recorded a 3-0 victory over Auriense in the Copa del Rey at the end of October before opening November with a 2-1 win over Osasuna in La Liga.

Deportivo will now take on Espanyol and Alaves either side of the November international break, before finishing the month with the small matter of a home fixture against Barcelona.

Deportivo La Coruna form (all competitions):

Espanyol are once again expected to be missing Jofre Carreras and Kike Garcia through injury this weekend, but the hosts are otherwise in strong shape.

This will be Sarabia's first match in charge, and it could be a case of same again in terms of the team given the nature of the performance and result against Villarreal.

Roberto Fernandez has scored seven times in 12 appearances this season, and the 24-year-old is set to continue in the final third of the field.

As for Deportivo, Bright Ede, Zakaria Eddahchouri and Marc Casado remain unavailable for selection, but Pierre-Emerick Aubameyang is now back in the fold, coming off the bench in the clash with Osasuna after six games out injured.

Aubameyang has scored five goals in eight league appearances this season but is again expected to feature as a substitute rather than being introduced into the starting XI given that Bil Nsongo has two goals in his last three appearances.

Adama Traore is again expected to feature off the bench for the visitors this weekend after impressing in the second half of the Osasuna fixture.

Fortuno; El Hilali, Drkusic, Cabrera, Hinojo; Exposito, Bauza; Santiago, Hernandez, Zaragoza; Fernandez

Deportivo La Coruna possible starting lineup:

Roman; Altimira, Loureiro, Gimenez, Quagliata; Soriano, Amatucci; Mella, Lusimi, Hernandez; Nsongo

Both teams will enter this match off the back of impressive league wins, and we are finding it difficult to separate them here, with an entertaining game potentially finishing all square.

#2275. Wouter Vrancken demands unity as Hearts seek to bounce back from Thun defeat

Wouter Vrancken insists Hearts will have to stick together following their self-inflicted 3-1 Conference League defeat by Thun in Switzerland.

The Scottish outfit, without 10 players through various injuries, fell behind in the 22nd minute when defender Jamie McCart knocked the ball into his own net.

Thun doubled their lead in the 33rd minute through striker Brighton Labeau after a poor attempted clearance from captain Stephen Kingsley – who went off later with an injury – and then Furkan Dursun grabbed a third in the 56th minute.

Jambos defender Stuart Findlay then gave some unrealised hope of a comeback in the 82nd minute when he headed in from close range.

After three Conference League fixtures Hearts have one point out of nine and have won just two of their last 10 fixtures in all competitions.

Vrancken told TNT Sports: “Like I said already to the guys, we have to stick together now.

“We know which situation we’re in with all the circumstances.

“And there’s only one thing that you have to do now and that is stick together and keep working hard.

“In general, it was not good enough in quality and can you blame them?

“Yeah, on one hand you can because you want to always expect the best and want the best.

“On the other hand, I’m still proud of what they did already in the run-up games.”

Kingsley was replaced in the second half by 19-year-old Matthew Gillies, called back from a loan spell at Stenhousemuir amid the club’s injury crisis, and Vrancken admitted there was an element of risk playing him on the artificial surface which the Jambos boss had criticised.

He said: “We talked it through before the game of course, a few days before the game.

“And yeah, it’s always a risk, especially when it’s another surface, and especially when it’s bad like that, but he was confident in playing and that’s where we are.”

Hearts’ next Conference League game is against Ligue 1 side in Monaco at Tynecastle on November 26 but Vrancken is more concerned about the home William Hill Premiership game against Falkirk on Sunday.

He said: “Monaco’s a really strong side, maybe the strongest side we will face in Europe.

“So we will see when we get there, because I think there are a lot of important games before that, especially on Sunday.”

#2274. Watford vs Middlesbrough - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 05 Nov 2026 15:40

Leaders Middlesbrough make the trip to Watford on Saturday afternoon looking to respond to suffering just their second defeat of the season.

At a time when the visitors sit in first place in the Championship table, the Hornets are down in 20th position.

Having not lost in the Championship since August 22, Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg would not have envisaged that the absence of leading marksman Will Lankshear would significantly impact his team against Wrexham.

However, the Championship frontrunners produced one of their worst performances of the season to lose 2-0 at the Racecourse Ground.

Although supporters have been reassured that the issue with the England Under-21 international is not serious, Hellberg will understand that his team need to deliver a positive display if the former Tottenham Hotspur youngster misses out again.

Despite sitting in top spot courtesy of 13 points from five matches before the trip to Wrexham, there are just three points separating first and sixth position.

Meanwhile, Boro only possess the seventh-best away record in the division, as well as keeping just one clean sheet in seven such encounters.

Watford boss Alessio Dionisi remains under pressure to keep his role at Vicarage Road with the club having recently held a record of six defeats in 10 matches.

Nevertheless, two victories have been posted in their last four outings, with their other two defeats coming against Wolverhampton Wanderers and Norwich City by one-goal margins.

Their victories have been against Charlton Athletic and Lincoln City respectively, both with clean sheets at Vicarage Road.

Thirteen of Watford's 14 points in this season's Championship have come at home, despite scoring just nine goals in seven matches on familiar territory.

With Hector Kyprianou serving a one-match ban for five yellow cards, Martin Payero could be recalled into Watford's midfield.

Mamadou Doumbia is an alternative to Myron Boadu in attack, but Dionisi will be against making many changes to his starting lineup.

The likes of Jeremy Ngakia, Edoardo Bove and Michail Antonio are among those expected to miss out through injury.

If Lankshear is unable to return from a minor muscle injury, David Strelec will likely replace Kyle Joseph down the centre of the attack.

Riley McGree and Myles Peart-Harris will come under consideration for a return in central midfield or as one of the number 10s.

Alfie Jones and Rokas Pukstas are still unavailable through injury.

Ravaglia; Traore, Keben, Pollock, Bola; Kayembe, Payero; Maamma, Bravo, Mavididi; Boadu

Vitek; Phillips, Malanda, Ayling; Brittain, Berhalter, Morris, Borges; Cozier-Duberry, McGree; Strelec

A positive spin can be put on Watford's recent four matches, while they are a far better outfit at Vicarage Road than on their travels. Nevertheless, we can only back Boro to come through this contest, even if it may initially prove to be a struggle.

#2273. Sheffield United team news and predicted XI to face Charlton Athletic: Joe Rothwell remains out as key man returns

Sheffield United head into Saturday’s clash with Charlton Athletic after an impressive victory over Preston North End at Deepdale. Sydie Peck stole the headlines with a first-half hat-trick as SUFC secured a 3-2 win on Tuesday night.

Making their second successive away trip, Chris Wilder’s side will be keen to build on that result and maintain their recent momentum. The Blades have won three of their last five matches and are averaging 1.57 points per game this season.

Their opponents, Charlton Athletic, go into the fixture following a disappointing 1-0 defeat against Bolton Wanderers. CAFC are now winless in their last 11 matches, collecting just five points since September.

However, returning to The Valley could offer some encouragement for Nathan Jones’ side. The Addicks are averaging 1.29 points per game on home soil this season and have conceded fewer than a goal per match in front of their own supporters.

Sheffield United will welcome back Kalvin Phillips for Saturday’s trip to Charlton Athletic, but fellow midfielder Joe Rothwell is set for a spell on the sidelines after undergoing a minor groin operation, as reported by The Sheffield Star.

Rothwell picked up the problem during the warm-up before last Friday’s win over Wrexham and is expected to miss the upcoming fixtures against Watford and Southampton. Wilder confirmed he should return after the international break.

Phillips, meanwhile, missed the victory at Preston North End with an ankle injury but has recovered and is available for selection. The Manchester City loanee was also named Sheffield United’s player of the month for October after helping the Blades build momentum.

Patrick Bamford remains a doubt, with the striker facing a late fitness test on the shoulder injury he sustained against Derby County. Wilder confirmed the forward has returned to training but must prove he can cope with physical contact before being cleared to play.

Given his injury record, Wilder will not want to take any risk with Phillips. Peck and Jairo Riedewald both remain in midfield following the midweek win.

The two changes come in attack and at the centre of defence with Tyrese Campbell and Liam Kitching return to the side. Campbell comes in for Tom Cannon alongside Ben Brereton Diaz, while Kitching is in for Mark McGuinness after he served his suspension in midweek.

#2272. Mourinho sends Endrick message amid Arsenal, Villa links

By Naim Beneddra | 05 Nov 2026 15:41

Real Madrid manager Jose Mourinho has admitted his frustration at being unable to give more playing time to some of his talented attackers, while offering fresh insight into Endrick's role amid continued speculation linking the Brazilian with Arsenal and Aston Villa.

The 20-year-old forward has struggled to establish himself in Mourinho's plans this season, despite returning to the Santiago Bernabeu following an impressive loan spell at Lyon.

Endrick was introduced during Wednesday's dramatic 3-2 Champions League victory over AEK Athens, featuring on the right flank as Madrid recovered from a 2-1 deficit to maintain their perfect record in the competition.

After the match, Mourinho acknowledged the difficulty of managing a squad containing so many high-quality players, while highlighting the particular threat Endrick offers against tired defenders.

His comments come at a time when the youngster's limited opportunities have once again raised questions about whether a January departure could be beneficial to his development.

Speaking after Federico Valverde's stoppage-time winner in Athens, Mourinho conceded that he found it difficult to leave talented players out of his team.

"It frustrates me for all those who are not playing because everyone deserves to play," the Portuguese coach explained, according to Real Madrid's official website.

Mourinho pointed to the performances of those who have received fewer opportunities as evidence of the considerable depth available to him.

Endrick was among the players singled out for praise, with the former Chelsea and Manchester United manager emphasising the problems his pace and directness can cause opponents.

"I think it's dramatic for a full-back, after playing an hour against someone of quality, to then face Endrick; it's truly complicated," Mourinho added.

The Brazilian replaced Bernardo Silva during the second half against AEK and provided an attacking outlet as Madrid searched for a way back into the contest.

Operating primarily from the right, Endrick created a promising opportunity for Kylian Mbappe late in the match, although the Frenchman's effort was saved.

He also saw a shot blocked during the closing stages, before Valverde secured victory with a powerful strike from outside the penalty area.

Despite those encouraging contributions, Endrick's overall involvement this season remains limited.

The former Palmeiras forward has managed only a handful of appearances and one goal since returning from France, with injuries initially delaying his return to Mourinho's matchday squad.

Competition for attacking positions has subsequently made regular minutes difficult to obtain.

Mbappe remains Madrid's principal centre-forward, while Vinicius Junior, Yan Diomande, Brahim Diaz and Carlos Espi provide further options in the attacking positions.

Mourinho nevertheless insisted that having so many alternatives was a considerable advantage rather than a problem for the team.

"There are many coaches who must look at me with a lot of healthy envy because, when I have problems, I look at the bench and have so many options," he said.

The remarks underline Mourinho's appreciation of Endrick's qualities, although they do not amount to a promise of increased playing time.

Endrick's lack of regular football has attracted attention from several European clubs ahead of the January transfer window.

Arsenal have reportedly made contact with the Brazilian's representatives over a potential move, according to TEAMtalk, with Mikel Arteta's side exploring opportunities to strengthen their attack.

The Gunners are understood to be interested in the forward's versatility and potential, although no agreement has been reported.

Aston Villa have also been linked with Endrick, having previously emerged as a potential destination for another loan spell.

Spanish radio station Cadena SER reported in October that the Birmingham club were among the leading candidates to secure his services, with Lyon and Roma also monitoring developments.

However, Mourinho has repeatedly resisted suggestions that the Brazil international should leave Madrid.

Last month, the Portuguese coach warned clubs interested in Endrick that he was not planning to sanction a January exit, describing the youngster as an important part of his squad.

That position helps explain why Wednesday's comments should not be interpreted as an indication that Madrid are preparing to negotiate his departure.

Endrick demonstrated the benefits of regular football during the second half of last season, scoring eight goals and providing eight assists in 21 appearances under Paulo Fonseca at Lyon.

His performances in France strengthened his reputation and provided valuable experience after a difficult period in Madrid.

Yet Mourinho's latest assessment suggests that he continues to see a specific role for the Brazilian within his own squad, particularly as a substitute capable of exploiting tiring defences.

Real Madrid face Valencia in La Liga on Saturday, with Mourinho confirming that further rotation will be required because of the demanding fixture schedule.

For Arsenal and Aston Villa, the central question remains whether Endrick's opportunities will increase sufficiently before January to justify another six months in Madrid.

Mourinho clearly values the youngster's attacking qualities. Whether that confidence translates into more substantial playing time may ultimately prove more important than his public praise when Endrick's future is reassessed.
