#2271. Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 6 novembre

MILAN, GONCALO INACIO IL NOME CALDO PER LA DIFESA, NAPOLI, TRE PROFILI PER IL CENTROCAMPO. FIORENTINA, PER IL CENTROCAMPO IL PRIMO NOME È TORREIRA. PARMA, GILARDINO CHIEDE IL RINNOVO DI BERNABÈ. BOLOGNA, RAIMONDO IL CENTRAVANTI DEL FUTURO

Il mercato delle società di Serie A comincia a entrare nel vivo, con diversi club già al lavoro per programmare la sessione invernale e, in alcuni casi, anche le mosse per la prossima estate. In casa Parma tiene banco il futuro di Adrian Bernabé: il centrocampista, che la scorsa estate aveva una valutazione di circa 25 milioni di euro, potrebbe lasciare l'Emilia a parametro zero. Il feeling mai sbocciato con Cuesta e l'insoddisfazione per il ruolo ricoperto potrebbero spingerlo verso una nuova esperienza. Alberto Gilardino, invece, vorrebbe trattenerlo e punta a incontrare il suo agente prima della sosta per discutere il rinnovo. La Fiorentina cerca rinforzi soprattutto a centrocampo e in difesa: Ederson e Ferguson sarebbero profili ideali, ma difficilmente raggiungibili a gennaio. La pista più concreta rimane quella di Lucas Torreira, che vorrebbe tornare a Firenze e potrebbe rappresentare una soluzione per il reparto di Vanoli. Il Bologna guarda invece al futuro di Antonio Raimondo, attualmente protagonista con il Frosinone e autore di otto gol in dieci partite. La Juventus lo sta monitorando da settimane, ma i ciociari non intendono privarsene facilmente già a gennaio. Per la prossima stagione il Bologna dovrà decidere se puntare sul proprio attaccante oppure monetizzare la sua cessione. Sempre in casa Frosinone, attenzione a Fares Ghedjemis: dopo l'interesse del Monaco in estate, la valutazione è salita a 25 milioni di euro. Il club sarebbe disposto a privarsene soltanto davanti a un'offerta importante, preferibilmente da una società impegnata nelle competizioni europee.

Sul fronte delle big, il Napoli prepara un mercato invernale importante, soprattutto a centrocampo, dove potrebbero arrivare due innesti anche alla luce delle incertezze sul futuro di Lobotka e Anguissa. Nel mirino ci sono due talenti del Monaco, entrambi classe 2004: Mamadou Coulibaly e Lamine Camara. Tra le alternative figurano Gabriel Sara del Galatasaray, valutato circa 25 milioni, Bakola del Sassuolo, Bergvall del Tottenham e Aranda del Boca Juniors. Attenzione anche all'attacco, con Varela del Monza in cima alla lista qualora Lucca dovesse partire nuovamente in prestito. Il Milan si prepara ad accogliere Markus Krösche, nuovo Head of Football rossonero, ufficialmente operativo dal 1° gennaio 2027, ma intenzionato ad anticipare il proprio ingresso per programmare già il mercato invernale. La priorità resta un difensore, con Gonçalo Inácio dello Sporting Lisbona in cima alla lista, nonostante una richiesta economica elevata. Piacciono anche il giovane Luca Reggiani del Borussia Dortmund e Massiré Sylla dell'Union Saint-Gilloise. Previsto inoltre un intervento sull'esterno, in funzione anche dei recuperi di Saelemaekers e Chukwueze. L'Inter segue invece Chris Richards del Crystal Palace: il difensore statunitense piace particolarmente a Chivu e potrebbe arrivare già a gennaio, oppure a parametro zero nell'estate 2027, quando il suo contratto sarà in scadenza se non verrà rinnovato. Infine, l'Udinese guarda a più reparti in vista di gennaio: per la difesa torna d'attualità Mass Willemsen, classe 2003 dell'Heerenveen, già cercato in estate, mentre per il centrocampo è seguito Tchaouna, classe 2005 del Catanzaro e nazionale del Ciad.

Il suo contratto dura fino al 2029, e ci siederemo al tavolo per discutere le cose al momento opportuno". #2270. Portsmouth vs Stoke City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 05 Nov 2026 15:52, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:34

Two in-form sides will face off when Portsmouth welcome Stoke City to Fratton Park for Saturday's Championship encounter.

Pompey head into the weekend in 10th position in the Championship table, while the Potters are in fourth spot and a point adrift of automatic promotion.

Portsmouth share the league's current longest unbeaten run with rivals Southampton after winning four and drawing two of their last six matches.

Pompey continued their impressive run of form with a dramatic 3-1 victory over promotion hopefuls Swansea in Tuesday's clash at Fratton Park.

After seeing his opener cancelled out by Zan Vipotnik's 85th-minute penalty, Terry Devlin put his team back in front in the second minute of stoppage time, shortly before Thomas Waddingham netted in a second straight home game to wrap up all three points.

As a result, Pompey, who have battled relegation in the last two seasons, are sitting just one point adrift from the top eight and four points away from automatic promotion, sparking dreams of ending their 16-year wait for Premier League football.

They will go into Saturday's fixture with hopes of recording four consecutive home league wins for the first time since January 2025.

The fourth win on that occasion came via a 2-1 scoreline against Stoke, which may be a good omen as they look to extend their five-game unbeaten home streak.

Stoke, meanwhile, have lost just one of their last 11 Championship outings, representing a significant improvement from starting the campaign with three consecutive league defeats.

In fact, they have won three of their last four outings since suffering their most recent loss in a home clash against Middlesbrough last month.

The Potters recorded clean sheets in wins against Derby County and Preston North End, either side of a goalless draw against Preston North End.

Mark Robins's side missed out on a fourth consecutive clean sheet in their midweek away clash against Blackburn Rovers, but crucially they left Ewood Park with all three points after claiming a 2-1 victory thanks to a Jack McGlynn effort and an own goal.

That result represented a fourth consecutive win on the road and made it five consecutive away games without defeat since losing to Wolverhampton Wanderers at the end of August.

The Potters enjoyed success on their last trip to Fratton Park in October 2025, when a 70th-minute own goal proved enough to fire them to a 1-0 victory.

Portsmouth remain without Benjamin Arthur, Connor Ogilvie, Mark Kosznovszky, John Swift, Luke Brooke-Smith, Keshi Anderson and Colby Bishop.

Striker Marko Milovanovic has been ruled out of Saturday’s contest after he pulled up with a quad issue in the second half against Swansea.

Midfielder Ilyas Mohamed was also forced off with a calf issue in Tuesday’s win, and while it is believed to be a minor problem, it is unclear whether he will be ready for the weekend.

As for the Potters, they head to Fratton Park without the injured trio of Eric Bocat, Lamine Cisse and Ato Ampah.

Aaron Cresswell and Sam Gallagher are on the verge of returning from injury, but it remains to be seen whether they will prove their fitness in time for Saturday’s away trip.

One certainty is that midfielder Ethan Galbraith will return to the fold after serving a one-match ban for an accumulation of yellow cards.

Schmid; Devlin, Poole, Dia, Meghoma; Pack, Adams; Segecic, Bailey, Kavuma-McQueen; Shade

Johansson; Wilmot, Lawal, Graham; Johnson, Soumare, Galbraith, Bombino; McGlynn, Devenny, Bozenik

Brimming with confidence, Portsmouth and Stoke will both fancy their chances of claiming the spoils this weekend, but with both teams proving tough to beat, we think they could cancel one another out in a score draw.

#2269. Scott Parker can make early West Ham statement by entrusting 19-year-old with starting role after Cardiff City impact: Opinion

West Ham started the Scott Parker era in dramatic fashion, as they came from 3-0 down to draw 3-3 away at Cardiff City. WHUFC’s new boss did not hesitate to make changes at the break in the bid to inspire a turnaround either.

Parker made three substitutions at half time, with Joel Piroe replacing Taty Castellanos, Mohamadou Kante coming on for Soungoutou Magassa and youngster Ezra Mayers taking Morato’s place at left-back.

19-year-old Mayers is the reigning Academy Player of the Year and has been around the first-team for much of the season. This was his first Championship appearance though, and Mayers’ impact has given Parker food for thought ahead of the clash with Blackburn Rovers.

Morato has been solid at left-back for West Ham. He has offered a more defensive presence while the starting right-back – usually Kyle Walker-Peters – is given more freedom to progress and link up with star player Jarrod Bowen in attack.

The Brazilian struggled on Wednesday night though, conceding a penalty as Jack Moylan ran riot. When deciding to replace Morato at the break, Parker had two academy graduates to pick from: the more experienced Ollie Scarles, or Mayers.

He went with the latter, and was rewarded for his faith. Mayers offered a front-footed and aggressive approach in his defending as he looked to win the ball back and set the Hammers on their way. The youngster made one tackle and one block, winning one ground duel and even volleying a late chance past the far post (all stats via FotMob).

Watching Mayers, he showed the hunger and desire that WHUFC fans want to see from players – just like new boss Parker did when he was running the show in the Hammers’ midfield.

Parker’s appointment has pleased the majority of supporters. Speaking to fans, you may hear them say ‘He’s West Ham’ – the energy, aggression and passion he played with in the midfield made him a firm favourite and a real standout, winning their Player of the Year award for three consecutive seasons from 2009 to 2011.

WHUFC fans are eager to see Parker manage in a similar way by setting up his team to be intense and fast-paced. Mayers’ substitute appearance showed just this against Cardiff City, and these attributes could see him force a way into the plans.

Pardon the cliche, but Parker ‘gets it’ at West Ham – he understands what it means to play for the club and knows what the fans want from those donning claret and blue. In his brief Cardiff appearance and academy displays, Mayers has shown this too.

By putting faith in Mayers and handing him a first league start for the club, Parker would be making a popular move that could set the tone for his tenure.

#2267. How much Celtic earned in Europa League win over Besiktas

Celtic claimed their first win in the Europa League this season as they beat Besiktas 3-2 at Parkhead.

Martin O'Neill's side came from behind to take their first three points in the competition after three losses up to that point.

And in bagging their first win, Celtic also earned a bonus payment from UEFA for achieving a win in the league phase of the competition.

UEFA offer bonuses to clubs for wins or draws in the league phase of the competition.

By beating Besiktas, Celtic banked €450k or £375k in prize money from UEFA.

The fee comes on top of the €4.31m paid to every club involved in the league phase of the Europa League this season.

Qualification for the knockout round would mean a further €300k from the UEFA prize pot, with Celtic guaranteed a payment for their final position in the league phase, regardless of whether they advance.

The bottom club in the league phase will earn €75k, with that figure increased by a further €75k for every position higher a club finishes in the table.

− victory in the league phase €450k, draw in league phase €150k

− qualification for the knockout round play-offs: €300k per club

− qualification for the round of 16: €1.75m per club

− qualification for the quarter-finals: €2.5m per club

− qualification for the semi-finals: €4.2m per club

− qualification for the final: €7m per club

The UEL winners can expect to receive an additional €6m

#2266. Cardiff City vs Burnley - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 05 Nov 2026 15:56

Cardiff City and Burnley square off on Saturday afternoon in what could prove to be a pivotal game in the battle to avoid relegation to League One.

While the Bluebirds sit in 18th position in the Championship table, Burnley are in 22nd spot, already four points adrift of safety.

With eight points from the last five games, Cardiff boss Brian Barry-Murphy can only be delighted with such a return.

The Bluebirds have played Watford, Middlesbrough, Birmingham City, Wolverhampton Wanderers and West Ham United in succession.

However, squandering a three-goal half-time lead against the latter of those teams would have hurt Barry-Murphy, regardless of West Ham benefitting from the arrival of new boss Scott Parker.

In the grand scheme of things, however, Cardiff are five points above the relegation zone and have put together a seven-match unbeaten run at the Cardiff City Stadium.

Trailing 12th place by just three points, the Welsh outfit are still in contention to launch a surprise playoff bid.

At the same time, defeat to Burnley on Saturday would see Cardiff only sit above the relegation zone by two points.

Fabio Celestini has got Burnley back on track with two respectable draws against Portsmouth and Queens Park Rangers.

While the well-travelled Italian knows that the Clarets need to deliver just a second Championship win of the season as soon as possible, there have at least been positives to take from his opening double header.

Burnley have collected five points from their last five away fixtures, arguably an encouraging return for a squad that has struggled to gel.

Nevertheless, if four results - including this one - go against them on Saturday, Burnley would end Saturday seven points adrift of safety.

With Cardiff right-back Perry Ng suffering an injury in the closing stages against West Ham, Ronan Kpakio is in line to deputise.

David Turnbull, Cian Ashford and Joel Colwill will all be considered for recalls due to the quick turnaround after the West Ham fixture.

Gabriel Osho, who has served a three-match ban, could get the nod over Will Fish in the middle of defence.

Barring any fresh injury concerns, Celestini may stick with the Burnley XI from the draw at QPR.

However, Josh Cullen, Aaron Ramsey and Jaydon Banel are all options in midfield and the final third.

Bashir Humphreys, Hannibal Mejbri and Chelsea loanee Dastan Satpaev remain on the sidelines.

#2265. Le partite di oggi: il programma di sabato 7 novembre

Tanti appuntamenti da non perdere nei principali campionati europei, oggi sabato 7 novembre. In Serie A spiccano soprattutto il derby Parma-Bologna e Roma-Sassuolo, mentre in Premier League i riflettori sono puntati su Arsenal-Hull e Nottingham Forest-Manchester City. Grande attesa anche in Bundesliga per Magonza-Bayern Monaco e in Liga per Valencia-Real Madrid. Di seguito tutte le sfide da seguire nelle prossime ore, in Italia e all'estero.

#2263. Bristol City vs Norwich City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 15:57, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 16:04

In desperate need of positive results, Bristol City welcome Norwich City to Ashton Gate on Saturday afternoon for a Championship clash.

The Robins suffered their third defeat in five matches on Wednesday, losing 2-1 to Wolverhampton Wanderers, while the Canaries continued their winning streak with a thumping 4-0 victory over Derby County at Pride Park.

There was plenty of optimism surrounding Bristol City following the appointment of Michael Skubala and the investment made during what appeared to be a productive transfer window, but 15 games into his tenure, the former Lincoln City boss is already under pressure and could find his position under threat if results do not improve in the coming weeks.

The early signs at the end of August and start of September were encouraging, with the Robins collecting nine points from a possible nine across a seven-day spell against Portsmouth, Preston North End and Burnley.

However, the South West side have won just twice since then, slipping out of the playoff places and into the bottom half of the table.

Scott Twine's 93rd-minute winner against Bolton Wanderers on Halloween lifted the mood around Ashton Gate, and Bristol City appeared on course to make it back-to-back victories when they entered the final 10 minutes against Wolves with their lead intact, only for a seven-minute brace from Sean Neave to turn the game on its head and condemn Skubala to yet another disappointing result.

Bristol City's main concerns have been their lack of attacking threat and continued struggles at home, with only four sides scoring fewer than the Robins' 16 goals this season and just two teams winning fewer than their two matches at Ashton Gate.

Their attacking problems are clearly a concern for Skubala, who switched to a two-striker system against Stoke City and Bolton Wanderers before reverting to a lone forward for the midweek defeat.

Norwich City, meanwhile, make the trip from East Anglia to Bristol in buoyant spirits, having enjoyed a dramatic turnaround in form that has significantly improved their position in the Championship table following three successive victories.

Wins over Charlton Athletic, Watford and, most recently, Derby County have helped Philippe Clement's side climb from 21st to 12th, leaving them closer to the playoff places than the relegation zone.

The Canaries secured their latest three points in emphatic fashion, travelling to Pride Park in good form and facing a Derby side who had not won since early September.

First-half goals from Anthony Musaba, Paris Maghoma and Darlin Yongwa put Norwich firmly in control before substitute Jovon Makamba came off the bench to add a superb fourth.

Before this winning run, Norwich had scored just twice in their previous 270 minutes of football, but Clement's men have since enjoyed a remarkable upturn in front of goal, finding the net 10 times across their last three matches, with their first clean providing another timely boost.

It was the Canaries' first clean sheet in 24 matches, although their defensive record remains a concern with 24 goals conceded in 14 Championship outings, a total bettered by only a handful of sides, while their recent scoring spree has lifted them to the point where only three teams have a stronger attacking record.

Both teams head into Saturday's encounter level on points and could finish the weekend just one point outside the playoff places.

Although Bristol City came through their midweek encounter without any fresh injury concerns, Skubala still has a number of selection decisions to make.

New signing Sam Tickle was recently dropped in favour of Brad Collins in goal, with the Chelsea academy product starting the last three matches.

However, Collins was at fault for both goals against Wolves and could now find himself relegated to the bench.

Lorent Tolaj was forced off with an injury during the Robins' goalless draw with Stoke City last month and is expected to return in the coming weeks, meaning Saturday's encounter is likely to come too soon for the forward.

Twine, who scored a superb first-half free kick against Wolves, is expected to retain his place in the starting lineup.

Having overseen their best run of the season, Clement will be keen to maintain Norwich's winning momentum for the trip to the South West.

Maghoma and Musaba have scored five goals between them across the last three matches and are expected to feature again in attack, while Mathias Kvistgaarden will continue to lead the line in the absence of Mohamed Toure.

Papa Amadou Diallo and Ali Ahmed remain the only other players unavailable for the Canaries.

Collins; Tanner, McNally, Eissat, Pring; Knight, Williams; Cardines, Tolaj, Twine; Ballard

Grimshaw; Stacey, Darling, McCoville, Yongwa; Wright, Field; Musaba, Maghoma, Brooks; Kvistgaarden

Both sides head into this encounter level on points but in contrasting form, with Norwich enjoying a three-match winning streak while Bristol City have struggled for consistency.

Nevertheless, the Robins are due a positive result on home soil, and we expect them to end their recent struggles with a closely fought victory on Saturday.

#2262. Middlesbrough team news and predicted XI to face Watford: David Strelec latest amid Will Lankshear concerns

Middlesbrough remain top of the Championship table despite suffering a disappointing 2-0 defeat away at Wrexham on Wednesday night. Kim Hellberg’s side missed the opportunity to extend their advantage at the summit but remain firmly in the promotion picture.

Boro are currently one point above Wolves in second place and two clear of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City. They were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury and struggled to find the firepower needed to get a result at the Racecourse Ground.

Watford, meanwhile, have endured a difficult start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. The Hornets sit 20th in the table after 14 fixtures and are just four points clear of the relegation zone.

However, they secured a much-needed victory last time out, beating Lincoln City 1-0 at Vicarage Road. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi scored the decisive goal to give Watford a welcome boost.

Hellberg will assess David Strelec’s fitness ahead of Middlesbrough’s trip to Watford on Saturday, but has hinted the striker may not be ready to star, as reported by Teesside Live.

Strelec made a positive impact from the bench during Wednesday’s defeat to Wrexham, offering better hold-up play during an 18-minute cameo. However, having only recently returned from injury, Hellberg believes starting him could carry too much risk.

The Boro boss said: “David has just come back and trained with us for only one week. We will see now, but, in general, I didn’t think he was fit enough, and there was too big of a risk to start him in a game now because of just two days in between.”

Lankshear remains hopeful of being available against Watford, although his absence was felt again on Wednesday as Kyle Joseph struggled to lead the line. Boro’s top-scorer also missed out on an England call-up due to his injury concerns.

Strelec’s campaign has already been disrupted by injuries, including a recent setback on international duty. Summer signing Rokas Pukstas and defender Alfie Jones remain out.

Strelec gets the nod in attack after his impressive cameo at the Racecourse Ground. That sees Joseph drop to the bench while Riley McGree returns to the side ahead of Tommy Conway.

#2260. Osasuna vs Athletic Bilbao - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 05 Nov 2026 15:59, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 16:01

An in-form Athletic Bilbao will aim to post a fourth straight win in all competitions when they continue their La Liga campaign against Osasuna at Estadio El Sadar on Saturday.

Athletic will enter the match off the back of a 2-1 win over Real Sociedad in the Basque derby, while Osasuna suffered a 2-1 defeat to Deportivo La Coruna last time out.

Osasuna boast a record of three wins, two draws and six defeats from their 11 league matches this season, with 11 points leaving them in 15th spot in the La Liga table.

Los Rojillos finished 17th in La Liga last season, so there is plenty of room for improvement this term, but it has been an inconsistent campaign to date.

Jagoba Arrasate's team lost 2-1 to Deportivo in their last league fixture, and they have actually now been beaten in three of their last five matches.

Osasuna have won two of their last four, with their last league win coming against Racing de Santander on October 18, while they beat Atletico Calatayud 6-0 in the Copa del Rey at the end of October.

Los Rojillos have only managed to win 22 of their previous 99 matches against Athletic in all competitions, and they suffered a 1-0 defeat when the pair last met in April 2026.

Athletic have only won one of their last four matches against Osasuna, while two of the last three meetings between the two teams have finished level.

The Basque outfit will enter this game off the back of three straight wins, including a 2-1 success over Real Sociedad in the Basque derby in their first match of November.

Edin Terzic's side beat Getafe 4-0 in their last league game of October, before progressing in the Copa del Rey courtesy of a 6-1 success over Prat.

The Lions have a record of four wins, three draws and three defeats from their 10 league matches this season, which has left them seventh in the table on 15 points.

Athletic finished 12th in La Liga last season, so there is plenty of room for improvement during the current campaign, and a strong challenge for a European finish is expected.

Osasuna will be missing Aimar Oroz, Jorge Herrando and Romain Del Castillo for this weekend's league fixture with Athletic through injury.

There is expected to be a change in the final third of the field on Saturday, with Ruben Garcia potentially being introduced into the side in place of Jonathan Dubasin.

Ante Budimir has scored eight times in 11 appearances this term, and the 35-year-old will again operate through the middle for the home team.

Athletic, meanwhile, will once again be without the services of Unai Egiluz, Gorka Guruzeta and Inigo Ruiz de Galarreta through injury here.

Maroan Sannadi had to be substituted in the early stages of the Basque derby and will also miss out on Saturday, with Robert Navarro expected to be introduced into the side.

Meanwhile, there will once again be starts for both Nico Williams and Inaki Williams.

#2257. Leeds United vs Tottenham Hotspur - prediction, team news, lineups

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 16:00, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 17:44

A resurgent Tottenham Hotspur side travel to Elland Road to take on an out-of-sorts Leeds United outfit in the Premier League on Saturday lunchtime.

The spoils were shared the last time these two teams butted heads in May, with Mathys Tel’s opener cancelled out by a Dominic Calvert-Lewin penalty to rescue a 1-1 draw in North London.

After making a bright start to the season, accumulating eight points from their opening four games (W2 D2), Leeds are now without a win across their last five matches (D3 L2) and have slipped down to 12th in the Premier League table.

The Whites continue to stumble after a 2-0 defeat against Bournemouth, adding to a rough patch that includes a 2-1 loss at champions Arsenal alongside draws with struggling trio Crystal Palace, Manchester United and Sunderland.

Head coach Daniel Farke – who reached an agreement in principle over a contract extension last month – has downplayed concerns over Leeds' recent winless streak, maintaining that his squad remains calm and confident while prioritising reaching the 40-point safety mark as quickly as possible.

Leeds should welcome a return to Elland Road where they are unbeaten across their last eight league matches (W4 D4); they are one of only four Premier League teams yet to lose on home soil so far this season along with Arsenal, Brighton and Hull.

However, the Whites have collected only five points across their last 13 top-flight battles with Spurs, losing 10 of those, while they have failed to keep a clean sheet in any of their last 16 encounters with the North Londoners – a 1-0 home win back in February 2000 was the last time Leeds did not concede against Spurs.

After enduring a six-game winless start to the Premier League, Tottenham’s early-season struggles now seem to be behind them as Roberto De Zerbi has steered his team to three consecutive victories against Coventry, Chelsea and Crystal Palace.

Spurs opened their account with a 3-1 home win over Coventry just under a fortnight ago and have since recorded back-to-back 2-0 successes against Chelsea and Palace, beating the latter last weekend after taking advantage of a 13th-minute red card for Eagles striker Jean-Philippe Mateta.

The North Londoners have risen up to ninth place in the Premier League table, just two points behind the top five and crucially five points clear of the bottom three; they are now bidding to win four league games in a row for the first time since September and October 2023.

De Zerbi has encouraged his players to build on their positive upturn in form, as three straight wins is “not enough” to please the supporters. The Italian also hopes to see a ‘nastier’ side from his attacking players in front of goal after scoring only two goals from 34 shots against Palace.

Spurs head into Saturday’s contest with Leeds seeking to extend their three-game unbeaten away run in the top flight, while they have won each of their last three visits to Elland Road by an aggregate score of 10-2.

Tottenham Hotspur form (all competitions):

Leeds duo Noah Okafor (adductor) and Anton Stach (broken toe) remain major doubts, while Farke "carefully optimistic" that striker Dominic Calvert-Lewin – who has scored seven goals in his last seven home games – will be fit after missing training on Thursday with a foot problem.

Former Spurs defender Joe Rodon is back in full training and in contention to return to the matchday squad after missing the last eight games in all competitions with a hamstring injury. A start is considered unlikely, though, so James Justin, Nico Elvedi and Tarik Muharemovic should all continue in the back three.

James Trafford will "remain in goal" despite his costly mistake in the defeat at Bournemouth, while Lukas Nmecha, Brenden Aaronson and Jean-Matteo Bahoya are three attackers who could be considered for a start should Farke look to shake up his attack.

As for Tottenham, Savio (muscle), Lucas Bergvall, Wilson Odobert (both knee), Mykhaylo Mudryk (ankle) and Xavi Simons (ACL) all remain out, though the latter is making positive progress in his recovery and could return soon than expected.

However, Tosin Adarabioyo has recovered from a calf injury and Rodrigo Bentancur is also available after serving a three-match suspension.

De Zerbi may look to make only two changes to his starting lineup, with Pedro Porro pushing to replace Archie Gray at right-back, while playmaker James Maddison could start at the expense of either Omar Marmoush or Dominic Solanke who are both battling for the centre-forward spot.

The momentum is certainly with Spurs right now, but Leeds have made themselves difficult to beat at Elland Road and we can envisage the spoils being shared in a low-scoring draw on this occasion.

#2256. Why West Brom’s £2m summer addition is becoming a smart signing under James Morrison

West Brom missed their chance to move into the top two as they drew 1-1 with Southampton on Tuesday night. As they prepare to host Millwall this weekend, James Morrison‘s WBA are serious about fighting for promotion this season, winning three of their last five including an impressive 3-1 win over West Ham at the London Stadium.

The start that they have had comes off the back of a successful summer window. Under Morrison, WBA made a real effort to revamp the squad with more youth and several new additions were made with that in mind.

Jimmy-Jay Morgan has taken a lot of the plaudits after all his goalscoring exploits. However, another one of their additions is starting to break into the side, Rabby Nzingoula.

The Baggies spent a reported £2m on the 20-year-old after a decent second half of the season on loan at Nurnberg. He has been a hugely important player over the past five games where West Brom have gone unbeaten.

The 20-year-old arrived from Strasbourg with plenty of potential but relatively little experience of English football. However, three months into his Hawthorns career, he is adapting to the Championship quicker than many would have expected.

Nzingoula has gradually moved from an impact substitute to a regular part of James Morrison’s midfield, making 14 appearances and accumulating 710 minutes. Seven of those outings have been starts, while his average rating of 6.73 suggests he is providing a dependable contribution despite his young age.

There is also more to his game than simply breaking up opposition attacks. Nzingoula has recorded 41 line-breaking passes and created nine chances in his Championship minutes so far. His 1.14 chances created per 90 puts him in the 75th percentile, while his shot attempts rank in the 80th percentile.

These figures suggest West Brom have a midfielder capable of contributing further up the pitch rather than simply protecting the defence.

At 20 years old, Nzingoula has a good level of experience at decent levels. The WBA man has played 37 times in Ligue 1 as well as featuirng in Europe for Strasbourg.

His versatility is another positive. Comfortable operating as either a six or an eight, Nzingoula gives Morrison different options in central and wider midfield areas. His recent performances, including a 7.3 rating against Southampton on November 3, suggest he is beginning to find his feet.

There are still areas for improvement. He has yet to register a goal or assist, his aerial numbers are relatively modest and three yellow cards highlight the occasional risks associated with his aggressive approach.

Nevertheless, West Brom should be encouraged. Nzingoula is only 20, has already demonstrated Championship-level defensive qualities and is showing signs of becoming a more complete midfielder. For a reported £2m, the Baggies could have secured a very smart long-term investment.

#2255. Man Utd transfer news: Red Devils 'tempting' Premier League defender with Old Trafford move

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 16:02, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:47

Manchester United have reportedly set their sights on Fulham defender Calvin Bassey ahead of the January transfer window.

Michael Carrick's men ended their worrying winless run on Tuesday night, when a Matheus Cunha brace helped the Red Devils come from two goals down to secure a 3-2 victory over Roma in the Champions League.

Absent from the Theatre of Dreams earlier in the week, Man Utd wonderkid JJ Gabriel is heading towards a switch away from England, with European giants Real Madrid keen on the teenager.

Despite their comeback success over the Italians on Tuesday, the Red Devils and head coach Carrick are still under an enormous amount of pressure given their wretched form in the top flight.

Man Utd have won just one of their opening nine top-flight contests of the 2026-27 campaign - their worst start in over four decades - leaving them 17th in the Premier League standings, level on points with the relegation zone.

According to Football Insider, Manchester United are desperate to make improvements in defensive areas during the January transfer window after conceding at a rate of knots so far this campaign.

The report states that the Red Devils are hoping to sign a new left-footed central defender, given that Lisandro Martinez's time at Old Trafford is seemingly drawing towards a toxic conclusion.

As a result, it is understood that Carrick's troops are eyeing up a New Year swoop for Bassey, who has been one of the standout performers for Fulham amid a difficult start to the term for the Cottagers.

Despite the mess at the Theatre of Dreams at present, it is believed that the Nigeria international could be tempted by a switch to Man Utd if they pushed for his signature in January.

Bassey's current contract at Craven Cottage expires at the conclusion of the 2026-27 season, however, Fulham can extend those terms by a further 12 months - an option that is likely to be triggered imminently.

After bolstering heavily in their attacking and midfield ranks in the summers of 2025 and 2026 respectively, Manchester United are now on a mission to bring defensive stability back to Old Trafford.

The Red Devils are relying on a host of long-serving assets at the moment, with Harry Maguire, Luke Shaw and Diogo Dalot all going through periods of struggle with the team in recent years.

At 26 years of age, Bassey is entering the prime of his professional career and would be a much-needed breath of fresh air for Man Utd, who are in danger of being involved in a relegation scrap this season.

#2254. Who says Martin O'Neill is out of touch? Besiktas win was a tactical masterclass

The AGM that Celtic will hold at Parkhead on Tuesday, December 1, promises to be as explosive as any of the fireworks displays that were staged in the East End of Glasgow last night.

The Scottish champions’ performance in their home Europa League league phase match against Besiktas last night was, after a few damp squib displays in recent weeks, pretty incendiary too.

As rockets lit up the night air around the stadium and the sound of pyrotechnics being set off rang out, Martin O’Neill’s men finally found their sparkle.

Beating opponents who were sitting in the automatic qualification spots in the table alongside the likes of Benfica, Bayer Leverkusen and Juventus and avoiding an unwanted record was always going to be a big ask.

But O’Neill, the septuagenarian whose suitability for his high-pressure, high-profile role has been widely questioned in the wake of losses to Rangers, Ferencvaros, Celta Vigo and Falkirk, showed the flame still burns brightly.

The Northern Irishman, who selected Sebastien Tounekti, Kasper Hogh and James Forrest in his front three, vowed that his men would go for it when he spoke to BBC Radio Scotland ahead of the game.

However, the flat first-half display looked a little like a damage-limitation exercise, an attempt by a manager who knew his side was lacking in confidence after winning just three of their previous 10 outings to avoid a heavy home reverse and further opprobrium from the stands.

That was understandable to an extent. The visitors, who had beaten Hoffenheim and Marseille and drawn with Crystal Palace in their opening three fixtures, had Leandro Trossard, the Belgium international, pulling the strings for them in the middle of the park.

Still, an arena which is normally a cauldron of noise on big European nights and which is renowned across the world for its febrile atmosphere was eerily silent at times as the hosts sat back, concentrated on containing their rivals and offered next to nothing going forward.

Celtic left back Kieran Tierney at Parkhead last night (Image: Andrew Milligan)

Oh Hyeon-gyu, the South Korean international who spent a season-and-a-half at Celtic between 2022 and 2024 and won five major honours in that time, has clearly endeared himself to Besiktas supporters since moving to Istanbul in a €14m transfer in February.

The 25-year-old was given a huge ovation by the thousands of travelling supporters who had been shoehorned into the corner between the South Stand and the Lisbon Lions Stand when he emerged from the tunnel before kick-off.

He stood on the turf he once graced on a weekly basis in his bare feet and conducted a rendition of the “Oh Oh Song” which Black and Whites fans chant whenever he nets a goal. Eat your heart out Graham Roberts.

Oh is one of those players, and there have been quite a few of them over the years, who goes from strength to strength after departing Celtic. The 6ft 2in striker would have improved the hosts’ forward line in the first half last night.

Benjamin Nygren had the perfect chance to put the home team ahead and set proceedings alight in the seventh minute when he received the ball from Sebastien Tounekti in space in the opposition area. The Swede had time to control the pass and place his shot. He fired over the crossbar.

It was the only attempt on goal in the opening 45 minutes.

Vaclav Cerny, the former Rangers winger who returned to Parkhead for the first time since he sprayed Celtic fans with a water bottle after a 3-2 derby victory in March last year, was booed by the home support every time he got on the ball.

There was a huge roar in the first half when Liam Scales slid in and scythed the Czech down. There was precious little else for the green-and-white-clad hordes who had turned up – and thousands of green seats lay empty – to shout about.

Preferring the experience of Jordan Lotomba to the raw youthful exuberance of Colby Donovan was, regardless of the Swiss full-back’s lack of recent game time, a smart move.

The defender’s rustiness perhaps showed when he conceded a penalty with a rash foul on Ernest Poku. Junior Olaitan netted after Sam Johnstone had saved his initial effort.

Celtic manager Martin O'Neill at Parkhead last night (Image: Andrew Milligan)

It was a cruel, even unfair, setback. Celtic had not looked like conceding up until that point. But O’Neill threw on Donovan, Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran for Lotomba, Tounekti and Forrest respectively. His inspired substitutions changed the game completely.

The 74-year-old has been portrayed as an old-school manager who is out of touch with the modern game in certain quarters in recent weeks as his charges have toiled and suffered painful losses. He silenced his doubters by producing a tactical masterclass.

Osmand equalised with his first touch of the ball and then set up Duran for a goal. The Colombian then put his side 3-2 ahead after Orkun Kokcu had lashed in a long-range effort from fully 30 yards out. That opportunist strike ultimately clinched a much-needed, morale-boosting and richly deserved victory.

“It was designed to do exactly what it did,” said O’Neill afterwards. “We have been playing Sunday, Thursday, Sunday, Thursday, Wednesday. We have played something like nine games in 24 days. Some players need a little breather. You can get re-energised from coming on the field.”

Can Celtic, who take on Kilmarnock in the William Hill Premiership at Parkhead on Sunday, tap into the feelgood factor this performance and result have generated and maintain this sort of form in the weeks ahead?

Their defensive play throughout the 90 minutes and the offensive play during the last half an hour augur well for the future.

Cameron Carter-Vickers, who was pilloried for his error-strewn showing against Falkirk last weekend, was a colossus before making way for Dane Murray due to hamstring tightness. Is there really friction between the centre-half and his manager? It didn’t look like it.

Celtic failed to push on after their 7-1 mauling of Hearts in the league last month - but this fine triumph over a formidable side indicates that better times might lie ahead.

#2253. Leeds lineup vs. Tottenham: Predicted XI for Premier League clash

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 16:05, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 18:28

Leeds United will assess the conditions of four players ahead of Saturday’s Premier League clash with Tottenham Hotspur at Elland Road.

Head coach Daniel Farke has confirmed that Noah Okafor (adductor) and Anton Stach (broken toe) are both major doubts, while he is "carefully optimistic" that star striker Dominic Calvert-Lewin will be fit after missing training on Thursday with a foot problem.

Joe Rodon has missed the last eight games in all competitions with a hamstring injury, but the former Spurs defender is back in full training and in contention to return to the matchday squad.

A start is considered unlikely, though, so a back three of James Justin, Nico Elvedi and Tarik Muharemovic is set to remain intact, while James Trafford will "remain in goal" despite his costly mistake in the 2-0 defeat at Bournemouth last weekend.

Ethan Ampadu and Ao Tanaka will likely link arms again in centre-midfield as Jayden Bogle and Gabriel Gudmundsson operate as wing-backs, the latter of whom is fit having recovered from a knock.

With Leeds struggling to deliver a regular goal threat this season, Farke may be tempted to hand Jean-Matteo Bahoya his first start for the club, with the French attacker having played for just 11 minutes across three substitute appearances since joining in the summer.

Brenden Aaronson is another pushing to start at the expense of either Daniel James or Harry Wilson, while Lukas Nmecha is also hoping to earn a recall and could line up alongside Calvert-Lewin should Farke opt to begin with two focal points in attack.

#2251. 2025 summer signing is slowly becoming a key midfielder for Millwall under Alex Neil: Opinion

Millwall have been a very dangerous side since Alex Neil was appointed boss. After narrowly missing out on promotion following a Championship play-off defeat to Hull City last term, the club are looking to bounce back once again.

Neil’s side have built a reputation for identifying players capable of making an impact in the Championship without spending excessively. This summer has seen Kyrell Lisbie come in and impress, while Lyndon Dykes has been an excellent free transfer.

While immediate impacts are always looked for with transfers, patience is also required sometimes. Derek Mazou-Sacko is becoming another example of that, and is showing that Millwall’s long-term recruitment working well despite a mediocre start to the campaign.

The French midfielder arrived from Rodez in August 2025 for a reported fee of around €500,000. There was little fanfare surrounding his arrival, but he has gradually established himself as an important part of Alex Neil’s side.

At just 22, he is already showing the qualities needed to become a reliable Championship performer. Mazou-Sacko is not a midfielder who will regularly grab headlines with goals, assists or eye-catching creative numbers.

His value lies in the less glamorous aspects of the game, and his latest statistics underline why Neil has increasingly trusted him. His aerial ability is another useful asset, standing at 6ft 1in, That physical presence helps Millwall compete for second balls and provides additional protection in front of their defence.

There is little suggestion that he should be expected to offer much more creatively. His five chances created place him in the 11th percentile, while his 440 touches rank in the 16th. However, that should not necessarily be viewed as a weakness.

He has completed 267 passes at an accuracy of 83.4%, including 46 line-breaking passes, showing he can retain possession and move the ball forward without taking unnecessary risks.

His development has been gradual. Having progressed through Troyes, spent time on loan at Nancy and subsequently made around 34 appearances for Rodez in Ligue 2, the move to English football represented an important opportunity.

He contributed to Millwall’s play-off push last season, making 23 league appearances and registering one goal and one assist. Now approaching 35 appearances for the club, he looks increasingly comfortable at this level.

Millwall will need more than one dependable defensive midfielder if they are to achieve their ambitions, but Mazou-Sacko is proving to be an excellent piece of business.

He might never become the most exciting player in Neil’s squad, but he makes the team more difficult to play through. Over a 46-game Championship season, that reliability can be just as valuable as an attacking match-winner.

#2249. Martin O'Neill reveals Celtic masterplan behind game-changing subs

Celtic manager Martin O’Neill claimed his game-changing triple substitution was always part of the plan after the switches rescued his side’s Europa League hopes.

Subs Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran were the heroes as the Hoops came from behind to beat Besiktas 3-2 and get off the mark in the tournament at the fourth time of asking.

The Hoops struggled to get into the Besiktas penalty box before Duran, Osmand and Colby Donovan came on just after the hour mark.

James Forrest had forced the only save for the visiting goalkeeper before then and Celtic trailed 1-0 after Jordan Lotomba conceded a penalty on his first start.

But Osmand fired home with his first touch and set up Duran for the first of the Colombian’s two goals as Celtic emerged victorious in a thrilling finale.

Kasper Hogh and Sebastian Tounekti had offered little threat or safe possession of the ball and the difference in performance after the triple change was striking.

“It was designed to do exactly what it did,” O’Neill said of his selection choices.

“We have been playing Sunday, Thursday, Sunday, Thursday, Wednesday, whatever day it happens to be. We have played, I don’t know, nine games in 23 or 24 days.

“Some players needed a little breather and you can get re-energised from coming on the field.

“So I’m going to say that this was exactly our plan and those players who came on were going to come on at some stage or another in the game.”

The crowd played their part in a memorable final half-an-hour as Celtic secured a fourth win in 11 matches.

Fans had reacted angrily after a 2-0 defeat at Falkirk on Sunday, but the players got them back on their side with their reaction to going behind and when Duran netted seconds after Besiktas equalised in the 86th minute.

“They were a credit to the football club and you can’t get a bigger accolade than that,” O’Neill said. “They were really, really terrific.

“We could have caved in. We were doing fine then the penalty goes in, but we came roaring back. Substitutes made a big, big difference, got a lot of pace up front, but I was just delighted with the whole team.

“It might just give us a platform to charge on. It’s not going to be easy, but at least we can park the European events for a few weeks’ time, but this definitely gives us a chance.”

#2248. Newport County vs Walsall - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 05 Nov 2026 16:11

Newport County and Walsall square off in the FA Cup first round on Saturday afternoon.

These two teams play in the fourth tier of English football, sitting in 14th and third position in the League Two table respectively.

Having signed a long-term contract with Walsall, Lee Grant will get time to secure promotion to League One.

However, the Saddlers entered November having lost just one of 13 games in League Two, that defeat coming by a 4-2 scoreline to runaway leaders Salford City.

Walsall have conceded just eight goals in their other 12 league games, while they bounced back from the Salford disappointment by beating Colchester United last time out.

Sitting in third place with a game in hand on the teams below them, Grant will be delighted with the progression of his team.

Only once in eight years - the 2020-21 coronavirus-hit campaign - have Walsall failed to reach the second round of the FA Cup.

Just as impressively, Newport have only not progressed to the second round of the competition on two occasions in 11 years.

After consecutive near-misses with relegation to the National League, Newport have 18 points from their opening 14 games in 2026-27.

Hayden Mullins has brought some much-needed stability to the club, losing just four times across the aforementioned period.

Although Walsall are yet to suffer a league away defeat, Newport have not lost in seven League Two fixtures at Rodney Parade.

This tie was drawn out in the first round of the 2016-17 FA Cup, Newport prevailing by a 2-1 scoreline on that occasion.

Having been able to make changes in the EFL Trophy in midweek, Mullins may stick with a similar Newport XI from the 4-1 win over Chesterfield last weekend.

Harrison Biggins played a key role with a goal and an assist, taking him to seven such contributions in League Two during 2026-27.

Shaquille Gwengwe is available again after serving a three-match ban for an incident in the fixture with York City on October 17.

Ryan Leak, James Furlong, Sven Sprangler, Courtney Clarke and Andy Dallas could all return to the Walsall XI, with Grant likely to want to freshen up his team.

Declan Skura, Mason Hancock, Isaac Moore, Reece Smith and Aaron Pressley - five players who have accumulated a lot of game time in League Two - could drop down to the substitutes' bench.

#2247. Free transfer signing is rediscovering his best form at Watford: Opinion

Watford have started this season poorly under Alessio Dionisi. Despite promising signs to start the year, Dionisi’s side have been stuck in a rut with led to talk of yet another managerial sacking from the Hornets.

Watford, who host Middlesbrough this weekend, need inspiration from somewhere as they look to climb away from the wrong end of the Championship table, and Myron Boadu could be providing exactly that.

The Dutch striker arrived at Vicarage Road on a free transfer in September after a difficult spell at PSV, but recent performances suggest he is beginning to rediscover the qualities that once made him such an exciting prospect.

His brace against Norwich City on October 31 was followed by an assist in the 1-0 victory over Lincoln City three days later. More importantly, these contributions have come alongside encouraging performances in other areas of his game.

Boadu is looking sharper, making intelligent runs and showing the work rate that made him so effective earlier in his career. There is a reason Boadu was once valued at more than €40m and attracted a €17m move to Monaco.

He scored 32 league goals in 64 appearances for AZ Alkmaar before enjoying another productive spell on loan at Bochum, where he registered nine goals in 19 Bundesliga appearances. Injuries and inconsistent opportunities subsequently disrupted his progress, but the underlying talent has never disappeared.

His early Watford numbers are encouraging. Across seven Championship appearances and 379 minutes, Boadu has scored twice and provided one assist, averaging approximately 0.7 goal involvements per 90 minutes, according to FotMob.

His 1.40 expected goals compared with two actual goals also suggests he is taking his opportunities well rather than relying on an unsustainable volume of chances.

There are encouraging signs beyond his attacking output, too. Boadu has registered five tackles, nine recoveries and 18 duels won, while recording 20 touches inside the opposition penalty area. These figures reflect a forward becoming increasingly involved in both Watford’s pressing game and their attacking transitions.

His developing partnership with Iker Bravo is another significant positive. Boadu’s understanding with the fellow forward appears to be growing, with their movement complementing one another without requiring extensive preparation.

The Norwich performance demonstrated their potential, while his contribution against Lincoln showed he can also influence games through his work without necessarily finding the net himself. Of course, seven appearances do not represent a complete revival, and Boadu still needs to deliver consistently over a longer period.

However, Watford cannot afford to overlook the encouraging signs. If he maintains this intensity and continues building his understanding with Bravo, the Hornets could have a valuable attacking weapon for the remainder of the campaign.

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 16:25, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:48

Manchester United have reportedly made contact with Didier Deschamps over potentially taking charge from Michael Carrick as head coach.

The former Red Devils midfielder is under extreme pressure at Old Trafford following an awful domestic start to the campaign, with the 20-time English champions currently sitting in 17th spot in the Premier League table.

Despite being level on points with the top-flight relegation zone after nine matches, Man Utd produced a marvellous comeback on the continental scene earlier in the week, beating Italian giants Roma 3-2 at the Theatre of Dreams.

The Red Devils have been heavily linked to Europa League specialist Unai Emery in recent weeks, however, Aston Villa are supposedly closing in on a contract agreement for the Spaniard.

One of the top-performing bosses in European football at the moment, Cesc Fabregas of Como recently masterminded a 9-1 battering of Lens in the League Phase of the Champions League on Wednesday night.

According to French publication Footmercato, Manchester United have added another high-profile name to their shortlist of possible managerial replacements for Carrick, who is walking on thin ice at Old Trafford.

The Red Devils have supposedly made initial contact with former France manager Deschamps, who has been out of work since taking Les Bleus to the semi-finals of the 2026 World Cup in North America.

The report states that the 58-year-old is not particularly keen on a switch to the Theatre of Dreams given the club's current predicament, however, a deal between the two parties cannot be ruled out.

It is understood that Man Utd have also touched base with Aston Villa hero Emery and ex-Germany and Bayern Munich head coach Julian Nagelsmann, with Carrick potentially one poor result away from the sack.

After departing his French post in July, it is believed that Deschamps was under consideration for the vacant position at Real Madrid before Los Blancos decided to reunite with Jose Mourinho.

To a certain extent, Didier Deschamps was the final evolution of Gareth Southgate on the international scene, receiving plenty of criticism during his long reign but ultimately lifting titles - something that his English counterpart failed to do.

The 58-year-old managed superstars such as Paul Pogba, N'Golo Kante, Olivier Giroud and Kylian Mbappe to success at the 2018 World Cup in Russia, where Luka Modric's Croatia were beaten in the final.

After that, a 2021 UEFA Nations League title did little to address the clamour for further major trophies in France, with Deschamps's side failing to lift the 2026 World Cup despite possessing arguably the best starting XI at the tournament.

#2245. Martin O'Neill gushes over 'class' Celtic star after European heroics

Camilo Duran earned glowing praise from Martin O’Neill after coming off the bench to fire Celtic to a dramatic Europa League victory over Besiktas.

The Colombian struck twice in a thrilling 3-2 win at Celtic Park as the Hoops finally got off the mark in the competition on their fourth attempt.

Duran was introduced alongside Callum Osmand and Colby Donovan shortly after the hour mark with Celtic trailing 1-0 and struggling to seriously threaten the visitors. The changes transformed the contest.

Osmand equalised with his first touch just 49 seconds after entering the action before setting up Duran to put Celtic ahead. Besiktas hit back to make it 2-2, but Duran struck again late on to complete his double and seal a huge European victory.

O’Neill was delighted with the impact of the forward and revealed he had always intended to introduce him from the bench. “Duran has been fabulous for us all season and got his two goals,” O’Neill said.

“He has played a lot of games and looked a bit tired in the last couple. But he’s a class player, and he was always going to come on.

“I’m delighted with the performance. It was fantastic.”

The victory brought back memories of O’Neill’s first spell in charge of Celtic as a raucous home crowd roared their side to victory. “That was a special European night,” he said. “It reminded me of the olden days.

“The subs re-energised us and played a big part in the win.”

Martin O'Neill reveals Celtic masterplan behind game-changing subs

Martin O'Neill fires 'a lot more' Celtic demand at £3.5m signing

The telling Michael Nicholson quip that reveals Celtic's transfer strategy

O’Neill was particularly pleased with the character his players showed after falling behind and then being pegged back at 2-2 late in the game. “I told the players afterwards that they are a credit to the club,” he added.

“You can’t get a bigger accolade than that, but we put our heart and soul into the game. We played really good stuff, particularly in the second half.

"At 2-2, it looked like we would drop two points but we came roaring back to win the game. There was so much spirit and desire to get the three points. The character shone through big time.

"The winners came to the fore, as did the new boys.”

Duran also earned praise from fellow substitute Osmand following his match-winning display. “Camilo did amazing; he’s such a poacher,” He said. “Credit to him.

“The team dug in, stayed strong and we got our rewards. I was so happy to come on, score a goal and get the win.”

The victory gave Celtic renewed hope of progressing in the Europa League ahead of their meeting with Portuguese second-tier side Torreense on November 26. O’Neill said: “We rose to the occasion and we have given ourselves a chance.

“We are off the mark now. We have points on the board, and we can park it for a few weeks now. There is still a thought about qualifying, but that is a long way off.

“Hopefully it gives us a platform to charge on, but it’s not going to be easy.”

#2244. Raul Jimenez reveals major four-word update that Wolves fans will love

Raul Jimenez has shared a fresh update on his ongoing recovery from an MCL injury, which will provide a serious boost to Wolves fans as the Mexican striker's return to action slowly but surely edges closer.

The good times are rolling right now at Wolves, who have ascended to second in the Championship behind league leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday lunchtime's crunch trip to Wrexham following three successive victories over Watford, Cardiff and Bristol City.

It has, of course, relieved some much-needed pressure and scrutiny upon the shoulders of Cesar Peixoto, though the Portuguese boss is still not completely out of the woods and remains searching for the backing of some of the Old Gold faithful since his arrival from Gil Vicente to replace Rob Edwards over the summer.

Nonetheless, Wolves are beginning to truly find their rhythm and that's only made all the more impressive by the continued absence of Jimenez, who begun the campaign in strong form upon his emotional homecoming to the Black Country but has not kicked a ball in anger since September.

Jimenez has, however, revealed a new progression update relating to his recovery update, and the words used will provide delight to supporters, teammates and Peixoto alike as all at Molineux eagerly anticipate the veteran frontman's return.

The 36-year-old has been out of action since before the three-week international back in September after suffering a serious MCL injury 36 minutes into Wolves' 1-0 EFL Cup third-round defeat at Everton, in which many believed he shouldn't have been risked in the first place.

Peixoto, then, was left to rue his selection decision when the 130-cap Mexican international's injury turned out to be more severe than expected, forcing the Championship promotion hopefuls to initiate ligament surgery instead.

Surgery would see Jimenez ruled out of proceedings until January, representing a significant blow for Wolves in the wake of a lightning-fast reintroduction to life at Molineux after lighting up the World Cup with co-hosts Mexico by scoring three goals from four matches upon leaving Premier League outfit Fulham.

Wolves' patchy form following the international window amid defeats to both Middlesbrough and Bolton Wanderers ensured that his absence was sorely-felt, although others have stepped up to the mantle in recent weeks to allow the ex-Benfica and Fulham striker to focus entirely on his recovery process instead of rushing to get back sooner rather than later.

The experienced striker recorded his 500th career appearance against Bristol City on Wednesday

His latest update is, however, an extremely positive one, and it may leave the Old Gold faithful optimistic of an even earlier return to the pitch.

Jimenez has kept supporters in the loop with many social media posts and updates throughout his time on the treatment table, which will only be appreciated by the club's fans as they await his return.

He's been stepping up his recovery process in recent weeks, moving on to more intensive physical work as his condition continues to improve.

Jimenez has generally appeared to be in good spirits, too, and that's evidenced by his latest update.

Speaking via his Instagram story on Thursday, Jimenez wrote: "Making really good process 🔥💪🐺."

Just four words were required for an admission that will leave Wolves supporters delighted, with the caption accompanying a video of the goal-getting ace undertaking leg exercises in a bid to regain his strength and mobility.

It was either side of a series of stories posted to show Jimenez going through his paces with some arduous gym work, and he's certainly not prepared to let up or allow this injury to get the better of him as he continues to work harder and harder ahead of a return early in the new year.

Jimenez's fellow frontmen at Wolves have begun to find the back of the net with increasing regularity in recent times, and he'll only be hoping that trend continues until he's back on the pitch himself.

One of the main worries when Jimenez suffered his injury was about exactly who was going to come to the fore and provide the cutting-edge in the final third to compensate for his long-term absence after kicking off his second stint at Wolves with three goals from six Championship fixtures.

Thankfully, both Adam Armstrong and Sean Neave are finding form to ensure that Jimenez's absence doesn't end up derailing Wolves' campaign.

Armstrong, who has largely struggled for form since arriving from Southampton in February, looks to have rediscovered his goalscoring boots - which are esteemed at this level, of course - after recording his first strikes since August in back-to-back wins against Watford and Cardiff.

He wasn't on the scoresheet during Wolves' most recent midweek victory at Bristol City, where Newcastle United loanee Neave instead stepped up to the billing by appearing from the bench to score his first professional goals in style with a late brace in the final 10 minutes to seal a dramatic 2-1 away victory for the white-hot visitors.

#2243. Parma vs Bologna - prediction, team news, lineups

By Jonathan O'Shea | 05 Nov 2026 16:44

One of Serie A's form sides, Bologna will aim to extend their success streak when they visit Emilian rivals Parma on Saturday evening.

While the resurgent Rossoblu have won three straight games before heading to Stadio Tardini, their hosts seem to be turning a corner too.

After being brought in during the last international break, Parma boss Alberto Gilardino experienced a tough start to life as their new head coach.

The club's second-highest Serie A scorer has a less stellar record in management, and he lost his first two matches after returning to the Tardini.

Yet, a gritty goalless draw with Udinese finally gave him something to build upon, before Parma beat an ailing Atalanta side on Monday night.

Rising star Simone Lontani notched the final goal in a stunning 3-0 away win, and that edged the Gialloblu out of the bottom three, with eight points from 10 games so far.

Having given their confidence a big shot in the arm, they can now anticipate the Derby dell'Emilia; and after February's 1-0 win, Parma will be seeking rare back-to-back victories over Bologna.

Of course, the visitors will have very different ideas, particularly as they have lost just one of their last seven league games at the Tardini.

Bologna also sit six points above their old foes in Serie A, due to a stunning run of results under new management.

Since Raffaele Palladino's arrival midway through September, only league leaders Lazio have picked up more points, and the Rossoblu recently posted their third win on the spin.

After beating Cagliari in Sardinia and conquering AC Milan at San Siro - the latter largely thanks to Samuel Mbangula's hat-trick - Palladino met up with his former club Monza.

It was far from plain sailing, as Bologna had to fight back from behind - and Lukasz Skorupski saved a penalty - but they finally got their first home win of the season.

In contrast to some dismal form at Stadio Dall'Ara, only Como have recorded more away wins in Serie A this calendar year, so they can certainly target maximum points in Parma.

The hosts will be without two potential starters on Saturday, as injury-prone midfielder Adrian Bernabe has suffered a thigh strain in training and young defender Dominik Drobnic has injured his left foot.

Abdoulaye Ndiaye is likely to replace the latter in Parma's back three, but it remains to be seen who starts in the final third.

Lontani's three Serie A goals have come from just four shots on target, and he is among several candidates to feature up front: El Bilal Toure leads a list also featuring David Romero, Nesta Elphege, Ousmane Diallo and Matija Frigan.

While Bologna's strikers have been very quiet this term, wingers Mbangula and Federico Bernardeschi have scored three goals apiece.

Furthermore, attacking left-back Juan Miranda has provided an assist in each of his last four matches.

Though Roberto Piccoli should be back in the Rossoblu's squad, they must travel without injured trio Lorenzo De Silvestri, Jay Enem and Emil Holm.

#2242. James Morrison issues firm statement over West Brom star’s Premier League potential

West Brom missed the opportunity to move into the Championship’s top two after being held to a 1-1 draw by Southampton on Tuesday night. James Morrison’s side now turn their attention to this weekend’s clash with Millwall, with the Baggies preparing to host the Lions in a bid to strengthen their promotion push.

Ahead of this weekend’s clash, Morrison has been asked about his midfielder Ousmane Diakite, who has been in excellent form this season. The WBA boss was questioned on Diakite’s potential, specifically whether he is a Premier League player in the making, as reported by Steve Tudgay.

Morrison issued a confident response, saying, “Yes he is. With us!” His comments back up those of former WBA man Paul Robinson, who was full of praise for Diakite when speaking to the Express and Star.

“He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him,” the former defender said. “It’s about consistency. We have seen Diakite in the Blackburn game, then the Preston game and now he has gone to that next level against West Ham.

“He stood out against players who are expected to win the league. He destroyed them in the middle of the park.”

Diakite has made an encouraging start to the 2026/27 Championship season, establishing himself as an important figure in the WBA midfield. The 26-year-old has featured in 14 league games, making 11 starts and averaging a FotMob rating of 7.22.

His biggest strengths have been his defensive contributions, recording 25 tackles, 19 interceptions and 58 recoveries. He has also won 81.8% of his aerial duels, placing him in the 95th percentile among midfielders.

Diakite has shown composure in possession too, completing 88.5% of his 544 passes, including 86 line-breaking efforts. His recent performances have been particularly impressive, earning ratings of 7.8 against Southampton and Blackburn and 7.7 against Preston.

Although he is yet to register a goal or assist, his physicality, defensive reliability and ability to retain possession have made him a valuable asset for West Brom.

Morrison is building an exciting, well-balanced midfield at West Brom, combining defensive strength, energy and attacking quality. Diakite and Jayson Molumby provide a solid foundation, while summer signing Felix Horn Myhre adds creativity and goals.

Isaac Price has also emerged as a key attacking threat, already contributing five goals this season. There is promising young talent in Rabby Nzingoula, Toby Collyer and Harry Whitwell, too, giving Morrison options for the future.

The group has helped West Brom make a strong start to the campaign, with its blend of experience and potential offering encouragement for a sustained promotion push.

The 29-year-old has made just 24 appearances in the nearly two-and-a-half years he's spent at St Andrew's and was loaned out last January back to Scotland to see out the season with Dundee FC.

With just one one-minute cameo appearance in their EFL Cup to his name this season, he'll surely be angling for more game time, and Birmingham should be in a position to grant that.

Wright's career is a peculiar one. The former youth Scotland international was on the books at Rangers for two-and-a-half years, but never during his time at Ibrox was he a certified starter.

The winger was a very solid squad player during his time with the Gers, and a move south of the border for the first time in his career to a side expected to dominate League One in 2024/25 presented a good opportunity for him to take a more important role elsewhere.

That never came to fruition at Birmingham, though. Despite scoring a late winner on his debut against Wigan Athletic, injuries stopped Wright from playing much of a part in Chris Davies' side's record-breaking campaign.

#2240. Next Man United manager: Special Cesc Fabregas agreement truth 'revealed' amid Red Devils struggle

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 16:50, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:51

Como president Miran Suwarso has denied the presence of a special clause in Cesc Fabregas's managerial contract.

Manchester United fans are watching the 39-year-old's coaching work with considerable envy at the beginning of this term, with the Red Devils supposedly reaching out to Didier Deschamps over potentially replacing Michael Carrick.

It is not only the dugout where the 20-time English champions are plotting change, with Man Utd keen to improve their defensive ranks in the New Year, leading to a supposed interest in Fulham and Nigeria centre-back Calvin Bassey.

Winning just one of their opening nine top-flight fixtures, Carrick's side are sitting in a lowly 17th position in the Premier League standings, lying outside of the division's relegation zone on goal difference.

Fabregas's up-and-coming Como outfit embarrassed Man Utd in the Champions League on October 21, when Argentinian ace Nico Paz bagged a brace in a 4-1 battering of the Red Devils in the League Phase.

Appearing on Piers Morgan Uncensored, Como president Suwarso discussed the future of head coach Fabregas, who has understandably attracted plenty of interest from other European clubs.

Reports in September suggested that the 39-year-old had a 'special agreement' in his contract that would allow him to depart the Serie A club to join three of his former sides - Barcelona, Arsenal or Chelsea - for a fixed compensation fee.

Como chief Suwarso has rubbished those rumours, saying: "He does not need a clause to go anywhere he wants. In a business like this, he is a friend more than anything else.

"He can go wherever he wants, whenever he wants. I could even say if tomorrow, he calls me and he says, 'Hey, I want to go to [Bayer] Leverkusen', for example. I would say, 'If that is something you want to do, then go'. I don't even need any compensation."

Fabregas's current contract at Como expires during the summer of 2028, before which intrigue in the young Spanish tactician is undoubtedly set to grow exponentially, including from clubs in the Premier League.

Given that it is down to Fabregas to decide the timing of his exit from Como, it is extremely unlikely that struggling Manchester United will be getting their claws on the Spaniard anytime soon.

The former Gunners midfielder is building something special at the Serie A newcomers, who could be the surprise package of the Champions League this term, following in the footsteps of Norwegian side Bodo/Glimt during 2025-26.

Man Utd are mess from the boardroom to the pitch at the moment, and nothing but the club's proud history stands above their competitors at present, leaving the Old Trafford faithful in relative misery.
