#2238. How many points do Man Utd need in the Champions League?

By Naim Beneddra | 05 Nov 2026 17:01

Manchester United's dramatic comeback against Roma has revived their Champions League campaign, but Michael Carrick's side still have work to do to avoid an early European exit.

Tuesday's 3-2 victory at Old Trafford ended a nine-match winless run and lifted the Red Devils to 17th in the 36-team league standings, with six points from their opening four matches.

However, United are only two points above the elimination places at the halfway stage, having suffered consecutive defeats to Atletico Madrid and Como before their recovery against Roma.

With four fixtures remaining, including a daunting encounter with Bayern Munich, the question is how many more points Carrick's men need to secure a place in the knockout rounds.

The results of the previous two Champions League campaigns offer a useful indication, although the eventual qualification threshold will depend on how the other 35 teams perform.

Under the Champions League's expanded format, the top eight teams progress directly to the round of 16, while those finishing between ninth and 24th enter a two-legged knockout phase play-off.

The bottom 12 clubs are eliminated, with no opportunity to drop into the Europa League.

United currently occupy 17th place with six points, having recorded two victories and two defeats. Their goal difference stands at zero, with eight goals scored and eight conceded.

That places them inside the play-off positions for now, but offers little protection against further setbacks.

In the inaugural 36-team league phase in 2024-25, Club Brugge claimed the final play-off position with 11 points, finishing ahead of Dinamo Zagreb on goal difference.

The qualifying threshold was lower last season, when Benfica finished 24th with nine points, narrowly edging out Marseille on the same total.

Meanwhile, 16 points were enough to secure eighth place and direct qualification for the last 16 in both campaigns.

Those figures provide three possible targets for United.

A single additional victory would take Carrick's side to nine points, matching the lowest successful play-off total recorded under the current format.

However, that would not guarantee qualification, particularly given how tightly contested the lower positions are after four rounds.

Five more points would take United to 11, equalling the 2024-25 threshold, while two victories would leave them on 12.

Reaching 12 points would represent a considerably more encouraging position based on recent history, although even that total cannot be regarded as mathematically sufficient this season.

The task becomes substantially harder if United want to avoid the play-offs altogether.

To reach the 16-point benchmark, they would need another 10 points from a possible 12, meaning three victories and a draw, or four consecutive wins.

There is also an important distinction between qualifying for the play-offs and reaching the last 16.

Finishing anywhere from ninth to 24th would keep United in the competition, but they would still need to win a two-legged tie in February to progress.

For Carrick, securing a play-off place is therefore the more realistic immediate objective, with a top-eight finish requiring a near-perfect second half of the league phase.

United's remaining schedule offers opportunities to collect the points they need, although two of their opponents currently sit above them in the table.

Their next European assignment is a trip to Sporting CP on November 25, with the Portuguese side occupying 10th place on nine points.

The Red Devils then welcome RB Leipzig to Old Trafford on December 8, before hosting Bayern Munich on January 20 and concluding the league phase away to Villarreal on January 27.

Leipzig and Villarreal have each collected only three points from their opening four matches, making those encounters particularly important to United's qualification prospects.

Two victories against those opponents would take Carrick's side to 12 points, even if they failed to collect anything from their meetings with Sporting and Bayern.

That would have been enough to reach the play-offs in both previous editions, though United's final position this season would still depend on other results.

The Sporting fixture could also prove significant. A positive result in Lisbon would reduce the pressure on United ahead of their December home match and potentially strengthen their position against a direct rival for the knockout places.

Their January meeting with Bayern presents a different challenge, particularly given the German champions' attacking quality and European experience.

United's situation is also notably less comfortable than that of several other English clubs.

Arsenal have accumulated 10 points, while Aston Villa, Manchester City and Liverpool each have nine, leaving United as the lowest-ranked Premier League representative in the competition.

Elsewhere, Napoli sit 27th with three points, while Atletico Madrid and Roma are also in danger of missing out on the play-offs after collecting four points apiece.

For United, the victory over Roma has provided valuable breathing space, but the margin remains narrow.

Another six points would take them to 12, a total that has historically been sufficient to survive the league phase. Their next two matches, against Sporting and Leipzig, could therefore go a long way towards determining whether the Bayern encounter becomes a battle for qualification or an opportunity to chase a top-eight finish.

#2237. Huddersfield Town and Middlesbrough won’t believe what's happening at non-league side

It's not uncommon for ex-EFL stars to wind down their careers in non-league, but what's happening at Harborough Town, especially, will catch the eye of Middlesbrough, Huddersfield Town and neutral fans, too.

Harborough are one of the fastest-rising non-league sides right now, having only just joined Step 6 in the non-league ladder in 2010. This season, they're competing in the National League North (Step 2) for the first time in their history, after winning the Southern League Premier Central last year.

With the likes of Morecambe, Macclesfield FC and Hereford now competing with them in league action, the need for extra quality and some ex-EFL professionals was needed in the summer, and continues to be needed as the year goes on.

And that led Mitch Austin's side to form quite the peculiar strike partnership.

Harborough hit headlines ahead of their first season at National League North level by securing the arrival of Britt Assombalonga.

The 33-year-old is still technically Middlesbrough's record signing, having arrived from Nottingham Forest for £15 million back in the summer of 2017. However, Will Lankshear will be set to break that once the add-ons in his move from Tottenham Hotspur are activated.

It's safe to say that Lankshear will probably be better remembered in a Boro shirt, but Assombalonga wasn't exactly a dud. He scored double figures in three of his four Championship campaigns at the Riverside, including 15 in his debut year to help his side reach the play-offs.

After leaving Teesside in 2021, the 33-year-old bounced around Turkish clubs, in between a short stint at Watford, before playing a small part in Barnet's season last year in League Two, and has now dropped a couple more divisions to play for Harborough.

Now, though, he has some competition up top in the form of Kyle Hudlin, the former Huddersfield man and widely recognised as the tallest outfield player in England, standing at six-foot-nine.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Hudlin signed for Huddersfield off the back of scoring in the National League play-off final for Solihull Moors in 2022. He made just 10 appearances for the Terriers, spending the majority of his time out on loan, including a stint at Newport County, where he scored six in 27 League Two appearances in 2024/25.

After a year in Vietnam, he's returned to England and has signed for Harborough.

Harborough have been on quite the meteoric rise, with four promotions in 15 years, the final three of which have come in the last five seasons.

Their first in the National League North has been steady, with Mitch Austin's side sitting two points above the bottom four in what is turning out to be yet another very competitive Step 2 campaign, after last season saw two sides relegated on 50 or more points.

Assombalonga has been in and out of the side, owing to a few niggling injuries, but has two goals and two assists in six league starts this season.

Harborough do have a small excuse for a slower start than in previous years, though, as they've gone through three qualifying rounds in the FA Cup to reach the First Round proper for just the second time in their history, and for the second time in three years.

Those who remember them from two years ago will know that they took League One side Reading all the way at the Select Car Leasing Stadium in the second round, losing in extra time after drawing 3-3 with them after 90 minutes.

This season, they travel to another third-tier side in the first round, with Mitch Austin's men heading to Leyton Orient. They'll be wanting to cause another scare, and even follow through on upsetting the O's this weekend.

It's not only Assombalonga and Hudlin who have EFL reps under their belts in this Harborough side. Behind them is former three-time League One champion Michael Jacobs, who has over 450 appearances in the Football League to his name and was playing at Northampton Town last year.

It's those players who will be called upon by the Bees to help them through difficult matchups, not only in the league against ex-league sides, but also in FA Cup bouts against teams three divisions higher.

Do you think Harborough Town could cause an FA Cup upset this weekend? Let us know in the comments.

With the amount of ex-EFL scoring power in the squad now, there'll be an expectation for the club to start firing more in the National League North, of which their 13 goals are a divisional low.

As and when they do, Huddersfield and Middlesbrough fans may keep a small glancing eye on whether it's either of their former pro players doing so. And if they both combine against Leyton Orient on Saturday, it's sure to get some traction amongst footballing circles, too.

#2236. Sean Neave won Wolves the game against Bristol City, and he can do the same vs Wrexham: Opinion

Wolves staged an impressive fightback to defeat Bristol City on Wednesday night. Scott Twine put the Robins ahead, and it appeared they would be securing a valuable three-points against an in-form Wanderers side.

However, Cesar Peixoto would introduce Sean Neave in place of Fer Lopez on the 73rd minute, and the move changed the game. First, the Newcastle United loanee pounced on the rebound to level the scores, before meeting Ladislav Krejci’s header back across the box to slide home and win it for the Old Gold with a minute to spare.

The goals were Neave’s first and second for Wolves, and in senior football. The 19-year-old has offered some bright performances off the bench to date, and the traits that helped him win the Bristol City game could see him play a key role against Wrexham this Saturday too.

Neave is a raw and developing young striker. He had only played two times for Newcastle United’s first-team before joining Wolves, but a prolific record at U21 (19 goals in 36 games) and U18 (21 goals in 37 games) means he comes highly regarded from his parent club.

For the most part, Neave has been used as an impact player from the bench. He started and assisted in the win over Burnley and played the first 45 minutes against Portsmouth in the following game.

However, at the moment, it feels he is most effective as an impact sub, and the Bristol City game showed that. Having watched Neave closely at both Wolves and in his Newcastle United development, he has a great burst of pace to pounce on loose balls and run beyond the defensive line.

This was on show in his goals against Bristol City – first pouncing on a rebound before meeting a header at the back-post to tap in his second. In between those two goals, he got the jump on the Robins defence to run in behind before guiding an effort wide off the inside of his foot.

Neave’s sharpness, speed and quick movement will be a worry for Wrexham. A lot of their centre-backs are strong, but they do not have the speed to deal with Neave.

By either playing in balls for Neave to run onto or subbing him on late against tired legs, he can expose the lack of pace among the likes of Joe Worrall, Max Cleworth or Dom Hyam.

Game time is key for the development of all young players, and Neave arrived at Wolves knowing he would have to earn his opportunities. Raul Jimenez joined to lead the line, while Adam Armstrong has a fantastic record at Championship level.

However, Jimenez’s injury means Neave has found opportunities, and he’s taking them. The brace against Bristol City will hopefully be an exciting sign of what’s to come from the teenage striker – both over the course of this season and in his career.

Neave will be taking it one step at a time, but just as he did against the Robins, he can be a thorn in the side of Wrexham’s defence with his pace and movement on Saturday.

#2235. Queens Park Rangers vs Lincoln City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 05 Nov 2026 17:09, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 17:10

Queens Park Rangers will face Lincoln City for the first time in 55 years when the two sides lock horns at Loftus Road on Saturday.

The R's are currently sitting in 11th place in the Championship table, while the visitors start the weekend in 19th position.

QPR may be one point adrift of the top eight, but they have found wins hard to come by over the last couple of months, having won just two of their last 10 matches.

The most recent of those victories came via an emphatic 5-0 scoreline against Swansea City, only to fall to a heavy 4-1 loss away to Southampton a week later.

Julien Stephan's side took a point from their latest outing against Burnley, with Jonathan Varane netting a second-half equaliser in a 1-1 draw at Loftus Road on Tuesday.

They should fancy their chances of picking up at least another point from Saturday's fixture, considering they have lost just one of their seven home league games this season (W3, D3).

A sturdy backline has helped provide the foundation for their strong home form, having conceded just six goals in their seven outings at Loftus Road, with only two teams letting in fewer on home turf.

While they have not faced Lincoln since 1971, QPR have won four of their previous six official meetings with the Imps (D1, L1).

Lincoln, meanwhile, are focusing on preserving their Championship status in their first season in the second tier since the 1960-61 season.

The Imps are currently sitting five points above the bottom three after winning four, drawing three and losing seven of their 14 Championship outings this season.

They have struggled to add to their points tally in recent times, having lost five of their previous seven matches, including their last two outings against Birmingham and Watford.

The Imps fell to a 3-1 home loss against Blues last weekend, before they were beaten by a narrow 1-0 scoreline in their midweek away clash against the Hornets.

As a result of Tuesday's loss, Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw's side have now gone three away games without a win since starting the Championship campaign with three consecutive victories on the road.

They have the league's joint third-best defensive away record with eight goals conceded, although they have netted just six times on their travels in the Championship this term.

QPR forward Alfie Jones will be out until the new year after sustaining a hamstring injury in the defeat to Southampton last weekend.

The R’s are also without the services of Amadou Mbengue, Tariq Lamptey, Nicolas Madsen and Rumarn Burrell, while there are doubts surrounding Jake Clarke-Salter’s availability.

Richard Kone, who has netted four goals in the last three games, is expected to be available for selection despite appearing to be forced off with a physical issue in the midweek draw with Burnley.

As for the Imps, they remain without the injured duo of Tendayi Darikwa and Mason Melia, while Conor McGrandles is a doubt after missing the last two matches.

Forward Ben House will drop out of the squad to serve a one-match ban after picking up his fifth yellow card of the Championship season in Tuesday’s defeat to Watford.

Lincoln are hopeful that Mikkel Ladefoged and Ethan Wheatley will prove their fitness in time for the trip to Loftus Road.

Queens Park Rangers possible starting lineup:

Charles; Adamson, Dunne, Edwards, Kemper; Poku, Varane, Kamara, Vale; Kone, Smyth

Wickens; Coubis, Elerewe, Bradley, Reach; Konak, Varfolomeev; Hackett, Street, Jefferies; Draper

QPR may not be in the best of form, but they have proven to be a tough team to beat on home turf this term, and considering Lincoln have lost five of their last seven matches, we think the signs points to a home win on Saturday.

#2234. Birmingham City fans will love Jude Bellingham's reaction to England news

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have received call-ups to the England senior squad under Thomas Tuchel for the first time as a duo, with the former paying tribute to their early-career spells at Birmingham City.

The Premier League and Championship will halt their respective campaigns after the upcoming weekend's round of fixtures, with the Three Lions in Nations League action against Croatia and Spain.

It has been a relatively successful year for England on the footballing stage, with a third-placed finish at the FIFA World Cup hosted in the United States, Canada and Mexico being the nation's best in the tournament since winning it in 1966.

Jude Bellingham was an integral part of the side's run to the semi-finals and has continued to impress in the four Nations League matches during the previous international window.

Many of the high-profile names at Tuchel's disposal can credit spells at various EFL clubs for aiding their eventual development, including Ballon d'Or winner, Harry Kane.

Unsurprisingly, Bellingham was quick to reference his boyhood club yet again in a proud family milestone.

A reunion with Chris Wood has been debated amongst Bluenoses ahead of the January window

Jude and Jobe Bellingham are the most high-profile names to have graduated from Birmingham's Wast Hills academy setup this century, with the former becoming the club's youngest-ever player and goalscorer seven years ago before initially moving to Borussia Dortmund.

The West Midlands outfit then opted to retire the '22' shirt to help create a legacy, and that has only grown stronger through the 23-year-old's current exploits with Real Madrid and England.

Jobe, 21, played 26 times for Blues between 2022 and 2023 before moving to Sunderland for £3m, and after the Black Cats' Premier League return, also signed for Dortmund in a £27.8m deal.

He has since shone for the Bundesliga giants, and after impressing domestically and in the UEFA Champions League, the 12-time Under-21s international earned his first senior Three Lions call-up on Friday.

Such feats were marked by his older brother on social media, who posted a picture of them sporting Birmingham colours during their academy days.

During the early years of his career, Jobe has often been compared to his older brother, despite rightfully wanting to create his own story on the pitch.

That has clearly been the case given his rave reviews at Dortmund, whilst he also racked up over 100 Championship appearances for Birmingham and Sunderland.

Like Jude, the Stourbridge native has never lost sight of the impact Blues staff had in order for him to flourish at such a young age.

“I’m so grateful for everything they’ve (Birmingham) done for me over many years, since I was really young," he said shortly after leaving B9.

Blues fans, how proud are you of Jude and Jobe Bellingham's exploits? Comment below!

“At Birmingham, a lot of the academy coaches helped loads, just with my understanding of the game. I think they’ve made me a better person, I believe. There’s also a lot of players in the first team who helped me grow into the first-team environment.

Whilst they have their own career paths to focus on, both Jude and Jobe will be loving the developments at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park right now, which is a stark contrast to how the club was run during their respective breakthrough years.

Knighthead's plans for the Sports Quarter and Powerhouse stadium have seen Jude involved in promotional videos, whilst he was also present at the pre-season friendly against FC Barcelona.

On the pitch, Chris Davies' side are doing their utmost to end a long-standing Premier League exile, as Blues sit third in the Championship having lost just two of their first 14 games.

Joyous feelings will be reciprocated by those associated with the club for the progress made by the duo, who, in turn, will hope to see the club back in the top-flight very soon.

#2232. Fleetwood Town vs Rochdale - prediction, team news, lineups

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 17:09

Aiming to record three consecutive successes at their Highbury Stadium fortress, Fleetwood Town host fellow League Two outfit Rochdale for a clash in the first round of the FA Cup on Saturday afternoon.

The Cod Army have taken Arsenal's scalp on home soil in recent times in the EFL Cup, whilst the Dale are struggling to get a grip on the fourth tier following their promotion from the National League.

Despite mulling around in the lower reaches of the League Two standings since their League One relegation in 2024, Fleetwood Town have enjoyed the spotlight recently after a historic cup success.

On October 27, the Cod Army welcomed Premier League champions Arsenal to Highbury and knocked their visitors out of the EFL Cup, with the hosts earning a quarter-final tie with Everton in the middle of December.

Scoring in both the beating of the Gunners and last weekend's 3-1 third-tier victory over Bristol Rovers, Aaron Loupalo-Bi is experiencing a breakthrough campaign in senior football, with the Fulham loanee netting five competitive goals for Fleetwood so far.

That success over the Gasmen last time out extended the Cod Army's unbeaten run at Highbury Stadium to five matches (W4 D1) in all competitions, whilst Matt Lawlor's men have lost just once across seven home league contests to date.

Rather unsurprisingly, that sole Lancashire loss came at the hands of Saturday's visitors Rochdale in September, when first-half efforts from Dajaune Brown and Aden Flint sealed maximum League Two points for the Dale.

Rochdale are back in the EFL this campaign following a three-year absence, winning the National League playoff final over Boreham Wood at Wembley Stadium, with that particular clash decided by penalty kicks.

Since returning, the Dale have failed to distance themselves from an impending relegation dogfight, with Saturday afternoon's visitors in the midst of a seven-game winless run (D2 L5) across League Two and EFL Trophy duties.

Alarmingly, Ian Watson's men have lost each of their past three competitive contests by an aggregate scoreline of 13-3, suffering a 4-1 EFL Trophy defeat by Doncaster Rovers at Crown Oil Arena on Tuesday night.

Last tasting fourth-tier success on October 3 at home to Swindon Town, Rochdale have slumped down to 20th spot in the League Two table, just three points above 23rd-placed Colchester United.

Runners-up in the EFL Cup in 1962, the Dale have not enjoyed such success in the FA Cup and last season's attempt at glory ended in the fourth qualifying round at the hands of York City.

Scoring Fleetwood's third goal in the win over Bristol Rovers, midfielder Josh Robertson is pushing for a rare start this weekend.

The Cod Army could involve attacker Will Davies from the start on Saturday, with the 27-year-old providing an assist against the Gasmen last time out.

A hero at wing-back for Fleetwood in their conquering of Arsenal in the EFL Cup, Scotsman Shaun Rooney should start in a more natural centre-back role on Saturday.

Captaining Rochdale at the age of 41 earlier in the week, Ian Henderson should keep his spot at the focal point of attack.

That being said, the Dale will be keen to get Derby County loanee Brown back into the starting XI for a winner-takes-all clash at Highbury.

Following their historic home success over Arsenal last month, Fleetwood will be extremely confident of repeating the trick against a fellow League Two side this weekend.

Rochdale have conceded 18 goals across their past five matches and are unlikely to keep the Cod Army at bay on Saturday.

#2231. Bailey Cadamarteri needs to escape Wrexham: Portsmouth & Plymouth must act

#2229. Spurs have one Preston North End transfer clause that already looks doomed

Preston North End's 12-season stay in the Championship is in serious jeopardy based on their early season form.

The Lilywhites were long-term stayers in the second tier the last time they were here, keeping themselves in from 2000 until 2011 with some near promotion misses along the way, before dropping into League One.

Since 2015, PNE have been back in the Championship, mostly being a mid-table team, albeit with some brief flirtations with the top six and also the drop zone, and there was hope over the summer that a switch in transfer business would unlock a new level for them.

North End are usually quite frugal with their transfer budget, cherry-picking some younh, high-quality Premier League loanees and opting to recruit permanent players on cheaper fees or free transfers.

There was much evidence of that last summer when Alfie Devine, Lewis Dobbin, Daniel Jebbison and Harrison Armstrong arrived on loan, whilst they saved paying fees until January of last season, when Odel Offiah, Andrew Moran and Callum Lang all arrived at Deepdale.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

For whatever reason though, that tact changed this past summer, with around £15 million spent on new faces and over £5 million recouped on Milutin Osmajic and Ben Whiteman's sales.

And £6 million of that windfall spent was making the loan signing of Devine a permanent one, which came as a surprise to many North End fans, given the interest surrounding his services.

Celtic, Southampton and Wolves were among the clubs that were keen on taking Devine from Spurs, but even after the initial deadline was missed to seal a deal with the playmaker, PNE were able to convince the 22-year-old that Deepdale was the best place for his future.

Devine was once the youngest player and scorer for Tottenham when he featured in an FA Cup clash with Marine in 2021, but he had to go out on several loans to develop, with stints taken in at Port Vale, Plymouth Argyle and Belgian outfit Westerlo before ending up at PNE.

And with Tottenham being a big-spending club looking to turn around their fortunes under Roberto De Zerbi, there was no room for Devine in his midfield for 2026-27, leading to the decision to let him move on permanently for a decent fee, much like they did with Will Lankshear and Ash Phillips when selling the pair to Middlesbrough.

Spurs hold extra clauses when it comes to Lankshear and Phillips, which means they have a vested interest in their futures and will want Boro in the Premier League, as well as matching rights on the pair, which means they are given the chance to match any accepted bid that comes in for their services - it's not quite like that with Devine, but there's still a sell-on clause in there.

The Daily Mail reported at the time of the deal that Tottenham had negotiated a 20 per cent sell-on clause with PNE for Devine, which is based on any profit the Lancashire outfit end up making on Devine - as it stands though, that could be in jeopardy.

The reason why Devine was so in-demand during the summer was his form for North End, with eight goals scored from midfield and six assists registered.

But he's showed nothing like that kind of ability for PNE in the 2026-27 season, scoring just one goal and notching one assist, with the Lilywhites looking like they're in big relegation trouble already.

Ahead of their 15th match of the Championship season against Southampton, North End have just five points on the board and are nine points from safety, which whilst not confirming relegation by any stretch of the imagination, they'll need to go some way to overturn the form they've showed so far.

Paul Heckingbottom's early sacking and club icon Graham Alexander coming into replace him hasn't helped results whatsoever, and Devine is yet to show what he's really capable of - with the general consensus of the fanbase being that he's missing the link-up with now-Southampton forward Dobbin that he shared last season.

Type the most common nickname next to each of the 24 clubs in the 2026–27 Championship. Leave off “The”. Some popular alternatives count too.

Clubs in the 2026–27 Sky Bet Championship, listed alphabetically.

Undoubtedly there's a lack of quality in the North End squad compared to last season, and that's showing on the pitch - Alexander didn't even turn to Devine in their 2-0 loss to Stoke City when he was sat on the bench, and he had to wait until the second half earlier this week against Sheffield United to come on, assisting Callum Lang's consolation goal in a 3-2 loss.

With the current trajectory that PNE find themselves going in, Devine will likely not be playing in League One next season if PNE find themselves there, but they won't be getting their £6 million back based on this season's performances, no matter how much potential the former Wigan Athletic youth prospect has.

It would also mean that Spurs do not get any more money for Devine despite their 20 per cent sell-on clause, which they will find a shame, as they would've been banking on Devine continuing to prosper at Deepdale - unlike their clauses for Lankshear and Phillips, Devine's clause is one that they can perhaps almost forget about.

#2228. West Bromwich Albion vs Millwall - prediction, team news, lineups

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 17:44

Aiming to record their maiden home success of November, West Bromwich Albion welcome struggling Millwall to The Hawthorns for an all-important Championship clash on Saturday afternoon.

The Baggies shared the spoils with promotion rivals Southampton earlier in the week in the Black Country, whilst the Lions were tamed by Max Bird and Birmingham City further East in the West Midlands.

Since a disappointing derby defeat by Birmingham on October 10, West Brom have strung together an impressive five-game unbeaten run (W3 D2) in the Championship, suggesting that the Baggies are genuine promotion contenders this campaign.

One of the pre-season title favourites were forced to split the points at The Hawthorns on Tuesday night, when Samuel Edozie's first Southampton goal since the 2023-24 term was not enough to defeat the hosts.

Scoring the opener from the penalty spot against the Saints earlier in the week, Isaac Price has joined summer arrival Jimmy-Jay Morgan on five second-tier goals at the beginning of this campaign.

Despite a lack of quality wide attackers, James Morrison's side are competent in forward areas, scoring thrice in a statement victory at the base of West Ham United last Saturday afternoon.

Tipped by pundits and supporters alike for a campaign of midtable mediocrity, the Baggies are currently occupying sixth spot in the Championship rankings, just two points behind arch rivals Wolverhampton Wanderers in second.

After being within a whisker of earning automatic promotion to the Premier League in May, Millwall have endured a fall from grace at the start of this campaign, with this weekend's visitors winning just two of their past eight matches (D3 L3).

The Lions were unable to build on their Halloween battering of Derby at St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park on Tuesday night, when first-half goals from Alex Cochrane, Bird and Jay Stansfield sealed a comfortable victory for in-form Birmingham.

Defeated in four of their opening seven league outings (W2 D1) in 2026-27, Alex Neil's side have now lost as many away matches as they did across the entire span of last season, when Millwall were the division's best travellers.

Only beating the league's basement boys in Preston North End and Derby since the middle of September, the Lions have slumped down to 13th spot in the Championship table, five points behind eighth-placed West Ham.

Despite the understandable doom and gloom at the moment, Millwall have cause for optimism ahead of Saturday's trip to the Black Country, with the Londoners unbeaten across their past 10 battles (W7 D3) against the Baggies.

West Brom will miss the presence of captain George Campbell this weekend, with the American forced off against Southampton because of a groin problem.

Nat Phillips is the obvious candidate to replace the 25-year-old in defence alongside Wales international Chris Mepham.

Brayann Pereira will continue to play at right-back in Noah Galves's long-term absence, whilst Tammer Bany is pushing for his first-ever Albion start.

Millwall are down to the bare bones at right-back, where Tristan Crama and Ryan Leonard remain sidelined.

As a result, summer signing Jordi Osei-Tutu should keep his spot, however the Lions are expected to make changes further up the field.

Neil's men were much-improved at Birmingham following the introductions of Romain Esse and Josh Coburn in the second half.

West Bromwich Albion possible starting lineup:

O'Leary; Pereira, Mepham, Phillips, Styles; Nzingoula, Molumby, Diakite, Price; Morgan, Heggebo

Crocombe; Osei-Tutu, Taylor, Cooper, Sykes; Kelly, Mazou-Sacko; Lisbie, Cundle, Esse; Coburn

After collecting four points from matches with promotion favourites West Ham and Southampton, West Brom should be confident of conquering Millwall this weekend.

The potential additions of Esse and Coburn into the Lions' XI should improve matters, however, the visitors are still likely to suffer defeat at The Hawthorns.

#2227. Liam Rosenior hints at untold story behind Chelsea exit

By Naim Beneddra | 05 Nov 2026 17:48

Former Chelsea manager Liam Rosenior has suggested that the full story behind his short-lived spell at Stamford Bridge has yet to emerge, promising to reveal what "really happened" when the time is right.

The 42-year-old, who was dismissed in April after just four months in charge, has broken his silence on his experience in west London during an interview with French magazine So Foot.

Now managing Paris FC, Rosenior described his time at Chelsea as both the best and most difficult experience of his career, while expressing frustration at the way he was portrayed in the English media.

More intriguingly, the former Strasbourg coach hinted that certain aspects of his Chelsea experience went beyond football, although he declined to explain precisely what he meant.

His remarks represent his most extensive public reflection on a turbulent period that began with considerable promise before ending in a dramatic collapse in results.

Rosenior arrived at Stamford Bridge in January 2026 after impressing at Strasbourg, another club owned by Chelsea's parent company BlueCo.

He replaced Enzo Maresca and initially appeared to justify the decision, overseeing six victories in his first seven matches, with the only defeat coming against Arsenal in the EFL Cup.

However, that promising start eventually gave way to mounting difficulties.

Chelsea won just four of their next 16 matches and lost seven of their final eight games under Rosenior, prompting the club to dismiss him in April.

Despite that disappointing conclusion, the Englishman insists that his experience was far more complicated than the results might suggest.

"It was the best experience of my life, and the hardest too," Rosenior told So Foot.

He then delivered a particularly intriguing assessment of what had happened during his brief tenure.

"I don't want to go into details now; I'll talk about it one day, when the time is right."

Rosenior went on to question whether the treatment he experienced reflected the values of tolerance he associates with England, suggesting that some of what occurred had little to do with football.

He offered no specific allegations or examples to explain that statement, leaving considerable uncertainty about which events he was referring to.

His comments should therefore not be interpreted as confirmation of any particular dispute behind the scenes.

Chelsea's decision to dismiss Rosenior followed a severe deterioration in results, including five consecutive Premier League defeats without scoring.

The Blues subsequently appointed Xabi Alonso as their permanent manager ahead of the 2026-27 campaign.

Rosenior, meanwhile, returned to France during the summer to take charge of Paris FC, where he has enjoyed an encouraging start to the season.

While Rosenior stopped short of revealing the full circumstances behind his departure, he was considerably more forthcoming about the public scrutiny he faced.

The former Chelsea manager recalled feeling uncomfortable even during his team's early winning run, when he believed the attention surrounding his personality began to overshadow events on the pitch.

He cited an incident during Chelsea's Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain, when he handed written tactical instructions to Alejandro Garnacho while his side were playing with 10 men.

The gesture attracted ridicule on social media, adding to criticism of Rosenior's touchline behaviour and communication style.

According to the Englishman, the attention quickly extended beyond his coaching decisions to his clothing and the way he spoke.

He then returned to the possibility of revealing more about his Chelsea experience.

"One day, I'll tell you what really happened," he insisted.

The former Hull City manager also questioned the expectations placed on Premier League coaches, arguing that there is a preconceived idea in England of how they should look, speak and behave.

Rosenior believes those expectations can create additional pressure on managers whose personalities do not fit the conventional image.

He acknowledged that criticism is an unavoidable part of football management but suggested that media coverage and social networks can exert a disproportionate influence over public perceptions.

For Rosenior, one of the most significant lessons from Chelsea was discovering how quickly a narrative about an individual manager can become established, regardless of their own attempts to explain their methods.

His comments come as he rebuilds his managerial reputation with Paris FC, who are currently fourth in Ligue 1.

That encouraging start has given Rosenior an opportunity to move forward professionally, although his latest interview makes clear that he has not entirely put the Chelsea experience behind him.

For now, he has chosen to keep the more sensitive details to himself, leaving his promise to tell the full story as the most significant unresolved element of his first extended account of life at Stamford Bridge.

#2226. 'There is a chance' - Sheffield Wednesday face Keillor-Dunn recall fear

A new era is underway at Sheffield Wednesday following the appointment of Dave Challinor as the club's new manager.

Challinor was named as Henrik Pedersen's replacement at Hillsborough this week, and while his immediate focus will be on improving Wednesday's form in League One, he may already have one eye on the January transfer window.

Wednesday made a total of 18 new signings during the summer transfer window as part of their rebuild under new owner David Storch, and Davis Keillor-Dunn was one of their standout additions, with the forward arriving on loan from Wrexham until January.

After scoring 35 goals in 78 games during a productive spell at League One side Barnsley, Keillor-Dunn joined Championship outfit Wrexham in January for a fee of around £2 million, but he made just seven substitute appearances for the Red Dragons in the second half of last season.

Having continued to struggle for game time at the SToK Cae Ras in the early stages of this season, Keillor-Dunn made the temporary move to Wednesday in September, and he has scored four goals and provided one assist in 10 appearances for the Owls so far, including netting the opener in his side's 1-1 draw with Blackpool at Hillsborough on Sunday.

However, Wednesday may be fearful of losing Keillor-Dunn in January after Wrexham striker Sam Smith was ruled out for the rest of the season with an ACL injury, meaning manager Phil Parkinson could look to bring the 29-year-old back to South Wales to provide extra cover in the attacking areas.

When asked if he was concerned that Keillor-Dunn could be recalled in January, FLW's Sheffield Wednesday fan pundit Patrick McKenna admitted there is a possibility the forward could return to Wrexham given their injury situation and their underwhelming start to the Championship season.

"I think it is kind of surprising that Wrexham have been so poor this season, so they may want to bring him back," Patrick told FLW.

"If Wrexham were having a decent season in the Championship, they wouldn't need him.

"There is a chance that they may want to buy someone else in the January transfer window and spend a bit of money, but there is a chance that they will want Keillor-Dunn back.

"I suppose we'll just have to wait and see on that one.

"Wrexham have been having a dreadful season, and with Josh Windass out as well, they may want to bring Keillor-Dunn back for some attacking prowess."

With Louie Barry out injured, Keillor-Dunn has been Wednesday's main attacking threat in recent weeks, so it would certainly be a major blow if they were unable to extend his loan beyond January.

Wrexham could opt to recall Keillor-Dunn in January after Smith's season-ending injury, but he has been deemed surplus to requirements for much of his time at the SToK Cae Ras, so it seems more likely that the Red Dragons will enter the market for a replacement.

#2225. He played with Scott Parker at Charlton Athletic - Then he was jailed for £15m fraud

Centre-back Richard Rufus was in the same Charlton Athletic team as Scott Parker, but despite an eleven-year career at The Valley, his post-retirement life has been one misstep after another.

The life of the professional footballer can be an extremely insecure one. All players are one serious injury from their career being over, while a downturn in form could mean that their next contract could be their last.

A lucky few make it through to their mid-to-late thirties without any mishaps, but there's also a number who don't even make it that far, and in the case of the former Charlton Athletic defender Richard Rufus, a series of injuries resulted in his career ending at just 29 years of age.

But Rufus's post-playing career has not been a good one. The former centre-back has repeatedly found himself in trouble with the law, to an extent that, despite having been inducted into Charlton's hall of fame in 2013, he's at risk of becoming better known for this post-career proclivities than he was for his talents on the pitch.

As a player, Richard Rufus was the definition of a one-club man. Born in Lewisham in 1975, he joined Charlton Athletic as a youth and progressed through the club's academy, making his debut for them as a 20-year-old against Wolverhampton Wanderers in April 1995.

He would go on to spend 11 years at The Valley, and the early years of his career suggested that he could go on to get to the very top of the game. He was selected six times for the England under-21s over the course of 1996 and 1997, and by this time he was an established face at the heart of the Charlton central defence.

In 1998, he was part of the Charlton team which returned to the top-flight for the first time since 1990, though his Premier League debut against Newcastle United in August 1998 only lasted 25 minutes before he was sent off, and Charlton were relegated back to what was then still known as the First Division again.

On this occasion, though, Charlton bounced straight back up at the end of the 1999-2000 season, winning the First Division title, with Rufus scoring six goals in 44 appearances for them. His team-mate at the centre of the Charlton defence for a while in the early 2000s, Gary Rowett, later stated that Rufus was "on the radar" for a full England call-up by this time.

Among the other players in the Charlton team of that time was Scott Parker, who would go on to hit considerable heights with West Ham United, Spurs and Fulham, and who's since made a successful career for himself as a manager.

Charlton Athletic made significant profit on Darren Bent and Scott Parker.

But while the new century started explosively well for Rufus and his club, it didn't take long for injury problems to start mounting up. Rufus made 32 appearances for Charlton in the 2001-02 season, but the following season he was limited to just ten as the result of a knee injury.

The start of the 2003-04 season suggested that he had recovered from his knee problems, but behind the scenes the injury returned, and throughout the year he underwent a series of operations. It looked briefly as though he was back when he appeared on their bench for a League Cup match against Luton Town in September 2003 which Charlton won on penalty kicks.

But in truth, the injury hadn't fully cleared, and after one final operation carried out by the renowned orthopedic surgeon Richard Steadman in the USA failed, he was forced to retire from the professional game at the age of just 29 in the summer of 2004. In total, he made 254 appearances for Charlton over 11 years, scoring twelve goals. In 2013, he was inducted into the club's hall of fame.

Rufus's post-playing life has been blighted by a succession of run-ins with the law. The problems started in October 2013 when, having set himself up as a financial advisor, he was declared bankrupt with debts totalling £6 million. Among his creditors were the Pentecostal church of which he was a member, which lost £5m, the equivalent to half of a £10.1 million windfall they'd received from the sale of land in Hackney, as well as family members. Rufus had promised his church a 72% return on their investment, but they only managed to claw back £1.4 million from it.

From here on, things only seemed to spiral. By May 2015, his bankruptcy had been discharged, but he was in court again following an investigation into misappropriation of funds and the unregulated taking of deposits. It was established that Rufus had operated a £16m Ponzi scheme involving more than 100 investors, including members of his family and congregation members of churches he had attended, pocketing more than £3 million. He received a 15-year bankruptcy restriction order for his troubles.

The matter ended up being treated as a criminal matter, and in 2019 he was charged with fraud, money laundering and carrying out a regulated activity without authorisation. It took a long time for the case to come to trial - it wasn't heard until November 2022 - but in January 2023 the former player was convicted and sentenced to seven-and-a-half years in prison. It was heard that Rufus had used his former career as a professional footballer to project a status of wealth while living off the money that he had promised to invest. In total, he paid back £7.4 million of investors’ money and lost them £8.7 million.

Both Gary Rowett and Scott Parker have gone on to have successful careers in management. Rowett has managed at eight different EFL clubs, while Parker has won promotion from the Championship three times and has recently landed a plum job at West Ham United. In stark contrast to that, though, the sort of management that Richard Rufus went into once his playing career ended up being of a very different sort, and he's lost pretty much everything as a result.

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 18:07, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:29

Looking to halt a three-game losing streak, ADO Den Haag host Sparta Rotterdam on Saturday in round 12 of the 2026-27 Eredivisie campaign.

This weekend’s fixture marks the first league meeting between the sides in five years, with their five encounters since 2021 coming in cup and friendly action.

Back in the top flight as Eerste Divisie champions, ADO Den Haag appeared to have shaken off a dismal start - six defeats from their opening seven matches - by going unbeaten in the following three matches either side of the September/October international break.

However, Robin Peter’s side have since suffered three consecutive defeats, most recently falling 1-0 to PEC Zwolle - a match in which they failed to score for the first time in six outings, with Dylan Mbayo’s goal on the stroke of half time proving decisive.

With only Fortuna Sittard creating fewer big chances than De Ooievaars’ 13, it is no surprise they have scored the third-lowest number of goals in the division. An increase in attacking productivity is essential if they are to move out of the bottom three.

ADO are one of six Eredivisie teams still without a clean sheet, and the added burden of conceding first - something they have done in each of their last eight matches - often leaves them chasing games from behind.

Having already beaten PSV Eindhoven at home this season, Saturday’s hosts have shown they are capable of competing with anyone when they hit their stride. This weekend’s assignment will require total focus and motivation from the first whistle.

Just four days after conceding a late equaliser and exiting the KNVB Beker at the hands of Fortuna Sittard, Sparta found themselves on the right side of late drama in Sunday’s five-goal thriller against Groningen at the Euroborg Stadium, as Andrej Kostic netted an 85th-minute winner.

A defeat would have left Rogier Meijer’s men outside the drop zone on goal difference alone, but they now travel to Den Haag this weekend with a chance to move six points clear of relegation danger - a welcome development for a side that started the season with just six points from the first 24 available.

While a win this weekend could also lift them into the top half, whether De Kasteelheren will still harbour European ambitions remains to be seen after a torrid end to last season with no wins from the final six matches saw them finish five points adrift of the last continental spot.

Sparta have conceded 2.4 goals per away league match so far, with 1.6 of those coming in the opening 45 minutes, highlighting their vulnerability before half-time on the road. However, Saturday’s clash pits them against a side yet to score a first-half goal at home this season.

Alejandro Pozo is also a doubt for the hosts, but Donat Barany returns to contention after making a 12-minute cameo off the bench against Zwolle.

Jens Toornstra was withdrawn 10 minutes into the second half of Sparta’s last outing against Groningen, making him a doubt for this fixture.

If Toornstra is unavailable, Robin van Cruijsen is the leading candidate to come into the visitors’ XI, although Shunsuke Mito could also be considered.

With nine goals and one assist in his last 10 matches, Milan Zonneveld is unquestionably his side’s biggest attacking threat and will be aiming to deliver once again.

#2221. Chelsea news: Liam Rosenior claims short-lived Blues stint "nothing to do with football"

By Darren Plant | 05 Nov 2026 18:11

Liam Rosenior has claimed that his exit from Chelsea in April had "nothing to do with football".

Despite being drafted into the managerial hotseat from fellow BlueCo-owned club Strasbourg, the Englishman lasted just 23 matches and 107 days at Stamford Bridge.

The 42-year-old is currently successfully rebuilding his reputation with Paris FC, winning five and losing just two of his opening nine matches in charge.

Nevertheless, it remains to be seen whether a scenario will arise that provides Rosenior with another Premier League job.

Speaking in an interview with So Foot, Rosenior suggested that matters behind the scene heavily contributed to him losing his role with the West Londoners.

However, Rosenior stopped short of revealing further details, presumably to remain focused on his current position in the French capital.

He said: "It was the best experience of my life, and the hardest too. It was fantastic, even though it only lasted four months.

"I don’t want to go into details now; I’ll talk about it one day, when the time is right. But in a country where tolerance is an important value, what happened there has nothing to do with football.

"The moment that worried me the most was at the beginning, when we went through that five-game winning streak. Any club would have been happy with such a run, but not Chelsea.

"You get good results and you’re in the spotlight. Then, there was a kind of snowball effect in the media.

"They made fun of those instructions I gave [Alejandro] Garnacho on a scrap of paper when we were down to 10 men against PSG. The clothes I wore became a joke, the way I spoke too. I’m not a joke.

"You’re the first people I’m talking to about this, and probably the last for a long time, but one day, I’ll tell you what really happened."

Despite what happened at Chelsea, Rosenior was quickly snapped up by Paris FC, who currently sit in fourth place in the Ligue 1 table.

There is an argument that every manager appointed by the Blues' owners have not suffered from their time at Stamford Bridge.

Enzo Maresca is at Manchester City, while Thomas Tuchel, Graham Potter and Mauricio Pochettino all managed nations at the 2026 World Cup.

Even Frank Lampard, who won just one game and lost eight in an 11-match interim spell, went to Coventry City and won a Championship title.

Although it remains to be seen whether Rosenior can build on his start at Paris FC, he has learned that his time at Chelsea will not define his career.

#2220. Manchester United confirmed lineup: Amad & Yoro return to starting lineup

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur return from the international break with plenty to prove when they meet at Old Trafford on Saturday.

Michael Carrick’s side have won just once in their last four domestic matches since beating Ipswich 5-2. A Carabao Cup defeat to Brighton was followed by a loss to 10-man Manchester City, before Manchester United drew with Everton and Fulham. Matheus Cunha’s late equaliser at Fulham rescued a point, but Manchester United still sit 12th.

Roberto De Zerbi’s side are bottom of the Premier League with only two points from their opening five games. Spurs have failed to score in four of those matches, while defeats against Aston Villa and Newcastle have added to the pressure.

Michael Carrick has named his starting eleven for the clash at Old Trafford, with Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans partnering in midfield as United look to get their season back on track.

Senne Lammens starts in goal, with Leny Yoro, Lisandro Martinez, Harry Maguire and Luke Shaw forming the back four. Carrick has opted for Mainoo and Tielemans in the middle, with Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes and Matheus Cunha tasked with supporting Bryan Mbeumo up front.

Leny Yoro had a brilliant international break where he made his debut for France. His performance on debut was enough for Zidane to praise the debutant.

Amad, after missing the start of the season with an injury, returns straightaway to the team.

Carlos Baleba starts on the bench and is expected to make his debut today. Marcus Rashford is also on the bench alongside Shea Lacey.

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Shaw; Mainoo, Tielemans; Amad, Bruno Fernandes, Mbeumo; Cunha.

Mbeumo gets his place through the middle, while Amad and Cunha start on the wing after scoring the late equaliser against Fulham. Fernandes will be expected to start producing the numbers from last season after a lackluster start to the season, except against Ipswich, before the international break.

United need a response after a difficult run, but Tottenham arrive under pressure of their own. With both sides desperate for points, the starting lineups set the stage for an important afternoon at Old Trafford.

#2219. We were superior to Celtic but ‘a bit of chaos’ cost us the result we deserved

Leandro Trossard believes Besiktas deserved at least a point from their 3-2 Europa League defeat to Celtic.

The former Arsenal winger felt the Turkish side were the better team for much of the contest but admitted “a bit of chaos” proved costly as Celtic fought back to claim their first win of the league phase.

#2218. Sheffield Wednesday match v Plymouth Argyle hinges on a little-known EFL rule

A new era is underway at Sheffield Wednesday following the appointment of Dave Challinor as the club's new manager.

After parting company with Henrik Pedersen last month, Wednesday named Challinor as his replacement this week, but it remains unclear what the 51-year-old's first few weeks in charge at Hillsborough will look like.

Challinor had been out of work since leaving Stockport County in June following a near five-year spell at Edgeley Park, during which he led the Hatters from the National League to League One, and he missed out on promotion to the Championship in what proved to be his final game as his side were beaten 4-1 by Bolton Wanderers in last season's play-off final.

As he prepares to return to the touchline for the first time since Stockport's Wembley heartbreak, Challinor will take charge of Wednesday for the first time when they face League Two side Oldham Athletic at Boundary Park in the FA Cup first round on Sunday.

Challinor's second game at the helm will also be a cup fixture as the Owls welcome another League Two outfit, Accrington Stanley, to Hillsborough in the EFL Trophy, but he is yet to discover when his first league game will be taking place.

Wednesday, who currently sit ninth in the League One table, are due to host fifth-placed Plymouth Argyle on Saturday 14th November, but after the South Yorkshire outfit had a number of players called up for international duty, there are doubts over whether the match will go ahead as planned.

With the Premier League and Championship out of action, international breaks used to be an opportunity for League One to take centre stage, but postponements have become more common in recent times.

During the extended three-week international break earlier in the season, just four League One fixtures took place on each weekend, but Plymouth were one of the few teams to play twice, losing 1-0 at home to Burton Albion before winning 2-0 at Leyton Orient.

In contrast, Wednesday had both of their games against Stevenage and Cambridge United called off, which led many to assume that it was a mere formality that their clash with Argyle next Saturday would also fall victim to the international window.

However, speaking in his pre-match press conference ahead of Sunday's FA Cup trip to Oldham, Challinor claimed that he expects the game against Plymouth to go ahead as things stand, although he admitted that the situation could change over the coming days.

"There's still, I suppose, a little bit of uncertainty around the Plymouth fixture, but at the minute that looks like going ahead," Challinor told The Star.

"At the moment, that's where we're at. We'll learn more today, and we'll learn more over the weekend. Because even within the squads that have been picked, if there are players that are on standby lists, if someone picks up an injury and drops out, then they might well be called up. So, at the minute, we're preparing and we're prepared in terms of our schedule for next week, that the game will go ahead on Saturday."

League One clubs can usually apply for a postponement of a fixture if three of their players are selected for international duty, and Wednesday have already surpassed that number, with Jordi Liongola, Sean Fusire and Kieran Morrison being included in the Burundi, Zimbabwe and Northern Ireland squads respectively.

However, as per EFL rules, in order for a postponement request to be made, the players who have been called up must meet certain criteria, including starting or making a substitute appearance before the 75th minute in at least half of the club's matches or four of its last five.

As Liongola made just two appearances before sustaining a hamstring injury that has kept him out since mid-August and Fusire has featured three times in all competitions this season, the duo do not satisfy the requirements, meaning that Morrison's call-up is the only one that counts towards the eligible total so far.

After suffering another injury setback, Ali Al-Hamadi has been left out of the Iraq squad for the upcoming international break, but the Owls could yet have more players summoned by their national team, including Yan Valery, who is waiting to discover whether he will be included in the Tunisia squad, which is expected to be named on Saturday.

Will Sheffield Wednesday finish higher than Plymouth Argyle in League One this season? Tell us in the comments below!

Valery has made 13 appearances for Wednesday this season, so it would certainly be a blow for Challinor to lose him for such a crucial game, and any further call-ups could convince him to ask the EFL to reschedule the Plymouth fixture.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2217. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2216. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2215. Birmingham City vs Swansea City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 18:28, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:24

Fighting to move into the top two before the November international break, in-form Birmingham City host fellow promotion hopefuls Swansea City at St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park on Saturday afternoon.

Blues blitzed past Millwall in the Second City on Tuesday night, whilst the Swans' away-day issues continued, losing late on against a Portsmouth side who are pushing for a playoff position.

For the first time since earning promotion to the Premier League in 2008-09, Birmingham City have recorded three consecutive Championship home victories without conceding following Tuesday's battering of Millwall.

Seemingly replacing Tomoki Iwata as Blues' long-range specialist, Alex Cochrane fired in a second thunderbolt in the space of four days to open the scoring against the Lions, with Max Bird and Jay Stansfield also getting on the scoresheet in an impressive first period.

Winning five of their past six second-tier matches, Chris Davies's side have flown up to third spot in the Championship standings, just a single point behind West Midlands rivals Wolverhampton Wanderers in second.

Earning his maiden start for Birmingham, summer signing Bird made up for an injury-interrupted opening to his Second City career, starring with a goal and an assist in Tuesday's Millwall demolition.

Blues extended their unbeaten league run at St Andrew's this season to seven matches (W3 D4), with Saturday's hosts winning each of their past three home games by an aggregate scoreline of 10-0.

The surprise leaders at the beginning of the season, Swansea City are enduring their first major dip of a promising promotion charge, with the Welsh outfit losing three of their past four Championship contests.

There was more agony on the road for Vitor Matos's troops on Tuesday night, when added-time goals from Terry Devlin and Thomas Waddingham secured a 3-1 success for Portsmouth at Fratton Park.

Collecting just three points from their most recent quartet of fixtures, Swansea have dropped down to fifth place in the Championship table ahead of this weekend's clash in Birmingham, level on points with Blues.

Scoring the Swans' momentary leveller at Pompey earlier in the week, Zan Vipotnik has experienced a strange start to the season, with last term's Championship Golden Boot winner playing second fiddle to Adam Idah up front.

The Slovenian was a second-half substitute when Swansea and Birmingham clashed at the start of August in South Wales, where a solitary August Priske strike secured first-round EFL Cup progression for Davies's men.

Missing Birmingham's win over Millwall due to a knock, leading goalscorer Priske is a doubt to feature on Saturday afternoon.

Should the Danish marksman be unavailable, Blues should stick with Bird at the 10 position and Stansfield leading the line.

Dael Fry and Paik Seung-ho are nearing a return to action, although first-choice goalkeeper James Beadle is expected to miss out this weekend.

Swansea defender Stephen Welsh was forced off following a nasty collision at Fratton Park; however, the Scot's issue is not as bad as initially feared.

The 26-year-old is still a major doubt to play at Birmingham, though, with Ben Cabango likely to join Jenson Seelt at centre-back.

The Swans will be boosted by the return of attackers Eom Ji-Sung and Moussa Yeo for the Second City trip.

Birmingham City possible starting lineup:

Allsop; Laird, Neumann, Klarer, Cochrane; Solis, Iwata; Roberts, Bird, Millar; Stansfield

Vigouroux; Lissah, Seelt, Cabango, Tymon; Franco, Fulton, Stamenic; Opoku, Just, Vipotnik

Unbeaten on home soil in the Championship this season, Birmingham are a real force in front of their St Andrew's faithful.

Swansea have struggled massively in recent away matches but should bounce back to claim a point on Saturday.

#2214. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2213. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”
